2026-08-09 ACTIVE LOCAL CLEANUP AND SC STRATUM COMPLETION

The loop-topology checkpoint is committed locally as 6e2c6f4ad6, "Make BREP
trim classification topology aware".  It was not pushed.  The final selected
object in the initial 14-database ray stratum, the 848-face SC solid, exposed
a direction-dependent legacy hit-cleanup failure even though adaptive depths
three and eight found the same nine surface roots.

SC deterministic pair 22 mixed one genuinely coincident near-hit/near-miss
observation with a later near-miss/clean pair several model units apart.  The
historical code let sorted adjacency imply event identity and erased or
reinterpreted the distant pair.  It then deleted a complete trailing suffix
after an unmatched entry, whereas the reversed stream retained the analogous
balanced interval.  The fixed and allocating cleanup implementations now
require the existing model-derived ray-parameter coincidence envelope for
every near-miss interaction.  They remove unmatched entries in a reverse scan
and unmatched exits in a forward scan before nonzero-winding compaction.  The
two scans exchange under ray reversal and retain the maximal balanced stream.

The C++ cleanup corpus contains reduced exact streams for both defects.  Exact
SC pair 22 and the complete 70-ray legacy-only comparison now pass in both
directions at depths three and eight.  The full prepared trace also passes,
but selects none of its 35 eligible rays: 31 are uncertified, two exhaust the
surface-box workspace, and two fail event classification.  Its 2,498
candidate spans, 18,336 subdivision boxes, 1,235 isolated boxes, and 225
unresolved physical events reinforce the planned performance priority:
profile normal production and reduce face/span candidate and proof work before
tuning Newton evaluation.  Correctness gates remain authoritative, production
depth remains eight, and the Abert-informed optimization phase follows only
after broader analytic and imported qualification.

2026-08-09 ACTIVE IMPORTED-RAY DEPTH COMPARISON AND STATUS-2 POLES

The preceding database profiling checkpoint is committed locally as
56363b08b6, "Profile BREP SurfaceTrees across databases".  It was not pushed.
The current checkpoint adds the missing ray-partition gate between the
experimental adaptive depth three and the production default eight, then uses
the first genuine imported failure it finds to extend prepared authority.

`brep-audit` now accepts

  --ray-depth-compare --candidate-depth=3 --reference-depth=8 \
    --ray-count=N file.g brep

and emits one `brlcad-brep-ray-depth-comparison-v1` JSON record.  Three
centered axis chords and N fixed-seed random sphere chords are shot in both
directions.  Each depth is prepared independently with direct primitive prep,
the complete returned segment list is sorted and validated, and the deeper
tree is destroyed before the shallower tree is built.  The core gate requires
finite ordered solid intervals, equality between depths within the active
model tolerance plus a coordinate-roundoff floor, and exact forward/reverse
line invariance under `t_reverse = chord_length - t_forward`.  Failure records
include the line and both depth/direction interval streams.

Direct prep is a correctness requirement, not merely an audit convenience.
The persistent librt prep-cache key currently hashes the object and transform
but not an explicitly requested adaptive SurfaceTree depth, and serialized
version-one BREP prep data does not record that request.  An `rt_gettree`
comparison could therefore load the same cached tree twice.  This audit calls
`ft_prep` on a freshly loaded BREP for each depth, verifies
`_rt_brep_prep_stats.surface_tree_depth_limit`, and never opens the prep cache.
Any future production-default change must revisit cache identity/versioning.

The JSON separately reports nodes, leaves, physical depth, CurveTree size,
prep time, hit/miss/segment counts, normal-orientation anomalies, prepared
attempt/selection and every fallback reason, surface boxes and proof work,
fixed-workspace exhaustion, physical-event state, seam/singular/regular/edge/
vertex certificates, and fixed-versus-allocating and local-versus-legacy
diagnostics.  In trace mode a selected prepared result is still followed by
the legacy solve for equivalence telemetry; `solver_calls` is therefore audit
work and must not be reported as normal production cost.  Normal production
returns immediately after a prepared selection.

The first three 70-ray imported runs establish useful but still small
continuity evidence:

  * `non_grabcad/0353/0353-02-57.g`, `Product_step18_item.s`: 186 faces,
    20 segments on 16 hit rays, ten prepared selections, 19 surface-box and
    41 uncertified fallbacks, zero depth or reversal differences.  Depth
    three/eight uses 2,633/77,858 nodes and 1,749/39,378 leaves.
  * `nist/NIST_MBE_PMI_4.g`, `Document_item.s`: 495 faces, 20 segments on
    14 hit rays, 14 selections, eight surface-box and 48 uncertified
    fallbacks, zero depth or reversal differences, and maximum depth-to-depth
    endpoint shift `9.17e-9`.  Depth three/eight uses 32,460/659,363 nodes and
    18,918/333,584 leaves.
  * `non_grabcad/recurta/step_ap203/10145.g`, `id2_item.s`: depth three and
    eight returned identical results, but the first run exposed one shared
    reversal failure.  The +Y line from `(0,-2.3815698604072066,0)` returned
    `[1.1315698604072066,3.6315698604072066]`; the same line from the opposite
    endpoint in the -Y direction missed.  Both depths overflowed the prepared
    32,768-box surface workspace and fell back to the legacy Utah path.  This
    is the concrete imported version of the remembered sphere-pole failure.

`10145.g` is a one-face solid radius-1.25 `ON_RevSurface` sphere.  Its rational
order-3-by-3, 9-by-5 NURBS form returns status 2, with four-by-two spans and
domains `[0,2.5*pi]` and `[-1.25*pi,1.25*pi]`.  The earlier pole theorem only
marked status-1 NURBS singular sides; the converted-ELL C/C++ fixtures could
not exercise this representation.  The fallback result was direction
dependent even though SurfaceTree density was not.

Prepared pole authority is now extended narrowly to valid `ON_RevSurface`
status-2 forms.  OpenNURBS constructs that form as the tensor product of an
angular arc and the profile curve, optionally transposed, so a singular
profile endpoint remains the corresponding collapsed NURBS boundary.  Prep
records this class-specific map only when both the original surface and the
converted NURBS declare the same singular side.  Ray time still validates
collapsed homogeneous controls, the scale-aware line witness, exact outward
deflation/exclusion and determinant sign, a pointwise parameter round trip,
3-D locus agreement for both the mapped pole and an interior one-sided normal
sample, and face CurveTree inclusion.  A classifier result marked trimmed is
accepted only within the existing edge-miss tolerance; a genuinely
trimmed-away pole still fails closed.  The complete fiber and
alternating/minimum-segment theorem remains unchanged.

A singular deflation proof now gets first refusal before a coefficient-hull
exclusion.  This is needed after small or distant rotated similarities, where
construction roundoff can place one ray-plane hull strictly on one side even
though its collapsed pole is within the separately proved line bound.  A
failed singular proof leaves the original hull exclusion intact; it does not
turn an ordinary miss into a candidate.  General status-2 seams, trim edges,
vertices, folds outside the existing clean policies, and arbitrary surface
classes remain unsupported without their own interval or class-specific map
proof.

The permanent in-memory pole corpus now builds analytic `ON_RevSurface`
spheres with the STEP-style arc-length domains and transforms the completed
BREP as a whole.  It shoots both revolution-axis directions at scale 1.25,
scale 0.01 after translation and arbitrary rotation, and scale 10,000 after
million-unit translation and another rotation.  All six rays must observe a
status-2 face and select the exact prepared singular theorem at adaptive depth
two.  They pass.  The repaired imported object now passes all 70 rays: 22 hit
rays, 66 prepared selections, four uncertified clean-miss fallbacks, zero
depth/reversal differences, zero maximum endpoint difference, no box
overflow, and 343/16,215 depth-three/eight nodes.  Its 32-random-chord audit
falls from the earlier pathological tens of seconds to about 0.66 seconds on
this host, primarily because the certified pole pair no longer drives the
trace into exhaustive local boxing and legacy fallback.

Focused optimized gates pass 8/8: directed analytic rays, status-1/status-2
poles, grazing trend, hard Crofton replay, regular stream, complete seam/Cobb
grid, and both audit modes.  The final-source seam grid took 280.46 seconds
(280.50 seconds for the parallel group).  ASAN/UBSAN
with leak scanning disabled passes the transformed pole fixture, the 14-ray
converted sphere audit, and the six-ray imported failure replay with no
diagnostic.  The production adaptive default remains eight.  Depth eight is
a continuity reference here, not a truth oracle; analytic implicit/BREP
Cauchy-Crofton tests remain the ground truth for shapes with exact
conversions.

Next extend this direct comparison across the recorded STEP strata, retaining
identical deterministic rays and fallback distributions.  Add face- and
feature-targeted lines because random chords undersample sparse parts and
almost never hit exact seams, poles, or grazes.  Promote each new discrepancy
into the C/C++ hard corpus with its line and event trace.  Only after the
partition, cache, sanitizer, and analytic-oracle gates are broad enough should
adaptive depth three replace eight.  Then profile normal (non-trace) prepared
production to prioritize face/span BVHs, compact trim data, vectorized
coefficient rejection, batched `vshot`, and only then evaluator/Newton tuning.

2026-08-09 ACTIVE STRATIFIED SURFACETREE CORPUS PROFILING

The structural-knot/regular-stream checkpoint is committed locally as
49c518ad50, "Certify BREP streams after structural splits".  It was not
pushed.  This increment adds reproducible database-wide preparation
profiling rather than choosing a production depth from two hand-selected
objects.

brep-audit now accepts

  --surface-trees-all --surface-tree-depth=N file.g

and emits one `brlcad-brep-surface-tree-database-summary-v1` JSON document.
It profiles every BREP object serially, avoiding simultaneous retention of
all trees, while reporting per-object scale, object status and reason,
failure faces/domains, loops, trims, native/rational NURBS makeup, exact and
status-2 form counts, maximum orders/CVs/spans, adaptive and physical depth,
nodes, leaves, CurveTree leaves, build time, and process peak RSS.  The final
record also contains database totals.  Empty or unloadable BREP objects are
distinguished from actual SurfaceTree face failures.  The original detailed
single-object mode remains available and now reports scale and object failure
reason as well.  A repository CTest runs the all-object path at adaptive depth
zero on regress/nurbs/sph.g.

The first paired stratum contains 14 databases, 75 BREP objects, 5,778 faces,
6,454 loops, and 28,798 trims.  It includes one-face and planar fixtures,
mixed analytic/status-2 STEP conversions, three NIST models, trim-heavy
bearings, a 21-object database, native rational NURBS, order-six/high-span
surfaces, SC_solid_stp_20260429.g, and all 40 BREP objects in FRAME.g.  Object
diagonals range from 4.33 to 1,197 model units.  The faces comprise 1,910
native NURBS, 3,650 rational NURBS forms, 3,473 exact forms, and 2,305
reparameterized forms.  Every face builds successfully at both adaptive
depths three and eight.

Across this paired stratum, the exact structural counts are:

                         adaptive 3       adaptive 8       depth-8/depth-3
  SurfaceTree nodes          664,796        15,165,658            22.81
  SurfaceTree leaves         419,208         7,637,698            18.22
  CurveTree leaves           884,649           884,649             1.00
  summed build seconds        18.630           339.180            18.21

The node ratio ranges from 4.79 to 47.27 per database, with median 23.32;
structural complexity therefore changes the benefit but does not erase it.
Build times are useful directional measurements, not a controlled benchmark:
moderate pairs ran in bounded parallel process groups, whereas the two
largest depth-eight cases ran alone.  Counts, failure results, and peak RSS
remain process-local and exact.

The high-span examples quantify the resource stakes.  The 848-face order-six
SC solid changes from 264,544 nodes, 167,741 leaves, physical depth 10,
4.064 seconds, and 156 MB RSS at adaptive depth three to 3,274,514 nodes,
1,669,855 leaves, physical depth 14, 52.420 seconds, and 942 MB at depth
eight.  FRAME changes from 281,379 nodes, 179,608 leaves, physical depth 11,
6.298 seconds, and 260 MB to 9,420,969 nodes, 4,727,854 leaves, physical depth
16, 153.138 seconds, and 2.45 GB.  CurveTree leaves remain 60,527 and 327,803
respectively; trim acceleration is consequently a first-class residual
performance target after surface density falls.

Two additional depth-three stress strata also build without a SurfaceTree
face failure.  Antikythera Mechanism 3dprint.g covers 75 BREP objects, 11,575
faces, order-six native surfaces, and up to 240 spans in one direction.
tork.g builds all 529 faces in seven nonempty BREP objects; its eighth BREP
directory entry, None_item_step38603.s, is empty and is now reported as such
rather than misdiagnosed as a tree failure.

The new optimized CTest passes.  ASAN/UBSAN runs of both the one-object
depth-zero and 21-object depth-three database modes pass with leak scanning
disabled and no diagnostic.  The production adaptive default remains eight.
This profiling proves a large prep opportunity and a plausible experimental
plateau; it does not prove arbitrary STEP rays correct at depth three.  Next
expand the reproducible manifest across corpus quantiles and couple it to
prepared-ray attempt/selection/fallback and exact-partition evidence before
changing the default.

2026-08-09 ACTIVE STRUCTURAL-KNOT CONTRACT AND DEPTH PLATEAU

SurfaceTree construction has two distinct kinds of recursion, and the
configured limit must govern only one of them.  Closed-surface seams and every
interior NURBS span knot define structural subdomains on which flatness,
bounding, and ray-root reasoning are meaningful.  Those splits are now
exhausted unconditionally.  Only after a leaf no longer crosses a structural
boundary does its recursion restart at zero and apply the requested adaptive
flatness/straightness depth budget.  The old ordering checked the shared
depth counter before the structural phase and could therefore stop with a
leaf crossing interior knots at a sufficiently small requested limit.  That
made reduced-depth behavior accidental and invalidated the intended meaning
of the experimental control.

The current libbrep change moves the depth termination behind the
structural-phase predicate.  A new brep_surfacetree_knots regression builds a
smooth 6-by-5-span NURBS surface with adaptive depth zero, requires exactly 30
leaves, and verifies that no leaf interval strictly contains any interior
knot.  It passes.  Requested depth is consequently an adaptive budget;
observed BBNode depth is the physical structural-plus-adaptive depth and can
legitimately be larger.  Test and audit reporting now say this explicitly.
Do not reintroduce a common cap unless a separate proof establishes a safe
structural fallback; arbitrary BREPs cannot assume knot-crossing boxes have
well-behaved parameter or derivative bounds.

This semantic change invalidates serialized SurfaceTrees even though their
byte layout is unchanged.  rt_brep_prep_serialize now emits cache version one
and rejects version-zero entries, which may contain leaves crossing internal
knots.  Such entries are rebuilt rather than trusted.  The cache still does
not encode the requested adaptive depth, so deserialized statistics report
that request as unknown while measuring the physical hierarchy actually
loaded.  The complete optimized serial/parallel BREP cache matrix now passes
all seven cases, including parallel single-object, identical-object,
different-object, and hierarchy preparation.

With the corrected contract, the focused clean regular-stream ratchet passes
at adaptive depth zero with 25 nodes and 16 leaves across the RCC, cone, and
torus fixtures; the physical maximum depth is two.  The 24-case hard replay
passes every torus case, including exact seam-aligned directions, at adaptive
depth one.  The deliberately near-trim disjoint-sphere-union line remains the
only failure at adaptive depths one and two.  Adaptive depth three passes the
complete hard replay with 2,513 nodes, 1,844 leaves, and physical maximum
depth five.  This is a useful conservative floor, not a claim that depth
three is globally minimal.

The broad evidence at adaptive depth three is now green.  The identical-line
Cauchy-Crofton gate passes all 21 BREP preparations with zero implicit/BREP
partition differences, including sphere, ellipsoid, RCC, cone, torus,
nontrivial CSG, nested transformed BREP leaves, and sewn Cobb cases.  It uses
10,410 nodes, 7,682 leaves, and physical maximum depth five.  The complete
seam/crack/classifier shard also passes: 78 solids, 26,637 nodes, 17,676
leaves, physical maximum depth five, the same two intended prepared
promotions, and no partition regression or ambiguity.  The grazing gate
passes as well.  These results establish a plausible common correctness
plateau for the current corpus while preserving mandatory structural
decomposition.  The production default remains eight until the remaining
stratified STEP-corpus distribution is checked.  Focused sanitizer,
rebuilt-cache, and broad optimized correctness gates are already green as
recorded below.

The full optimized CTest gate now passes 18/18 shards after updating one stale
coverage ratchet.  The regular-stream theorem increases prepared production
selection in the 416-ray Cobb seam matrix from 124 to exactly 322 at both
adaptive depth three and the production default.  All 22 seam certificates,
44 seam events, two intended partition promotions, oracle intervals, and
ambiguity/regression counts are unchanged.  The assertion now requires the
new exact 322 count; this was theorem coverage growth, not a geometry or
tolerance change.

2026-08-09 LATER PERFORMANCE WORKSTREAM RECORDED

Correctness remains first, but the plan now contains a dedicated performance
phase after density reduction and cache qualification.  The legacy solver is
recognizably descended from Abert, Geimer, and Muller (2006): two orthogonal
ray planes, a UV Newton solve, and surface-box UV seeds.  BRL-CAD did not
adopt that work's principal performance machinery, notably precomputed SIMD
Cox-de Boor evaluation and four-ray packets.  The new phase does not propose
recreating it verbatim.  It first profiles the certified pipeline, removes
global traversal work, builds a real object-level face/span BVH, compacts
immutable rational-Bezier and trim data, vectorizes coefficient projection
and conservative rejection, and implements a true batched rt_brep_vshot.
Newton/evaluator optimization follows only if profiles justify it.

Sloup and Havran (2021) is added as a modern performance reference for compact
two-dimensional trim search and GPU feasibility.  GPU work remains optional
and later; double precision, divergence, transfer cost, and CPU certification
must be measured.  Every optimization is promoted independently under the
same analytic, grazing, seam, nested-CSG, and exact-line Crofton partition
gates.  Do not trade the solid-event or crack-tolerance contract for visual
rendering throughput.

Focused ASAN/UBSAN checks pass with leak scanning disabled for the new
multi-span structural-knot test, the depth-zero regular-stream production
ratchet, and the complete 24-case hard replay at adaptive depth three.  There
are no sanitizer diagnostics.  The hard run retains 2,513 nodes and 1,844
leaves with physical maximum depth five under instrumentation.

Two imported STEP samples show that the depth-three plateau has material prep
benefit even after mandatory structural splitting.  On the 462-face
Product_step18_item.s, adaptive depth three versus eight changes serial
SurfaceTree nodes from 123,919 to 5,392, leaves from 62,451 to 3,263, build
time from 2.577 to 0.154 seconds, and peak RSS from 59.8 to 46.9 MB; physical
maximum depth is five versus ten.  CurveTree leaves remain 13,060.  On the
status-2-heavy 175-face Product_step46_item.s (33 exact and 142
reparameterized faces), nodes change from 199,958 to 7,755, leaves from
98,758 to 4,888, time from 5.930 to 0.364 seconds, and peak RSS from 87.2 to
41.9 MB.  CurveTree leaves remain 36,385.  These are strong samples but not
yet the required stratified corpus distribution; do not change the production
default from them alone.

2026-08-09 ACTIVE COMPLETE REGULAR EVENT STREAM

This checkpoint adds a deterministic --crofton-hard-only replay
corpus and a clean-interior regular-stream theorem.  The replay regenerates
the fixed-seed 8000-line Crofton sequence, shoots only named indices, compares
complete implicit/BREP partitions, and prints the exact line plus production
trace on failure.  A --regular-stream-only subset requires the new prepared
path and is registered in CTest at SurfaceTree depth zero.  The full hard mode
runs at the production default and retains the exact torus seam-multiplicity
and disjoint-sphere near-boundary cases for later increments.

The first traces disproved the narrower periodic-lateral hypothesis.  RCC and
cone failures usually had one cap root and one lateral root; both were already
Krawczyk-certified, but their coarse t enclosures overlapped and the otherwise
correct ENTER/LEAVE events failed the complete-line state check.  Random torus
failures had two correct roots on different spans, often with one additional
unresolved rootless box around an already observed endpoint.  The missing
abstraction was a complete stream of independently certified physical root
components, not another nonlinear root finder.

The new theorem is screened to clean, non-grazing, trim-inside roots forming
an alternating stream with every material segment larger than the BRL-CAD
minimum.  It runs only when an unresolved terminal box remains or ordinary
coarse event t intervals overlap.  For each same-face/span physical root it
uses the existing fixed-workspace component proof to own all connected boxes
and exclude any rootless remainder inside that component.  It then constructs
a separate strict local Krawczyk/determinant certificate and tight t enclosure
around the corrected root.  It never takes a parameter-space hull across
faces or spans.  Every local root and terminal box must belong to exactly one
component; every component determinant must agree with the independently
tightened event determinant and the outward normal direction.  Only after all
components pass are boxes marked, events published, and the complete
alternating/minimum-segment state machine run.  Status-2 faces are allowed
only for regular interior events because all authority remains in their NURBS
parameter space and only the final point is mapped for publication.  The path
adds no ray-time allocation, random perturbation, mutable global, or
SurfaceTree traversal dependency.

At depth two the theorem closes all selected random RCC, cone, and torus
failures: 5/5 for each fixture.  The focused production-path ratchet also
passes at depth zero with seven SurfaceTree nodes/leaves across the three
BREPs.  A complete depth-two Crofton rerun now gives zero differing lines for
RCC and cone (formerly 12 and 9) and reduces torus from 114 differing lines to
10.  All previously passing sphere, ellipsoid, ARB8, Cobb, and CSG fixtures
remain at zero except the already known disjoint-sphere line.  The current
full depth-two result is 16 comparison failures total: three exact torus
directed cases, ten random torus lines plus its aggregate discrepancy, and one
disjoint-sphere-union line.

The remaining traces divide cleanly again.  Torus line 737 has one fully
certified fold root and one regular root; it needs a general mixed
fold/regular stream across spans.  Lines 2477 and 3819 enter the new stream but
leave a box unowned; failure-stage telemetry is being refined to distinguish
root, box, and event ownership.  Lines 2399 and 2977, plus disjoint-sphere
1262, contain a valid trim-inside root classified NEAR_HIT at a parameter or
topological boundary and are intentionally excluded from the clean theorem.
The exact torus diameter and vertical tube lines have several UV
representations of the same physical events across status-2 spans and remain
a separate source-union problem.  Do not loosen CLEAN_HIT tests without an
interval/topological proof that preserves crack handling.

2026-08-09 POST-POLE REDUCED-DEPTH CROFTON BOUNDARY

The committed pole theorem was followed by a complete paired-Crofton run at
SurfaceTree depth two.  The sphere (32000 lines), oblate ellipsoid (8000), and
ARB8 (8000) have zero differing implicit/BREP lines.  The ellipsoid pole miss
reported before the pole theorem is gone.  The overlapping-sphere union,
sphere lens, sphere cavity, box-minus-cylinder, nested transformed sphere
shell, and sewn Cobb sphere also have zero differing lines.

The remaining primitive differences are localized.  The RCC has 12 differing
lines of 8000, the truncated cone has 9, and the torus has 114 plus three
directed partition failures.  The first RCC indices are 649, 1789, 2073, 2219,
and 2961; the first cone indices are 649, 2219, 3053, 3684, and 4103.  The two
fixtures use the same bounding sphere and therefore the same deterministic
lines.  Shared failures 649 and 2219 strongly implicate the periodic lateral
surface decomposition or its ownership rather than a primitive-specific
implicit solve.  Their missing material chords are 0.325--1.444 model units,
well above the 0.0005 modeling tolerance but short relative to the fixtures.
The first torus indices are 122, 148, 158, 189, and 198, with missing chords
2.05--5.88; its directed failures include the two-interval diameter in both
directions and the vertical tube line.  This is a broader completeness case,
not merely a close same-span pair.  The disjoint-sphere union has one differing
line, index 1262 with chord 3.12287, likely exposing the same unresolved
cross-span/leaf behavior through CSG evaluation.

The immediate implementation step is a fast deterministic C/C++ replay mode.
It must regenerate the fixed-seed 8000-line sequence, select these exact
indices, compare complete implicit and BREP partitions, and report the BREP
prepared trace.  Do not make the known-failing reduced-depth replay a default
CTest until its production certificate is implemented.  Use the trace to test
the current hypothesis that RCC/cone roots occupy adjacent Bezier spans of one
periodic face and therefore miss the existing same-face-and-same-span regular
pair theorem.  Preserve full torus cases separately: they may require a
general multi-span source-union/component proof rather than the narrow lateral
surface extension.

2026-08-09 ACTIVE DEGENERATE-POLE CORPUS AND THEOREM

The regular-root-pair increment is committed locally as 146a61a9cb,
"Certify close BREP regular root pairs".  It was not pushed.  The current
increment, "Certify collapsed BREP pole events", is also committed locally
and was not pushed.  It implements and tests a complete-line collapsed-pole
theorem.  The production SurfaceTree default remains eight and the version-zero
cache payload is unchanged.

Controlled-depth results isolate a parameter-degeneracy defect rather than a
generic nonlinear convergence failure.  The full directed sphere suite fails
at depth two, passes at depth three, produces invalid normals at depth four,
misses the large translated pole at depths five and six, and passes at depth
seven.  At the failing depths, a sphere pole appears as many roots with
different U values and one boundary V value even though all those UV pairs
map to one 3-D point.  Arbitrary representatives yield inconsistent limiting
normals and ENTER/LEAVE directions.  At depth two the prepared isolator visits
16384 terminal/fold boxes, retains only the fixed 64-box trace prefix, and
cannot Krawczyk-certify the singular Jacobian.  At depths five and six its fold
logic can find the two geometric endpoint locations, but the ordinary root
and direction ledgers still contain the collapsed fiber's multiplicity and
cannot publish a partition.

Prepared status-1 Bezier spans now record which outer sides inherit an
ON_Surface::IsSingular assertion.  Status-2 parameter remaps remain excluded
because they need an interval proof of the side mapping.  At shot time a
candidate singular span is considered only when it has exactly one singular
side.  Its homogeneous control points must collapse to one finite pole within
a 4096-ulp coordinate-scale allowance; both ray-plane boundary residuals and
the line-to-pole distance must satisfy the same bound.  These checks qualify
the binary64 realization before the OpenNURBS singular-side assertion is used
to set the residual boundary coefficients to exact zero.

If a degree-n Bernstein polynomial is zero at its minimum side, division by
the boundary parameter maps coefficient p_j to (n/j)p_j; the maximum-side
formula uses n/(n-j).  New outward interval deflation applies this identity to
both ray-plane residual surfaces.  A direct coefficient sign, a derivative-
free separating axis over the two-dimensional interval coefficient hull, or
the established rotated-hull test must then exclude zero from the deflated
pair.  This proves that the span contains no line root distinct from the
collapsed side.  The residual Jacobian determinant has the same positive
interior boundary factor; deflating it and requiring every resulting
coefficient to have one strict sign proves the crossing orientation without
using the singular UV Jacobian at the pole.

One-sided normals are sampled at a fixed 1/4096 normalized interior offset and
oriented by the face.  They are not used to establish root existence or
direction: the determinant sign is authoritative.  Before publication every
span belonging to one face/side must be present, have the same determinant
direction, have overlapping ray-parameter enclosures, and have one-sided unit
normals in a common narrow cone.  Their normals are averaged only for the hit
record.  All candidate spans on the entire line must be singular-owned, with
no ordinary terminal box, local root, or fold root left over.  The resulting
pole groups must form a complete alternating ENTER/LEAVE stream with every
gap larger than BRL-CAD's minimum-segment tolerance.  Mixed pole/regular
streams, multiple singular sides on one span, non-smooth or inconsistent
normal cones, status-2 surfaces, incomplete ownership, workspace overflow, or
any unexcluded deflated root fail closed to the established path.

The implementation adds no ray-time allocation, random perturbation, mutable
global state, or SurfaceTree dependency.  Fixed trace workspaces record each
singular span, all line/collapse/exclusion/determinant stages, ownership,
normal agreement, and final pole events.  Singular events carry their
qualified normal explicitly so hit construction never reevaluates an
arbitrary UV pole normal.  Prepared face/span metadata is rebuilt after cache
deserialization, so this nonserialized addition does not require a cache
version change.

The focused --pole-only C/C++ corpus covers base north and south pole lines,
an inside-to-pole line, a radius-0.01 translated sphere, and a radius-10000
sphere translated to million-scale coordinates.  It requires prepared-path
selection, all eight sphere spans to pass every deflation stage, exactly two
owned singular events, no ordinary subdivision boxes or local roots, correct
analytic distances, and finite outward normals.  CTest runs it at SurfaceTree
depth two, the clearest former failure.  It passes independently at every
depth zero through eight; at depth zero the three BREP fixtures have only
three SurfaceTree nodes total.  The full directed suite now passes at depth
two.  All fifteen optimized default-depth rt_brep_ray shards pass, including
the seam, defect, contact/trim, grazing, paired Crofton, and interval gates.
ASAN/UBSAN pole runs at depths zero and two pass with leak scanning disabled
and halt-on-error enabled.

Do not reduce the production SurfaceTree default yet.  The next independent
reduced-depth work remains the RCC/cone/torus paired-Crofton grazing misses and
four transformed seam cases previously observed at depth two.  General mixed
pole/regular event authority and an interval-certified limiting-normal cone
are later extensions; the current complete-line theorem intentionally does
not claim them.

2026-08-09 ACTIVE CERTIFIED REGULAR-ROOT-PAIR CHECKPOINT

The controlled SurfaceTree measurement increment is committed locally as
ad9cc18119, "Measure controlled BREP SurfaceTree depth".  It was not pushed.
This checkpoint's increment, "Certify close BREP regular root pairs", closes
the exact depth-six grazing gap identified by that experiment.  It is also a
local-only commit and was not pushed.  The production SurfaceTree default
remains eight and the cache format remains version zero.

The ordinary physical-event dispatcher previously required each terminal box
to have one Krawczyk-certified root and one determinant sign.  The newer
component theorem was reachable only as cleanup around an exact edge event.
Consequently a near-tangent sphere ray could have two correct, independently
converged ENTER/LEAVE roots in one face/span and still publish no event: all
three coarse boxes were unresolved, while the sparse legacy fallback retained
only one hit.

A new regular/regular theorem handles this case before the fold dispatcher.
Its first production scope is deliberately narrow.  It requires exactly two
finite, non-grazing CLEAN_HIT observations on one face and Bezier span, with
opposite directions and ray-parameter separation strictly greater than the
BRL-CAD minimum segment tolerance plus scale-aware roundoff.  It owns the
entire shot's terminal-box ledger; every box must belong to that face/span and
must either observe one of the roots or be an unresolved rootless box.  It is
not used for status-2 reparameterized surfaces, exact tangency, minimum-size
contacts, trim-near roots, or a ray with additional components.

The two roots receive independent local certificates with radii smaller than
0.2 times their normalized L-infinity separation.  Their boxes are therefore
disjoint.  The complete rectangle spanning all terminal boxes is then split
at both local-box boundaries, and direct/rotated Bernstein bounds exclude
every remaining part with the existing fixed 64-entry stack, depth-24 limit,
and 8192-visit cap.  Stored terminal boxes need not touch: an earlier certified
exclusion may omit the root-free gap between them, and this theorem re-proves
that gap over the complete hull rather than trusting the omission.

Each root then receives a smaller nested event box which independently passes
coefficient restriction, strict Krawczyk inclusion, constant determinant
sign, outward t enclosure, face-reversal direction agreement, and the
minimum-segment gap classification.  The whole transaction is committed only
after both roots, every box, the complement, and the ordered ENTER/LEAVE stream
pass.  Component boxes receive a distinct RESOLVED_REGULAR_PAIR disposition
with no single determinant sign, since the two roots necessarily have
opposite signs.  New telemetry records attempts, certificates, owned boxes and
roots, complement visits/high-water, and a bounded failure stage.  No ray-time
allocation, random perturbation, or mutable global state was added.

The sphere/torus grazing gate now passes at every controlled SurfaceTree depth
from six through zero.  At depth zero the sphere's six formerly missing
material rays certify six root-pair components owning 18 boxes; complement
proofs visit 254 boxes in aggregate with stack high-water nine.  All eight
resolved sphere lines are selected by the prepared production pipeline.  The
exact tangent remains a miss/contact observation, the chord exactly equal to
T retains its conservative boundary fallback, all mirrored outside rays
remain misses, and the status-2 torus similarity path is unchanged.

Broader depth-two probing now identifies the next independent work rather
than another grazing-pair defect.  Endpoint, non-isoparametric, contact/trim,
affine, isolated-defect, and the focused grazing gates pass.  The directed
gate exposes sphere-pole behavior; Crofton finds small grazing misses on RCC,
cone, and torus conversions; and four seam transform cases still depend on
denser legacy leaves.  Pole behavior is especially diagnostic and
non-monotonic: the directed suite misses poles at depth two, passes at depth
three, returns geometrically wrong pole normals at depth four, misses the
large translated pole at depths five and six, and passed at depth seven in
the preceding checkpoint.  This confirms that selecting a lucky tree depth
is not a sound solution.  The next theorem/test corpus should explicitly own
degenerate parameter-space pole events and reconstruct their geometric normal
independently of the arbitrary UV representative.

All eleven optimized default-depth shards pass in the final state.  During
validation an initially broad dispatch screen intercepted 198 two-observation
Cobb seam rays before rejecting their different-face roots; narrowing the
screen to the theorem's exact same-face/span, clean-root, compatible-box domain
restored the established edge/seam paths.  The complete seam shard then passes
and reports zero regular-pair attempts for the Cobb matrix.  ASAN/UBSAN grazing
and isolated-defect gates pass at depth zero with leak scanning disabled and
halt-on-error enabled; neither reports a sanitizer diagnostic.  Do not change
the default based on the new grazing result alone.  The pole,
primitive-Crofton, and transformed seam gaps must first move onto certified
prepared paths and the broad matrix must pass at a common reduced depth.


2026-08-09 ACTIVE CONTROLLED SURFACETREE-DENSITY CHECKPOINT

The tolerance-healed edge-transition increment is committed locally as
e67336b607, "Certify tolerance-healed BREP edge transitions".  It was not
pushed.  The current uncommitted increment adds measurement and per-rt_i
control of BREP SurfaceTree construction depth.  It deliberately leaves the
production default at BREP_MAX_FT_DEPTH, currently eight, and does not change
the version-zero cache payload.

The control is an rt_i property rather than an environment variable or
mutable library global.  _rt_brep_set_surface_tree_depth accepts depths zero
through eight before prep, and each rt_brep_prep passes that immutable choice
to brep_build_bvh.  The resulting brep_specific records the requested limit,
observed maximum BBNode depth, SurfaceTree node and leaf counts, CurveTree
count and leaf count, and SurfaceTree construction time.  Cache version zero
can report the deserialized hierarchy sizes but reports the requested depth
as unknown, because that fact was not stored by the old format.  Any eventual
production-default or cache-layout change must bump or explicitly migrate the
cache version.

rt_brep_ray accepts --surface-tree-depth=0..8 with any focused mode and prints
aggregate prep measurements at process exit.  Direct test solids and in-memory
database models use the same per-instance control; no shot-time allocation or
thread-global setting was introduced.  brep-audit accepts
--surface-tree-depth with --surface-trees-only and includes the requested and
observed depths plus total/per-face node counts in its serial profile.  A
compile-time assertion prevents the librt test default from silently
disagreeing with libbrep's BREP_MAX_FT_DEPTH.

The isolated Cobb trim/edge defect gate passes unchanged at every controlled
depth from eight through zero.  At all nine levels it selects all 96 material
rays, deliberately falls back for the same 24 exact contacts, publishes the
same two tolerance-healed transitions, and has the same 2.02e-8 maximum
endpoint error.  Its nine-solid prep measurements are:

  limit   BBNodes   leaves   SurfaceTree seconds
    8      136566    68796          0.308134
    7       68094    34560          0.155170
    6       33534    17280          0.077380
    5       16254     8640          0.035225
    4        7614     4320          0.021501
    3        3294     2160          0.011600
    2        1134      864          0.008249
    1         270      216          0.006677
    0          54       54          0.006135

The 54 CurveTrees and 221 CurveTree leaves are unchanged.  This proves that
the new certified seam/trim component path does not depend on dense initial
surface boxes for this deliberately defective corpus.  At the time of this
measurement, observed BBNode depth could exceed a requested nonzero limit
because knot splits consumed part of the same recursion counter before
adaptive recursion restarted; for example, some limit-seven models reached
depth nine.  However, the termination check still preceded the knot
predicate, so a sufficiently small limit could truncate structural
decomposition.  The newer structural-knot increment above fixes that
semantic defect.  Treat this table as a historical measurement of the
one-span Cobb fixtures, not as proof that the old implementation always
exhausted arbitrary surface knots.

The broader experiments locate a concrete current boundary:

  * non-isoparametric and contact/trim gates pass at limit zero;
  * the complete affine and affine-grazing gate passes at limit two;
  * limits zero through six fail the same six material near-grazing sphere
    rays, at h/T ratios 10, 2, and 1.1 in both directions;
  * limit seven passes the complete sphere and torus grazing/status-2 gate.

For those six limit-six failures, the prepared path finds the two correct
opposite-direction local roots in three coarse boxes.  They occupy one
connected face/span component, however, so the existing single-regular-root
theorem cannot exclude the component complement.  No complete physical event
transaction is published, and the conservative whole-ray fallback sees only
one root in the sparse legacy leaves.  The correct next step is a bounded
same-face two-regular-root theorem: independently contract disjoint root
neighborhoods, prove determinant direction and outward t enclosure for each,
and exclude the remainder of the shared hull before atomically publishing the
ENTER/LEAVE pair.  Simply lowering the default to seven would preserve this
known proof gap and is not yet justified.

At limit seven, the directed correctness shard passes with 13 BREP preps and
203149/108055 BBNodes/leaves.  The paired Cauchy-Crofton gate passes 128000
identical implicit/BREP lines across the sphere, ellipsoid, box, cylinder,
cone, torus, CSG unions/intersections/subtractions, transformed nested sphere
shell, and sewn Cobb sphere with zero differing lines.  Maximum endpoint
error is 1.07e-7; the aggregate estimates and checkpoint confidence intervals
also agree.  Endpoint, non-isoparametric, contact/trim, affine, defect, and
grazing shards pass at limit seven.  The long comprehensive seam shard also
passes with 78 BREP preps, 560493/285828 BBNodes/leaves, and all classifier,
vertex, crack, isolated-defect, and transform ratchets unchanged.

A brep-audit smoke comparison on the 462-face Product_step18_item.s from
step_corpus_g/non_grabcad/0353/0353-00-24.g validates the same measurement path
on imported geometry.  Limit eight reports 123919/62451 nodes/leaves, maximum
depth ten, 2.51 seconds, and 59.4 MB peak RSS.  Limit seven reports
66943/33985, maximum depth nine, 1.43 seconds, and 48.0 MB peak RSS.  The 13060
CurveTree leaves are unchanged.  This single-object result is evidence that
the synthetic reduction is relevant, but it is not yet a corpus-wide timing
or memory claim.  The ASAN/UBSAN defect and grazing gates both pass at limit
seven with leak scanning disabled and halt-on-error enabled; neither reports
a sanitizer diagnostic.

There is no sign that the intended solution is failing to converge.  Sparse
tree failures have become smaller and more classifiable as local proof
coverage grows.  Exact tangency is necessarily a topological transition from
two crossings to one contact to a miss, so the contract is stable
tolerance-relative classification and monotone limiting behavior, not a
continuous raw hit count at the singular point.  The tractable target is
valid and tolerance-healable solid BREPs.  Arbitrarily defective topology or
singular parametrizations cannot be assigned a unique solid meaning without
an explicit policy and remain bounded-fallback cases.


2026-08-09 ACTIVE TOLERANCE-HEALED EDGE TRANSITION CHECKPOINT

The preceding certified-component increment is committed locally as
1ae947e957, "Certify local BREP seam root components".  It was not pushed.
The current uncommitted increment classifies and resolves all six material
trim-only Cobb fallbacks that remained at that checkpoint.  SurfaceTree
density, cache data, and allocation behavior are still unchanged.

Four cases were rejected at the edge-candidate entry point.  Their angular
edge-sector states were finite and opposite, and their actual incident
surface roots were non-grazing, but line_state_valid was also conditioned on
the fixed BREP_GRAZING_DOT_TOL applied to a face normal evaluated at a
displaced trim-frame lift.  That mixed a topological wedge question with a
conditioning estimate from a discrepant location.  line_state_valid now
depends only on the nonzero angular wedge states.  This does not publish a hit
by itself: the complete incident root/box transaction still requires finite,
non-grazing surface roots, exact represented-edge authorization, and the
applicable local component theorem.

The other two cases are the g/T=0.9, chord/T=1.1, inward trim-bow rays in both
directions.  The literal displaced trim frame says contact on both sides, but
the complete incident root set has one root on each face and every root gives
the same ENTER or LEAVE direction.  A nearby opposite regular root lies more
than T away and shares the same coarse terminal component.  The edge
candidate may now record this as a provisional tolerance-healed transition,
but only under all of these conditions:

  * prep established the strict edge correspondence and discrepancy envelope;
  * the ray has an exact represented-edge witness rather than a near-edge snap;
  * both incident faces contribute complete, finite, non-grazing root/box
    observations with one common crossing direction;
  * no selected root or box conflicts with another candidate; and
  * the joint boundary/regular theorem contracts both local roots, excludes
    the complete remainder of their connected hull, certifies the boundary
    box's determinant sign, applies face reversal, and agrees with the
    proposed crossing direction.

The transition remains unpublished if any joint precondition or proof fails.
Promoted boxes receive RESOLVED_BOUNDARY rather than RESOLVED_CONTACT.  New
trace fields record edge-candidate failure stages and the count of successfully
published tolerance transitions, so later changes cannot silently broaden
this policy.

The isolated defect ratchet now has 96 prepared selections and 24 explicit
UNCERTIFIED fallbacks.  Trim-only selects 32/40 and edge-only selects 64/80.
Thus every one of the 96 material rays is handled by the prepared production
pipeline; the only fallbacks are the 24 exact zero-length contacts.  The
matrix publishes 36 ordinary regular components, 60 joint edge/regular
components, 64 common-lift exact witnesses, and two tolerance-healed
transitions.  Its fixed complement stacks visit 1,068 boxes in aggregate and
reach high-water four.  Maximum endpoint error is 2.02e-8 and maximum relative
defect calibration error is 9.32e-6.

A separate transform-invariance gate replays the newly healed transition in
both ray directions after arbitrary rotation and translation at scales 0.01,
1, and 10,000.  All six rays require exact discrepancy authorization, one
joint component, one published edge transition, the tolerance-transition
marker, a valid directed material interval, and prepared-production
selection.  All pass.  The worst endpoint error normalized back to base model
units is 6.22e-9.

2026-08-09 CERTIFIED SEAM/REGULAR COMPONENT CHECKPOINT

The isolated Cobb defect corpus is committed locally as 6afabb5efd, "Add
isolated Cobb trim and edge defects".  The certified component increment is
this checkpoint's local commit, "Certify local BREP seam root components".
Neither was pushed.  The latter replaces most of the corpus's deliberately
recorded whole-ray fallbacks with fixed-workspace proofs.  SurfaceTree density
and cache format are unchanged.

The first new theorem owns a connected many-boxes-to-one-root component on one
face and Bezier span.  Seed boxes must match immutable numerical observations
of one regular root and have a regular or unresolved disposition.  Rootless
unresolved boxes may join only by UV/t adjacency.  The complete component hull
is restricted with outward coefficient error and accepted either by one
strict Krawczyk inclusion or by a smaller expansion-valued contraction box
whose entire hull complement is excluded by direct/rotated Bernstein bounds.
A constant determinant sign and outward ray-parameter interval are then
certified before any box disposition or physical event is committed.

The exact manifold-edge theorem now also accepts a displaced 3-D edge only
when both independently evaluated incident trim lifts coincide with each
other and with the represented ray to coordinate roundoff.  Both lifts must
remain inside the edge discrepancy tolerance established by the prep-time
global correspondence and strict gap-bound proof.  This does not move a
merely near ray onto an edge.  It supplies a topologically exact witness for
the isolated edge-only Cobb variants, whose surface seam and trims agree but
whose 3-D edge curve is bowed by less than T.  A CLEAN_MISS surface observation
may participate only when trim classification explicitly says it is within
the authorized near-trim band; arbitrary outside roots remain ineligible.

The difficult close-graze case contains two physical roots in the same coarse
terminal boxes: one exact seam/edge root and one nearby regular root of the
opposite direction.  A joint theorem now collects the complete connected
component and independently contracts both roots.  Each ownership box is at
most 0.2 times their normalized L-infinity separation, so the two certified
neighborhoods are disjoint.  The contraction routine retains the largest
successfully certified neighborhood for complement ownership.  A generalized
fixed-stack complement walk aligns subdivisions to both local boxes and proves
that there is no third root.  The regular event then uses the smallest nested
box which independently passes coefficient restriction, strict Krawczyk
inclusion, constant determinant sign, duplicate-observation containment, and
outward t enclosure.  The boundary event uses only the exact topological
witness's roundoff t interval.  This separation of wide ownership from tight
event enclosure is necessary: tiny boxes can be dominated by restriction
roundoff, while the widest unique-root box may produce overlapping event
intervals.

All new ray-time arrays are fixed capacity.  The complement walk has a
64-entry stack, a depth-24 limit, and an 8,192-box visit limit.  On the current
positive matrix it visits 954 boxes in aggregate and reaches stack high-water
four.  No allocation, random perturbation, or mutable global surface state was
introduced.

The isolated defect ratchet now passes with 90 prepared selections and 30
explicit UNCERTIFIED fallbacks, improved from the original 0/120 baseline and
the intermediate 36/84 regular-component checkpoint.  Trim-only selects 26 of
40 rays; edge-only selects 64 of 80.  All 64 material edge-only rays use the
common-lift exact witness.  The proofs publish 36 ordinary regular components
and 54 joint edge/regular components.  All 24 exact-contact rays continue to
fall back deliberately, leaving only six material trim-only fallbacks.  Two of
those reach but fail the joint preconditions; four do not yet form an exact
edge candidate.  Every final interval still matches the analytic sphere and
implicit ELL oracle.  Maximum endpoint error is 4.78e-8 and maximum relative
defect calibration error is 9.32e-6.

Focused optimized verification completed so far:

  cmake --build .build --target rt_brep_ray -j8
  .build/src/librt/tests/rt_brep_ray --defect-only
  ctest --test-dir .build -j8 --output-on-failure -R
    '^(rt_brep_ray_(defect|grazing_root|affine|crofton|seam|local_root|
    source_union|trim_interval|interval))$'

All nine shards pass.  The comprehensive seam sweep took 199.93 seconds and
the whole parallel batch took 199.98 seconds, demonstrating that independent
test-process parallelism captures essentially all of the shorter shards'
wall-clock cost.  The final ASAN/UBSAN defect run also passes with
detect_leaks=0 and halt-on-error enabled, with no sanitizer diagnostic.  Leak
scanning remains disabled for the previously documented host ptrace-wrapper

This result strengthens the tractability assessment.  The observed failure
was a finite proof-partition problem rather than uncontrolled iteration: the

boxes moved it past that stage, and separating ownership from event enclosure

2026-08-09 ACTIVE ISOLATED TRIM/EDGE DEFECT CHECKPOINT

The nested CSG-leaf increment is committed locally as e60c0fbcc7, "Test
nested transformed BREP-leaf CSG".  It was not pushed.  The current increment
adds the first independently isolated trim-only and edge-only Cobb
representations and registers them as the parallel rt_brep_ray_defect CTest
shard.

The trim-only generator leaves all six rational sphere surfaces and the shared
3-D edge fixed, bows only one incident 2-D trim toward its face interior, and
records the independently measured U/V displacement in the corresponding
anisotropic ON_BrepTrim tolerance fields.  Because the pristine Cobb face uses
its natural surface boundary, the opposite bow would leave the valid surface
domain; this first valid-model matrix therefore covers the inward trim sign.
The edge-only generator leaves both surface lifts and both trims fixed, copies
the shared rational edge curve, and bows only its interior control points
radially inward or outward while preserving its endpoints and weights.  Both
generators calibrate the maximum 3-D edge/lift discrepancy by dense sampling,
preserve roundoff-level surface radial error, and set the target edge's
declared tolerance to the unchanged model T only after calibration.

The ray geometry was corrected during development.  A line centered under the
seam by h has roots O(sqrt(R*h)) away from the seam and primarily tests generic
grazing isolation.  It does not isolate a sub-T seam defect.  The permanent
fixture instead fixes one analytic sphere endpoint exactly at the target seam
midpoint.  If the desired chord is L, it chooses a unit conormal/radial
direction D satisfying N dot D = -L/(2R), so the other endpoint is P+L*D.
The expected directed interval is exactly [2R,2R+L].  It uses L/T = 100, 10,
2, and 1.1 plus exact contact L=0, both conormal signs, and ray reversal.

The complete valid-model matrix has g/T = 0.25 and 0.9: 40 trim-only rays and
80 edge-only rays, 120 total including 24 exact contacts.  Every final BREP
answer matches the analytic sphere and the implicit ELL oracle; maximum BREP
endpoint error is 7.08e-8.  The discrepancy calibration error is at most
9.32e-6 relative.  All strict prep-time correspondence, endpoint, and gap
bounds pass.  The baseline deliberately records zero prepared selections and
120 explicit UNCERTIFIED whole-ray fallbacks, so a later theorem can ratchet
that count upward without hiding a final-answer regression.

The failure population is localized.  Trim-only rays invoke the exact
manifold-edge resolver 40 times but form no publishable candidate because the
surface terminal boxes at the seam endpoint are still unresolved.  Edge-only
rays cannot use an exact edge witness after the 3-D edge locus moves; they make
80 seam attempts with 192 eligible local-root observations, all conservatively
falling back.  A representative 100T chord has three local observations at
the seam endpoint (one trim miss and one tolerance-near incident-face hit) and
the second material endpoint, with six terminal boxes.  Boxes duplicate about
the surface subdivision boundary v=0.5 and have neither Krawczyk orientation
nor a complete fold certificate.  This identifies the next authority needed:
a bounded component/root theorem which can own all duplicate terminal boxes,
certify orientation and trim/edge containment, and publish the seam endpoint
transactionally.  Merely loosening hit cleanup or proximity tests is not an
acceptable fix.

2026-08-09 ACTIVE NESTED CSG-LEAF CHECKPOINT

The status-2 similarity increment is committed locally as 8fea341aa0,

current increment extends the topology-identical implicit-leaf versus
BREP-leaf CSG oracle in src/librt/tests/brep_ray.cpp.

The new in-memory fixture preserves one nested Boolean tree in both models:

  oracle.r = (outer.s translated -8 union outer.s translated +8)
             subtract (inner.s translated -8)

outer.s is one reused radius-5 sphere definition and inner.s is a radius-2
sphere.  One model exports the original ELL leaves; the other replaces those
same definitions with their exact ft_brep conversions before applying the
identical member matrices and Boolean operators.  It therefore tests a reused
transformed BREP leaf, a non-region nested union combination, subtraction,
an internal cavity, two disconnected material components, and three ordered
partitions on one ray without conflating those checks with evaluated
region-to-one-BREP conversion.

The independent analytic oracles are:

  area   = 2(4*pi*5^2) + 4*pi*2^2 = 216*pi
  volume = 2(4*pi*5^3/3) - 4*pi*2^3/3 = (968/3)*pi
  bounds = [-13,-5,-5] through [13,5,5]

Four directed rays require the exact forward three-interval partition, its
reverse, the left shell's two intervals, and the right solid's one interval.
The shared fixed-seed Cauchy-Crofton run then shoots 8,000 identical lines
through the implicit and BREP-leaf hierarchies.  It passes with zero differing
lines, zero tangent-band exceptions, maximum paired endpoint error 3.84e-8,
signed total chord difference 5.80e-6, and absolute total chord difference
6.88e-6.  At 8,000 lines both representations estimate area 697.640914
(standard error 15.1) and volume 1059.37503 (standard error 23.5), within the
predeclared confidence bands around 216*pi and (968/3)*pi.  Equality of the
paired estimates is not accepted in place of the analytic gate; both are
checked independently.

Verified in this increment:

  cmake --build .build --target rt_brep_ray -j8
  .build/src/librt/tests/rt_brep_ray --crofton-only

Both pass.  The cache-object warnings printed during the run are the known

2026-08-09 ACTIVE STATUS-2 SIMILARITY CHECKPOINT

The preceding grazing increment is committed locally as 14d4c7c65a,
"Resolve status-2 BREP grazing roots locally".  The similarity increment
described below was subsequently committed as 8fea341aa0, "Qualify status-2
grazing under similarities".  Neither commit was pushed.

The new matrix transforms the difficult status-2 torus tube section by an
arbitrary rotation plus translation at scales 1, 0.01, and 10,000.  It scales
the model tolerance with the geometry and shoots the 2T and 1.1T chords in
both directions.  All 12 rays now require one analytic BREP interval, status-2
mapping, selected prepared production events, exact expansion availability,
rigorous exclusion, bounded refinement, and no arithmetic or workspace
exhaustion.  The matrix passes with all 12 selected.  Maximum normalized BREP
endpoint error is 1.34e-5 model units; maximum refinement/high-water is 17/15,
well below the fixed 64-refinement and expansion capacities.

The test exposed two distinct transform effects.  First, the implicit TOR
quartic returns two segments rather than the one analytic normal-section
interval on all four large-scale cases.  These are recorded as four implicit
artifacts; the analytic circle remains the boundary oracle.  Second, the
large 1.1T status-2 BREP reaches one regular box plus one complete fold box.
Its conservative box intervals prove a strict gap greater than T, but optional
nested fold-localization attempts report some failed levels.  The mixed
fold/regular eligibility check previously treated any failed optional level as
a failed theorem and fell back.  It now consumes the actual stored fold
interval (either the best certified contraction or the complete parent box)
and lets the existing strict outward gap proof decide.  This is conservative
and restores similarity invariance; localization failures remain telemetry.

  .build/src/librt/tests/rt_brep_ray --directed-only
  .build/src/librt/tests/rt_brep_ray --affine-only

All pass.  The directed partition includes the new status-2 transform matrix
and the existing status-1 sphere similarity matrix.  Before the next commit,
run git diff --check and review the focused diff for cleanup and ownership.


2026-08-09 ACTIVE GRAZING CHECKPOINT

The current uncommitted checkpoint adds a rational torus normal-section
ratchet and the first production use of bounded, on-demand exact refinement.
The authoritative modified files are src/librt/librt_private.h,
src/librt/primitives/brep/brep.cpp, src/librt/primitives/brep/brep_local.h,
and src/librt/tests/brep_ray.cpp.  Inspect git status and diff before resuming;
do not discard this work.

The full torus conversion is an ON_RevSurface.  Its GetNurbForm return value is
2, meaning the converted NURBS has the same locus but a nonidentity parameter
map.  The prepared-span builder now accepts status 2 and records the status per
face.  Solver boxes and certificates remain in NURBS parameter space.  Final
root points are mapped with GetSurfaceParameterFromNurbFormParameter, checked
by the inverse map and by model-space point agreement, then classified against
the original face's normal and trims.  Published hit UV is in original-surface
space.  Status-2 seam, edge, and vertex publication still fails closed because
there is not yet an interval enclosure for that parameter map; only clean
interior regular or complete fold events may publish.

The torus ratchet uses two analytic circular normal sections away from seams:
the tube circle and a major-direction circle.  It shoots both ray directions
at chord lengths 100T, 10T, 2T, 1.1T, 1T, 0.9T, 0.5T, 0.1T, and 0.01T, where T
is the model minimum distance.  The analytic circle is authoritative at the
boundary.  All 100T, 10T, 2T, and 1.1T cases now publish the correct full-solid
BREP interval in both directions.  Exactly 1T is deliberately classified
ambiguous by the certified event stream and falls back: legacy returns a
tolerance-sized segment for the sphere and torus major section but no segment
for the torus tube section.  Below T no prepared material interval publishes.
Exact tangency has contact roots but no prepared material interval, and all
analytic outside cases have no BREP material interval.

The improvement is local and allocation-free; SurfaceTree density is
unchanged.  Ordinary isolation still has its depth-24 budget.  At this
checkpoint the extra tier is enabled only for status-2 faces, whose production
gate already permits clean interior regular/fold events and rejects seam,
edge, and vertex publication.  A terminal status-2 box which remains
unresolved uses the exact per-coefficient expansion intervals already prepared
for the ray/surface equations.  It applies direct
and conditioned/rotated Bernstein hull exclusion and a tight expansion
Krawczyk attempt.  If the exact rotated hull retains the box, a fixed local
refinement may split it for at most eight additional per-box levels and 64
total per-ray refinements.  A certified regular box reached through this path
is refined further when its ray-parameter interval exceeds 0.01T, so two roots
only 1.1T apart can still prove a strictly resolved outward gap.  All work uses
the existing 64-entry stack and fixed coefficient arrays; there is no malloc
in the ray path.  At the 2T torus tube case the exact rotated hull eliminates
the spurious middle/far box that the worst-common-error representation could
not separate.  At 1.1T further local splits isolate two signed Krawczyk boxes
whose outward t gap exceeds T.

An experimental theorem which joined the opposite root box with a connected
rootless box was removed.  Component ownership succeeded, but both ordinary
and expansion-valued Krawczyk correctly refused the union because it included
the singular fold neighborhood.  It certified no controlled positive case
and would only have added production cost.  The narrower two-box mixed
fold/regular theorem remains: it resolves the previously observed 10T case
when one complete fold parent and one regular parent own the two roots.

The dense legacy SurfaceTree path on the original tube-direction torus case
visits 24 leaves and returns only one of the two physical roots in either ray
direction, hence no solid segment.  This confirms that the problem is missing
root isolation, not later hit cleanup.  The new prepared path now supplies the
analytic interval above T without changing the tree.

The implicit TOR quartic is not a trustworthy boundary oracle: this fixture
currently observes one false tolerance-sized segment on each exact tangent
and 16 false outside segments across the 18 directed outside samples for each
normal-section family.  These are recorded telemetry.  Positive-clearance
implicit endpoints still match the analytic section.  The analytic circle,
not the implicit quartic near zero clearance, gates BREP correctness.

ESTABLISHED FINDINGS
--------------------

1. Commit 23eabb2c056fc7d0c4ccc6c5d9299f72eedbb432 correctly replaced the
   unsafe file-static SurfaceTree::hasSplit cache with a per-SurfaceTree
   SurfaceSplitCache.  Do not restore the static cache.

2. brep_build_bvh currently leaks each SurfaceTree.  This leak prevents
   SurfaceTree::~SurfaceTree from double-deleting its root after that root is
   attached to the master BVH.  CurveTrees likewise lack an owner after cache
   deserialization.  No repository reader of ON_BrepFace::m_face_user.p was
   found.

3. The current Newton solver fails to accept an initially converged root before
   checking its Jacobian.  Its singular "jitter" is identically zero on the
   first iteration and uses drand48.  Ev1Der/finite/normal results need checks.

4. The current fixed BREP_EDGE_MISS_TOLERANCE is used in incompatible UV and
   model-space roles.  This will be addressed only after Phase 1 behavior is
   isolated and tested.

5. The historical Cobb generator is a valid but non-solid six-face patch set
   with 24 independent edges.  libbrep now provides both that representation
   and a valid, closed, oriented variant with 8 shared vertices, 12 shared
   edges, and 24 trims.  The support surfaces are an exact sphere to sampled
   roundoff.  Controlled seam/trim perturbations are still required.

6. Primary correctness validation will pair original implicit/CSG raytracing
   with converted BREP raytracing on identical lines, including Crofton
   crossing/chord statistics.  Grazing tests will sweep signed clearance and
   verify the two-root -> tangent-contact -> no-root limit.

7. The old native ID_BSPLINE ray path dates to the 1991 Release 4.0 NURB
   module.  It was compile-time unreachable after ID_BSPLINE began converting
   to BREP in 2009, and it could not compile if re-enabled after its hit-list
   helpers were deleted in 2010.  Its live nmg_nurb_intersect engine projects
   a surface onto two ray planes, applies control-hull clipping, and recursively
   subdivides.  Those concepts are useful precedents, but the implementation
   allocates and copies surfaces during recursion, has fixed-size/order
   assumptions, stops on a UV-only tolerance, has no trim support, and the old
   wrapper fabricated a 0.01-unit exit for odd hit counts.  Do not port it.

8. The first R=10 mm grazing sweep found a deterministic but incorrect
   baseline.  As h -> 0+ the converted BREP chord stopped converging and
   approached about 0.001812758 mm.  At exact tangency it reported that false
   interval, and it reported false intervals for small negative h out to
   between approximately -7.45e-8 mm and -1.49e-7 mm.  The conditioned root
   test described in Finding 11 has repaired this baseline.  Keep the history
   as evidence for why the permanent exact-tangent and negative-h gates exist.

9. Solver work follows a corpus-first ratchet rule learned from the STEP work:
   reproduce difficult cases in C/C++ with analytic or paired implicit
   oracles, keep permanent passing gates, permit documented defects to improve
   without freezing their bad answer, and promote each repaired defect to a
   hard gate.  Avoid isolated changes tested only against their motivating
   model.

10. A ray starting exactly on the +X sphere seam and pointing outward is a
    raw miss in rt_sph_shot, which explicitly rejects an outside/on-surface
    origin pointing away.  Current BREP shot instead returns the wholly
    nonpositive interval [-2R, 0].  The in-memory corpus permits that known
    result to disappear, but requires determinism and forbids any forward
    material.  Once BREP applies the normal half-ray/partition policy, promote
    this case to exact implicit parity.

11. A fixed perpendicular line residual is not a sufficient root criterion at
    tangency.  For a unit surface normal N and ray direction D, residual divided
    by |N dot D|/|D| is the first-order error along the ray.  Root acceptance
    now requires that estimate to satisfy the numerical root tolerance, with a
    1e-13 residual floor for exact/double contact.  Residuals are evaluated
    from S-ray_origin instead of a global plane offset, avoiding cancellation
    on million-unit translated fixtures.  This retained the singular north
    pole, small/large scale, and translated cases while repairing the grazing
    limit.  This is solver conditioning, not the final contact or seam policy.

12. The correct first CSG stress test preserves the Boolean hierarchy and
    replaces only exactly convertible implicit leaves with BREP leaves.  It
    does not collapse the tree through ON_Boolean.  Disjoint and overlapping
    sphere unions, a sphere-intersection lens, a concentric subtraction
    cavity, and box-minus-cylinder all agree on every directed and shared line
    in this form.

13. A diagnostic experiment that did collapse the same binary trees to single
    evaluated BREPs separated Boolean-conversion defects from ray-solver
    defects.  Disjoint sphere union and concentric cavity happened to match,
    but overlapping union, intersection lens, and box-minus-cylinder were
    geometrically wrong before raytracing.  At 8,000 lines, overlapping union
    produced BREP area/volume about 313.2/527.7 versus implicit 497.6/937.5;
    the lens produced about 265.4/469.5 versus 121.9/105.5; and the drilled
    box produced zero BREP chord volume with invalid partitions.  Treat this
    as a later ON_Boolean/conversion qualification problem, not evidence
    against the leaf BREP intersection solver.

14. Two later-stage duality tiers are now specified in the plan: replacing
    exactly convertible leaves throughout larger existing CSG databases while
    preserving their trees, and using the experimental `brep csg`/
    brep_to_csg shape recognizer to reconstruct full or partial implicit trees
    from controlled and NIST BREP examples.  Fully implicit reconstructions
    are independent oracles; mixed reconstructions must disclose residual
    BREP leaves and make a weaker claim.

15. The openNURBS expert NewFace documentation says its vertex indices are
    ordered (SW, SE, NW, NE), but NewOuterLoop in the implementation consumes
    them in boundary order (SW, SE, NE, NW) and relates edge i to vertices i
    and (i+1)%4.  Reusing two adjacent Cobb edges exposed the inconsistency.
    Cobb construction follows the implementation order and the topology tests
    make this dependency observable.

16. The first controlled bowed-seam sweep proves that current tolerance logic
    does not reliably preserve the intended solid.  Moving only the three
    interior boundary CVs of one Cobb face inward, while preserving endpoints,
    shared topology, and declaring the measured edge tolerance, can remove one
    near-grazing root.  At g/T=1 the defect persists through h/T=0.9, where the
    analytic two-root separation is about 379T (a 0.1897 mm chord for the
    current T=0.0005 mm).  Normal partition assembly may then discard the
    entire interval.  The 416-ray matrix currently records 15 below-envelope
    zero-partition leaks and one forward/reverse inconsistency.  Leakage does
    not currently spread to h greater than the measured gap; that is now a
    one-way ratchet, while any repaired cases are allowed to disappear.

17. Near tangency, a surface displacement g can amplify into an along-ray
    endpoint change much larger than g, approximately through the same
    1/|N dot D| conditioning governing the solver.  Therefore seam validation
    cannot gate endpoint error directly by g.  It must distinguish geometric
    support uncertainty, root conditioning, interval existence/winding, and
    final minimum-segment policy.

18. The arbitrary-direction nominal h=0 seam lines expose a zero-chord
    implicit contact partition to the normal callback, with tangent normals
    that intentionally fail material entry/exit orientation.  Raw axis-aligned
    exact tangency remains a miss.  Machine reports preserve this distinction;
    contact events must be canonicalized explicitly rather than being treated
    as ordinary material partitions.

19. Raw-shot instrumentation localizes 14 of the 15 below-envelope crack
    leaks before legacy trim and event cleanup: fewer than two distinct
    geometric roots were found.  For inward g/T=1, h/T=0.9, two subdivision
    leaves converge to the same clean adjacent-face root at t about 19.9051;
    duplicate cleanup correctly reduces them to one event, while the bowed
    target support has no mathematical intersection.  Reversing the line
    produces the same physical root at t about 20.0949.  The missing solid
    crossing therefore requires a topology-local seam closure event inside
    the authorized 3D edge envelope.  It cannot be repaired by loosening the
    Newton residual, accepting a trim miss, or retaining duplicate seeds.
    The remaining leak has at least two distinct candidates and is lost in
    cleanup; no leak in this matrix is caused by trim classification.

20. A direct line-to-shared-edge observation supplies the missing model-space
    evidence independently of the UV trim tree.  Across all 416 bowed-seam
    rays, the target-edge distance follows |h| with maximum error about
    3.59e-15 mm, is unchanged by ray reversal, and qualifies exactly inside
    the edge's declared tolerance.  All 15 below-envelope leaks have this
    target-edge evidence; target-edge observations outside the declared
    envelope do not qualify.  The current implementation is deliberately
    trace-only and uses a fixed sampling/refinement budget.  It must not
    change production results until prepared edge spans provide conservative
    exclusion and the local manifold-sector transition is implemented.

21. Eligible two-trim edges can be prepared without enlarging the serialized
    cache format.  Exact-parameter NURBS forms are decomposed into nonempty
    rational Bezier spans; positive weights make the Euclidean control-point
    hull a conservative curve bound.  A line within the declared edge
    tolerance must intersect the span box expanded by that tolerance.  Cobb
    produces 12 supported edges and 12 spans, and every one of the 15 current
    leaks selects its target span conservatively.  Curves whose NURBS form
    changes parameterization, nonpositive or invalid weights, incomplete
    spans, and malformed topology are explicitly unsupported and remain on
    the legacy fallback path.  The bounded closest-point estimate remains
    diagnostic rather than a proof; only box exclusion is presently claimed
    conservative.

22. Edge proximity alone is insufficient: the closest ray point must also be
    on the intended material side of an authorized manifold sector.  The
    shadow classifier maps the edge fraction to both trims with m_bRev3d,
    evaluates both surface lifts and oriented normals, projects loop-interior
    conormals into the edge cross-section, and selects the interior angular
    sector from the material side of both faces.  It rejects the sector unless
    both lifts are within the then-effective edge tolerance.  A sharp convex ARB8
    edge, a 270-degree concave edge on a capped L extrusion, and a periodic
    same-face sphere seam now permanently check inside/contact/outside offsets
    in both ray directions.  With the initial model/declared tolerance policy,
    252 target observations in the bowed Cobb matrix qualify: 152 inside, 32
    contact, and 68 outside; all 15 current crack leaks have valid inside-
    sector evidence.  Maximum target lift-discrepancy error is about
    2.51e-15 mm.  This remains trace-only.  General STEP use still needs
    stronger trim/edge parameter-correspondence qualification and vertex-fan
    fixtures.

23. An unresolved solid hit can be considered for topology-local closure only
    when exactly one eligible inside-sector edge is incident to its existing
    face and lies on the correct side of that event along the ray.  The new
    shadow classifier records the edge, its projected t, the existing root t,
    and whether the missing event is entering or leaving.  It identifies all
    15 current below-envelope Cobb leaks with exactly one candidate: forward
    rays have a missing leave after the existing enter, and reversed rays have
    a missing enter before the existing leave.  It does not activate on the
    target edge's contact or exterior states.  Identity, rotated/translated,
    0.01x, and 10,000x copies preserve all sector and closure classifications
    when both geometric and modeling tolerances are scaled as lengths.  The
    small-scale nonlinear root moves about 6e-7 mm in baseline-normalized
    coordinates, well below T=0.0005 mm; classifier invariance and solver
    accuracy therefore have separate gates.  This remains trace-only and does
    not emit a synthetic hit.  The edge projection is not an accurate missing
    endpoint near tangency, so production activation requires a bounded local
    continuation solve rather than using that projection as the root.

24. Exact-parameter positive-weight NURBS surface spans can be prepared by the
    same policy used for edge spans.  Each face record owns its rational
    Bezier patches, original UV domains, and Euclidean control-hull box.
