    Reparameterized GetNurbForm results, invalid/nonpositive weights, malformed
    knot spans, and incomplete conversions mark the complete face unsupported
    rather than admitting unsafe partial data.  The two homogeneous ray-plane
    numerator coefficient hulls now conservatively reject spans in trace mode.
    Their floating-point margin includes the pre-cancellation |CV|+|O*W|
    arithmetic scale, not just the final coefficient magnitude; this is
    essential for translated geometry.  All six Cobb faces produce one span,
    and the representative grazing line retains two of six spans.  Candidate
    counts are invariant across the tested similarities.  Surface spans are
    rebuilt from the cached BREP, so cache format version zero is unchanged.

25. For the controlled bowed seam, the existing adjacent-face root supplies a
    deterministic seed for a shadow continuation: map the authorized edge to
    that face's trim, reflect the root across the local trim tangent, and solve
    the prepared Bezier support in a bounded extension outside its ordinary
    [0,1]^2 span.  A fixed 24-iteration Newton/line-search budget uses the same
    conditioned ray residual and does no ray-time allocation.  All 15 current
    below-envelope leaks yield exactly one continuation with the required
    orientation and ordering.  Maximum error against the implicit sphere's
    missing endpoint is about 2.71e-8 mm.  The g/T=1, h/T=0.9 representative
    converges in three iterations from the reflected seed and agrees with the
    implicit endpoint to about 1e-12 mm in both ray directions.  Identity,
    rigid, 0.01x, and 10,000x cases preserve the candidate and continuation
    classifications.  This is deliberately not production-ready: reflection
    in parameter space is only a seed, the extension has not yet been covered
    by conservative coefficient subdivision, and vertex/multiple-root cases
    need explicit fallback and uniqueness proofs.  No synthetic hit is
    emitted.

26. Tensor-product scalar Bernstein nets are sufficient for allocation-free
    conservative root isolation on the ordinary [0,1]^2 surface span.  The
    prepared homogeneous control points generate two ray-plane numerator nets
    plus a ray-t numerator and positive weight net.  A depth-first stack keeps
    only parameter boxes; fixed scratch arrays re-extract each subdomain with
    de Casteljau restriction.  Orders above 16 make the face unsupported, the
    pending stack is capped at 64, and depth is capped at 24.  Every capacity,
    overflow, maximum depth, visited-box count, retained-box count, and stack
    high-water mark is observable.  Retained UV boxes carry conservative ray-t
    intervals from the rational convex hull.  Across the bowed Cobb matrix the
    maximum is 226 visited boxes, six retained boxes, depth 24, and stack high-
    water 24; every legacy Newton root lies in both a retained UV box and its
    t interval.  The representative root is narrowed to duplicate
    trim-boundary boxes with t about [19.90313,19.90660], far smaller than its
    0.1897 mm analytic chord.  The ordinary sphere grazing sweep retains both
    analytic roots down through one model tolerance and no one box covers both
    roots once the chord is at least 10T.  Exact tangency retains a contact box
    while production emits no interval.  On the miss side, only nine
    floating-limit cases remain ambiguous and the largest clearance is about
    2.27e-9 T; ambiguity beyond 1e-6 T is now forbidden.  Peak sphere work is
    228 boxes, eight retained boxes, and stack high-water 25.  Similarity cases
    preserve candidate, visited, and retained counts.  This still needs safe
    coefficient treatment for the continuation's outside-span extension and
    an adaptive termination/box corrector before it can replace dense trees.

27. A bounded Bezier continuation interval can be handled without assuming
    the rational convex-hull property survives arbitrary extrapolation.
    Algebraic de Casteljau reparameterization maps the requested outside-span
    rectangle back to [0,1]^2.  Its exact control-net operator infinity norm
    is computed from transformed Bernstein basis vectors and used to amplify
    all inherited coefficient error bounds.  The reparameterized weight hull
    must remain positive beyond that error or the certificate fails.  The
    normal fixed-capacity isolator then proves which UV/t boxes remain.  A
    continuation is certified only when every retained box contains the
    solved root cluster and none can also contain the existing root.  All 15
    below-envelope leaks pass.  Maximum certificate work is 93 boxes, stack
    high-water 20, and maximum retained t-cluster width about 5.15e-5 mm;
    there are no positive-weight, workspace, trace-capacity, or existing-root
    overlaps.  The certified transition pair assembles exactly one shadow
    material segment for all 15 leaks, with both endpoints within 1e-7 mm of
    the implicit sphere interval in both ray directions.  Normal segment
    emission is still unchanged.  General activation remains blocked on
    stronger trim/edge correspondence, declared/inferred tolerance policy,
    non-isoparametric and vertex cases, stable shading endpoint provenance,
    and conservative fallback integration.

28. Edge tolerance metadata must not collapse unset, explicit zero, and a
    positive declaration into one sentinel test.  Prep now records the ray
    model tolerance, raw edge declaration, initial measured edge-to-both-trim-
    lift discrepancy, effective envelope, inferred/declaration source, and an
    explicit discrepancy-authorized bit.  The effective envelope is at least
    rti_tol.dist; valid edge and endpoint-vertex declarations can enlarge it.
    Explicit zero remains a declaration rather than being inferred.  An unset
    edge tolerance is initially inferred from the larger of model tolerance
    and sampled discrepancy.  The discrepancy must actually lie within the
    envelope before edge boxes, sectors, closure, or continuation qualify.
    A g=2T Cobb metadata matrix proves these outcomes: correct and double
    declarations authorize visibly; unset is inferred and authorizes; zero
    and half-discrepancy declarations remain under-authorized and do not even
    select an expanded edge span.  Rotation, translation, 0.01x, and 10,000x
    tests also preserve model tolerance, measured discrepancy, and
    authorization after scaling.  The present prep measurement is a fixed
    33-sample observation, deliberately reduced from an expensive 257-sample
    diagnostic.  It is not a conservative discrepancy proof, so this policy
    cannot by itself authorize general production closure.  The narrowly
    promoted one-event resolver additionally requires both incident trim
    lifts evaluated at the event's edge parameter to be within rti_tol.dist,
    plus unique topology/sector evidence and a complete root certificate.
    General edge-envelope policy still requires knot-aware adaptive bounds.
    Cached prep now explicitly rebuilds both edge and surface derived records
    using the active rti tolerance; the serialized cache remains version zero.

29. A retained coefficient box can seed a mathematical surface root without
    consulting the legacy SurfaceTree.  Trace mode maps a fixed deterministic
    set consisting of the box center, four corners, and four edge midpoints
    into its prepared rational Bezier span.  Each seed runs a fixed
    24-iteration, line-searched corrector bounded by that box.  Projecting
    Newton steps onto the box is necessary for roots on shared Bezier-span
    boundaries; the residual test still prevents a projected boundary point
    from becoming a false root.  Same-span results are deduplicated with a
    scale-aware numerical t tolerance.  Attempts, failures, duplicates,
    unique candidates, and capacity overflow are separately observable.  The
    ordinary convergence target scales with the local support diagonal, while
    a separate 32-epsilon world-coordinate floor admits only the cancellation
    inherent in evaluating translated geometry.  The retained-box t check
    uses the same scale-aware numerical allowance rather than a fixed model-
    space constant.

    On the analytic R=10 sphere, all 36 cases with chord at least T recover
    two distinct local root clusters and both analytic endpoints within
    8.56e-8 mm.  All 23 positive cases below T collapse to one contact-sized
    cluster; exact tangency also forms one cluster and no material segment.
    On the miss side, numerical contact candidates occur only within about
    2.84e-10 T, tighter than the existing 1e-6 T ambiguity gate.  The complete
    bowed Cobb matrix has no local root absent from the legacy candidate set.
    Fourteen of the 15 below-envelope leaks expose one in-domain local cluster.
    The remaining sub-tolerance boundary case has three: the valid entry, the
    bowed-face crossing, and the exact seam-boundary crossing.  A center-only
    solve had found only the first two; the alternate boundary seeds make the
    third basin observable.  All 15 still require the separately certified
    adjacent-support root to reproduce the implicit interval.  The machine
    report records every local face, span, UV, t, residual, normal dot, and
    iteration count plus per-ray attempt accounting.

    Identity, rigid rotation/translation, translated 0.01x scale, and
    translated 10,000x scale now gate the local corrector's failure count,
    normalized regular-root positions, residuals, and physical contact-cluster
    count alongside the edge-sector and closure classifier.  Raw numerical
    representatives of an exactly singular contact may differ while still
    forming the same one physical cluster.  This answers the similarity
    question for the present controlled corpus: the classifier and bounded
    local solve are invariant when all model-space tolerances scale with the
    geometry.
    Arbitrary nonuniform scaling is not a similarity and remains a separate
    transformed-surface case.  These roots are still trace-only: production
    traversal, trim/event classification, adaptive termination, and fallback
    integration are not yet changed.

30. Local mathematical roots now feed a first allocation-free clustering
    stage rather than relying on test-side vectors alone.  A fixed insertion
    sort orders at most 64 roots by t.  The initial compatibility rule joins
    only roots on the same face whose t values are within 0.1 of the active
    model distance tolerance; each cluster records its t and normal-dot range,
    root count, entering/leaving/tangent observations, face, and preliminary
    ENTER/LEAVE/CONTACT/UNRESOLVED class.  Capacity and the provisional
    clustering tolerance are explicit trace fields.  This 0.1T value is a
    shadow rollout policy, not the final ray-interval or minimum-valid-size
    tolerance.

    Every sphere case with analytic chord at least T now produces exactly two
    ordered clusters, ENTER followed by LEAVE.  Exact tangency produces one
    CONTACT cluster.  The classifier similarity matrix gates cluster counts;
    exactly singular raw representative counts may change with floating
    conditioning, but identity, rigid, 0.01x, and 10,000x cases all retain one
    physical contact cluster.  The bowed leak matrix produces cluster-count
    histogram 14/0/1 for one/two/three clusters.  Machine output includes each
    cluster and exact attempt = unique + duplicate + failure accounting.

    This is deliberately not the final topology resolver.  Cross-face roots
    require shared-edge/same-surface-seam compatibility, trimmed candidates
    require evidence-preserving classification, clusters need certified t
    intervals rather than point-distance grouping, and minimum-segment policy
    must remain separate.  No production event or segment is emitted from
    these preliminary clusters.

31. Surface-tree leaf discovery now has an allocation-free fixed-array
    overload in shadow mode.  It traverses the existing prepared child vectors
    in exactly the same depth-first order as the std::list overload, stores up
    to 128 leaf pointers in caller-owned memory, continues counting after
    capacity, and reports overflow explicitly.  Trace-mode shooting still
    invokes the allocating traversal first and uses its list for the live
    solver loop; the fixed traversal is compared by total count and pointer
    order, so the list remains an automatic correctness fallback while the
    shadow path is qualified.

    Every traced sphere grazing/contact/miss ray, tolerance-metadata ray,
    convex/concave/periodic sector ray, classifier similarity ray, and all 416
    bowed-seam rays now hard-gate count equality, order equality, and absence
    of overflow.  The ordinary sphere grazing sweep reaches 8 intersected
    leaves, the bowed matrix reaches 12, and the translated/rotated 0.01x
    similarity reaches 68.  The latter initially exhausted a 64-entry trial
    capacity, demonstrating that geometric similarity does not imply an
    invariant dense-tree leaf count even though the physical event classifier
    remains invariant.  The retained 128-entry trace workspace is 1 KiB of
    pointers on 64-bit systems and has explicit accounting; it is not a claim
    that 128 is sufficient for arbitrary corpus models.

    This shadow checkpoint removes allocation from leaf collection itself,
    not from the complete production shot.  Hit lists, miss/trim cleanup, and
    fallback/event integration still allocate.  Any live promotion must make
    its capacity decision before partial solver or cleanup side effects.

32. After the count/order shadow gate passed, production leaf discovery was
    promoted to the 128-pointer fixed array.  Capacity is resolved before any
    surface solver or hit-cleanup state is mutated.  A ray exceeding capacity
    immediately rebuilds the legacy std::list and runs the unchanged solver
    loop from that list; a bounded ray runs the solver in the already-proven
    fixed-array order.  Trace mode deliberately builds both representations,
    reports whether fallback was selected, and continues to gate their exact
    equivalence.  All current trace fixtures take the bounded path, and the
    focused cache, analytic, Cobb, and NURBS suite remains 13/13.  A separate
    Debug build instrumented with AddressSanitizer and UndefinedBehavior-
    Sanitizer also passes the complete rt_brep_ray corpus without a sanitizer
    diagnostic.  LeakSanitizer cannot run under the host ptrace wrapper and is
    explicitly not claimed by this gate.

    This promotion removes dynamic allocation from production surface-tree
    leaf collection for bounded rays.  It does not yet make BREP shooting
    allocation-free: brep_hit and cleanup structures remain std::list based,
    and an over-capacity leaf ray intentionally allocates in the safe legacy
    fallback.  Root/event conversion must preserve the same rule: decide to
    fall back before applying any partial cleanup or material-state change.

33. ThreadSanitizer qualification of parallel cache prep exposed four real
    publication races before it could validate SurfaceTree ownership.  The
    libbu small-object heap raced on its process-global lazy atexit flag, and
    bu_dir used a process-global scratch buffer even when callers supplied an
    output buffer.  Heap registration is now serialized only when a per-CPU
    size bin is initialized, and the legacy implicit bu_dir result buffer is
    thread-local.  The ordinary bu_dir and heap tests pass.

    openNURBS ONX_Model construction, archive reading/writing, and destruction
    rebuild render-setting defaults through non-reentrant localtime().  All
    ONX_Model lifetimes in the BREP primitive are now wrapped by the reserved
    datetime semaphore.  This serializes BREP archive import/export, not
    SurfaceTree construction or ray prep.  It can be narrowed or removed once
    the external OpenNURBS implementation uses localtime_r/localtime_s.

    More importantly, _rt_find_identical_solid published a soltab by setting
    st_id before primitive prep or cache deserialization completed.  A second
    tree-walk worker could therefore consume st_specific while
    rt_brep_prep_serialize was still assigning it.  soltab now has an explicit
    prep-complete publication flag protected by the same directory-hash
    semaphore used for identical-solid lookup.  Matching workers wait without
    holding the semaphore, and success or failure publishes all primitive
    pointers, bounds, and path state before reuse.  The hierarchy fixture also
    exposed the documented race caused by incrementing directory d_uses under
    different region and solid locks; both uses now share the directory-hash
    semaphore without nested lock ordering.

    The normal focused suite remains 13/13, the ASan/UBSan focused suite is
    12/12 with leak detection disabled for the host ptrace limitation, and all
    seven serial/parallel cache tests pass under ThreadSanitizer with
    halt_on_error=1 and no suppression file.  This is the first direct race
    qualification of the repaired per-BREP SurfaceTree and CurveTree ownership
    path; it does not prove unrelated librt operations outside this cache
    matrix race-free.

34. A topological edge parameter and an incident trim parameter do not in
    general have the same normalized fraction, even when both curves describe
    exactly the same locus.  The former discrepancy sampler, material-sector
    frame, and continuation seed all made that representation-dependent
    assumption.  They now map an edge point to each incident surface-lifted
    trim geometrically: an orientation-aware normalized value is only an
    initial hint, a fixed 33-sample scan finds a deterministic basin, and a
    bounded 24-step Gauss-Newton corrector with an eight-step line search
    minimizes 3D lift distance.  The composed trim/surface derivative is used
    only by correction; point-only scans avoid unnecessary derivative work.
    The routine allocates no ray-time memory and rejects invalid domains,
    topology, evaluation results, and nonfinite arithmetic.

    A copied bowed Cobb sphere converts both trims of the target edge to NURBS
    and applies opposite Möbius reparameterizations c=0.05 and c=20.  This
    changes the UV point evaluated at the trim-domain midpoint by at least
    0.452 while preserving the curve locus to 2.48e-16.  The fixture refreshes
    each OpenNURBS proxy domain after knot roundoff and requires the BREP to
    remain valid.  Against the identity case it preserves all inward,
    contact, and exterior sector states in both ray directions, measured
    discrepancy, unique closure and missing direction, continuation
    certificate, and published production interval.  The existing rotation,
    translation, 0.01x, and 10,000x similarity cases remain unchanged.

    This establishes regular two-trim correspondence invariance for the
    controlled fixture.  By itself it is not a conservative proof of the
    maximum seam discrepancy: the preparatory 33 edge samples and
    correspondence basin scan remain fixed.  Finding 35 adds a bounded shadow
    certificate for its supported regular subset; ambiguous or
    self-overlapping trims, exact-locus promotion, and vertex fans remain open
    and must retain explicit unsupported fallback.

35. A prep-time shadow pass now constructs a knot-aware conservative interval
    for the geometric gap between each supported shared 3D edge locus and
    each of its two surface-lifted trim loci.  The edge was already decomposed
    into exact-parameter positive-weight rational Bezier spans.  The new pass
    converts each trim to an exact NURBS form, splits it at every positive knot
    span, and uses the prepared exact-parameter positive-weight surface spans.
    It rejects unsupported order, weights, domains, topology, incomplete trim
    coverage, inconsistent orientation, nonfinite evaluation, and ambiguous
    endpoint correspondence instead of manufacturing a certificate.

    Each initial cell pairs one edge Bezier span with the incident trim
    interval found by the fixed geometric edge-to-lift projection.  Adjacent
    mapped intervals must be continuous and cover the complete trim domain in
    the direction required by m_bRev3d.  Child midpoints repeat the geometric
    projection inside the parent trim interval using a bounded nine-sample
    basin scan.  This preserves the nonlinear trim-reparameterization result
    without restoring the equal-normalized-speed assumption.

    Positive rational Bezier convexity supplies the actual bound.  The edge
    control points bound its Hausdorff deviation from its endpoint chord.  A
    knot-aware restricted trim control hull bounds the UV interval, and each
    overlapping prepared surface patch is restricted to that rectangle with
    fixed-array homogeneous de Casteljau operations.  The restricted positive
    surface control hull then bounds the lifted trim's deviation from its
    endpoint chord.  Exact segment Hausdorff distance plus or minus the two
    chord deviations gives a conservative cell interval; maxima combine cells
    and the two incident faces.  The UV rectangle is padded by a
    parameter-scale roundoff allowance before surface restriction.

    Cells subdivide until the aggregate interval is no wider than 0.1 model
    tolerance, with maximum depth 24.  Prep work is limited to 4,096 visited
    cells per solid.  Capacity exhaustion is recorded on both an interrupted
    edge and any later otherwise-eligible edge; no partial result is marked
    bounded.  Per-edge lower/upper limits, target width, cells, maximum depth,
    and exhaustion state are exposed in the trace and Cobb CSV.  Temporary
    vectors and Bezier objects are prep-time only; the pass adds no ray-time
    allocation.

    Scope is deliberately narrow at this checkpoint.  Exact-looking records
    at or below max(roundoff, 1e-6 T) remain unbounded because a zero fixed
    sample is not an exact-locus proof.  Records whose established sample is
    already beyond the model envelope skip the expensive upper certificate.
    The bound is shadow evidence only: discrepancy_authorized and production
    crack repair still use the pre-existing sampled policy.  Promotion needs
    complete exact-locus handling, unsupported/capacity fallback policy, and
    a tighter decision at the g=T boundary.

    The bowed Cobb target requires 2,046 cells at depth 9 and its worst final
    interval width is 0.0823 T.  It remains bounded under identity, opposite
    c=0.05/c=20 trim reparameterizations, rigid transform, 0.01x similarity,
    and 10,000x similarity with scaled model tolerances.  Every dense
    calibrated g<=T bowed case lies inside its reported interval; g>T cases
    are explicitly outside this shadow pass.  A separate three-seam matching
    fixture consumes exactly 4,096 cells, certifies two seams, and marks the
    third exhausted, proving that capacity neither truncates nor silently
    qualifies a record.

    Prep-cost experiments prevented an attractive local algorithm from
    becoming a new global cost.  Allocating OpenNURBS patch restriction in
    every cell made rt_brep_ray take 47.8 seconds; fixed homogeneous arrays
    reduced that to about 33 seconds; the 0.1 T target took 22.15 seconds.
    Skipping exact-looking records reduced it to 9.06 seconds.  Imported NIST
    geometry still exposed roughly 60 seconds of prep on many sub-tolerance
    seams; skipping already-rejected samples reduced that to 11.74 seconds,
    and the solid-wide budget reduced NIST03_MISS_01 to about 0.7 seconds.
    The current normal focused gate completes in about 10 seconds.  These are
    engineering observations on this host, not performance acceptance limits.

36. At this checkpoint edge-sector and crack-repair evidence first required a
    prep-time regular correspondence screen in addition to sampled discrepancy
    authorization.  Finding 64 supersedes the screen's authority with a
    conservative global proof while retaining its result as telemetry.
    Each incident trim is converted to NURBS, visited four times in every
    positive knot span (at most 256 samples), lifted through its face, and
    projected geometrically back to the 3D edge.  The fixed projection uses a
    33-point basin scan followed by at most 24 second-derivative corrections
    and an eight-step line search.  In trim-domain order, projected edge
    parameters must remain monotone with the direction implied by m_bRev3d,
    and the unit lifted tangent must retain positive oriented agreement with
    the local unit edge tangent.  Closed or numerically degenerate edges,
    failed NURBS/evaluation/projection, more than 256 samples, a reversal, or
    a stationary/opposed tangent leave correspondence_supported false.

    At that checkpoint the sampled result populated correspondence_supported
    and gated expanded edge spans, material sectors, the discrepancy pass, and
    crack repair without suppressing ordinary surface intersection or the
    legacy result.  It was explicitly only a bounded rejection screen: a
    localized higher-order reversal could occur between samples.  Finding 64
    implements the required exact/interval derivative proof and moves the old
    result to correspondence_screened.

    The ambiguity fixture starts with the known g=T bowed Cobb seam whose
    h=0.9T ray otherwise activates the certified production repair.  One trim
    is replaced by a piecewise-linear NURBS that follows the identical UV
    boundary forward, backward, and forward.  Its 3D locus and sampled gap
    remain authorized, but the middle span reverses both edge parameter and
    tangent.  In both ray directions the screen is false, no candidate edge
    span or sector is published, and the legacy one-event crack remains
    observable without closure, continuation, or a manufactured segment.

    A converted convex ARB8 supplies the initial vertex-fan fallback fixture.
    Body-diagonal rays in both directions and rays beginning exactly at the
    vertex observe all three incident edges as CONTACT while preserving the
    two-hit/one-interval solid result and never invoking closure.  A second
    line passes 0.25T inside the vertex fan; three incident edges qualify and
    two independently classify the ray as material-side, but neither ray
    direction attempts edge closure.  This gates non-repair at a multi-edge
    locus while a cyclic oriented vertex-fan resolver is still absent.

    The focused normal gate passes 13/13 (rt_brep_ray about 10.2 seconds and
    NIST03_MISS_01 about 0.82 seconds).  The focused ASan/UBSan gate passes
    12/12 with halt-on-error and leak detection disabled for the existing host
    limitation; rt_brep_ray completes in about 40.2 seconds without a
    diagnostic.

37. Fixed trace storage now records bidirectional root-set coverage between
    the prepared positive-weight Bezier-span corrector and the existing
    depth-eight SurfaceTree-seeded Newton solve.  Each side is sorted and
    deduplicated by face and ray parameter using the similarity-scaled 0.1 T
    cluster tolerance, then the ordered sets are matched one-to-one without
    allocation.  BREP_SAME_POINT_TOLERANCE is used only when no positive model
    tolerance exists.  One-to-one matching prevents a single prepared root
    from masking the loss of one of two nearby legacy roots.  Accounting
    remains explicit when either fixed trace array overflows; completeness
    claims are gated only when all involved workspaces remain within capacity.

    Across the analytic sphere grazing sweep, every unique legacy candidate
    root has a prepared-span match.  The prepared solver has one direct-only
    root on the closest representable outside line, h/T approximately
    -2.84217e-10.  Four span-boundary representatives collapse to one physical
    contact at t=2R, residual approximately 1.42e-13, zero normal dot, and no
    final material interval.  The gate accepts outside candidates only when
    they form exactly one CONTACT cluster at 2R, publish zero segments, and
    remain within the pre-existing 1e-6 T numerical ambiguity band.  It still
    fails any legacy root not covered by a prepared span.  The 416-ray bowed
    Cobb matrix has zero unmatched roots in either direction.

    The classifier similarity matrix now goes beyond an exact cyclic axis
    permutation.  It includes pure translation, the cyclic rotation, an
    arbitrary-axis unit-scale rotation, and independent arbitrary-axis
    rotations at 0.01x and 10,000x scale with translated geometry and all
    model-space tolerances scaled as lengths.  Normalized local root clusters,
    ENTER/LEAVE/CONTACT classes, edge-sector state, crack closure, continuation
    ordering, and the final production interval remain invariant in both ray
    directions.  Every legacy root in every transformed case has a prepared-
    span match.

    Acceleration work is deliberately not required to be invariant.  The
    oblique 0.01x case visits 104 of the 128 fixed dense-tree leaf slots,
    whereas the former exact-permutation matrix reached 68.  Candidate-hull
    and subdivision effort can change with the ray-plane basis even when the
    physical classifier does not.  This observation strengthens the need for
    explicit fixed-capacity fallback and is not evidence for increasing the
    fixed array.  Root coverage is still untrimmed candidate equivalence, not
    trim/event equivalence, and does not yet authorize reducing SurfaceTree
    depth or replacing the legacy event path.

    The focused normal gate passes 13/13 with rt_brep_ray at about 10.3
    seconds.  The focused ASan/UBSan gate passes 12/12 in about 40.7 seconds
    with halt-on-error and no sanitizer diagnostic.

38. CurveTree now provides a face-wide isTrimmed query over its existing
    prep-time m_sortedX leaf vector.  It reuses the same trim-candidate and
    final-classification implementation as BBNode::isTrimmed, creates no
    filtered list, and stops once the X-sorted remaining leaves begin beyond
    the query parameter.  The dense leaf-local classifier remains production;
    the face-wide form supplies SurfaceTree-independent classification for
    prepared-span roots.

    Every stored prepared root now records trim status and distance, initial
    CLEAN_HIT/CLEAN_MISS/NEAR_HIT/NEAR_MISS class, closest near-edge adjacent
    face, and oriented direction.  Missing face CurveTrees are explicit trace
    failures.  At every legacy Newton root, trace mode independently reruns the
    face-wide classifier at the exact same UV and requires identical trim
    status, near/clean hit class, and adjacency whenever the event is within
    BREP_EDGE_MISS_TOLERANCE.  The sphere grazing matrix checks 256 such roots
    and 512 full-face trim candidates; the bowed Cobb matrix checks 1,112 roots
    and 2,328 candidates.  Both have zero semantic mismatch and zero near-trim
    distance error.  Prepared roots make 324/648 and 620/1,356 query/candidate
    visits respectively, without allocation.

    Root equivalence is now event-cluster equivalence rather than arbitrary
    representative-root equality.  Same-face candidates within the scaled
    0.1 T t envelope form a fixed stack cluster.  The cluster prefers actual
    material-side candidates over trimmed candidates, selects the minimum
    absolute trim distance within that state, derives near/clean class from
    that evidence, and combines root normal signs using the established
    BREP_GRAZING_DOT_TOL into ENTERING, LEAVING, or CONTACT.  Only near events
    require adjacency equality; a closest trim on a clean root has no cleanup
    semantics.

    This clustering distinction is essential.  A first raw comparison made
    all 110 sphere matches appear different because irrelevant clean-root
    closest-adjacency choices differed, and nine tangent candidates crossed
    zero normal dot while remaining far inside the grazing band.  The oblique
    0.01x exact-contact fixture also emits four legacy candidates (one clean
    and three near) versus four prepared near candidates.  Both contain the
    same exact near-edge contact, and both correctly collapse to one CONTACT;
    selecting the numerically earliest raw root would instead compare the
    legacy clean outlier.  The fixed physical event cluster avoids that error
    without weakening regular two-root separation.

    The final analytic sphere sweep has 110 matched event clusters and zero
    trim/class/direction/adjacency mismatch.  Its maximum matched t, UV, and
    normal-dot differences are approximately 1.96e-6, 3.46e-8, and 1.96e-7;
    no matched event is near a trim.  The 416-ray bowed Cobb matrix has 556
    matched clusters, zero semantic mismatch, and maximum corresponding
    differences about 9.84e-8, 6.92e-9, zero, and 9.84e-9.  All expanded
    similarity cases retain zero legacy-root and event mismatch.

    This establishes trim-aware initial-event equivalence on the controlled
    corpus, but it is not final cleanup equivalence.  Prepared clusters have
    not yet been materialized as fixed brep_hit events and passed through the
    duplicate/grazing/direction cleanup pipeline.  The face-wide trim query is
    allocation-free but scans a prefix of the X-sorted trim leaves; complex
    STEP faces may require a stronger prepared index after measurement.  Tree
    depth remains eight.  The focused normal gate passes 13/13 with rt_brep_ray
    at 10.23 seconds.  ASan/UBSan passes 12/12 in 41.10 seconds with no
    diagnostic.

39. Prepared roots are now materialized in two allocation-free, fixed-stack
    shadow streams before any SurfaceTree density change.  The first retains
    every non-CLEAN_MISS prepared root as a brep_hit candidate.  It rebuilds
    the robust face normal with surface_EvNormal, preserves trim/near/adjacent-
    face/direction state, sorts in the existing fixed workspace, and executes
    the same near-miss, near-hit, grazing, duplicate, and direction cleanup
    implementation as production.  A parallel workspace made from the legacy
    Newton roots supplies per-stage physical signatures as well as raw count
    comparisons.  These paths are trace-only and are not called when the
    production trace pointer is null.

    Raw candidate multiplicity is intentionally not treated as physical
    equivalence.  The prepared solver sees roots from neighboring Bezier
    spans that the dense leaf solver may represent differently, especially at
    a tangent.  The sphere matrix has 324 prepared candidates and 25 raw
    stage-count differences.  Its only semantic difference at each of the
    five stages is the already classified prepared-only, zero-material contact
    on the nearest representable miss-side line.  The bowed Cobb matrix has
    620 candidates and 955 raw count differences.  Physical stage signatures
    differ 0/16/19/19/19 after near-miss, near-hit, grazing, duplicate, and
    direction cleanup respectively.  Exact near-miss-stage equivalence is a
    hard gate because that is where seam evidence is resolved; later
    differences are retained as observability and must be judged by their
    final material partitions rather than candidate count.

    The second stream materializes the already qualified physical event
    clusters.  CLEAN_MISS and CONTACT clusters are omitted because neither is
    a finite solid boundary.  The remaining events pass through fixed cleanup
    and the same certified single-event crack repair.  Up to eight final solid
    intervals are stored directly in the trace for comparison to both legacy
    output and independent implicit oracles.  Missing normals, unsupported
    direction, hit or segment capacity, and repair failures remain explicit;
    no capacity is silently increased.

    Across the analytic sphere sweep, 111 prepared groups contain 35 contacts
    and produce 76 material events, 38 aggregate segments, and zero legacy
    partition differences.  Across the 416-ray bowed Cobb family, 556 groups
    contain 38 contacts and produce 518 material events, 16 certified repairs,
    and 226 aggregate segments.  There is exactly one partition difference
    from the depth-eight solver and it is an independently demonstrated
    improvement, not bug-for-bug equivalence.  For the reverse inward-bowed
    g/T=0.1, h/T=0.1 case, legacy repairs
    [19.9997585419, 20.0316227402], while prepared events produce
    [19.9683772359, 20.0316227918].  The implicit sphere oracle is
    [19.9683772629, 20.0316227371]: prepared maximum endpoint error is about
    5.47e-8, while legacy error is about 3.14e-2.  The corpus classifies every
    final prepared/legacy difference as oracle improvement, regression, or
    ambiguous; it hard-fails regressions and ambiguous changes.  The present
    totals are 1/0/0.

    This is final-partition shadow qualification, not production promotion.
    Later raw cleanup stages still expose tangent/duplicate representation
    differences, adaptive ordinary-span subdivision still retains boxes down
    to depth 24, and the production SurfaceTree remains depth eight.  The
    focused normal gate passes 13/13 with rt_brep_ray at 10.29 seconds.
    ASan/UBSan passes 12/12 in 40.55 seconds with no diagnostic.
    After the final stage-label refactor, direct normal and ASan/UBSan
    rt_brep_ray reruns pass in 9.24 and 36.80 seconds respectively.

40. A provisional interval Krawczyk test now supplies the first adaptive
    early-termination rule for ordinary prepared surface spans.  Given a
    bounded corrector root x in local parameter box X, it computes

      K(X) = x - C F(x) + (I - C J(X)) (X - x),

    where F is the pair of homogeneous ray-plane Bernstein polynomials, J(X)
    is bounded by the convex hulls of their derivative Bernstein
    coefficients, and C is the inverse of the midpoint interval Jacobian.
    Only strict containment of K(X) in the interior of X qualifies.  A failed
    corrector, singular preconditioner, failed inclusion, tangent Jacobian, or
    root on a subdivision/patch boundary says nothing and continues the
    existing subdivision path to depth 24.  Small UV, model-space, or ray-t
    width and numerical convergence alone never terminate a box.

    The implementation uses fixed arrays and performs no ray-time allocation.
    Coefficient uncertainty is propagated into function and derivative
    intervals; interval endpoints receive deliberately conservative error
    enlargement and nextafter expansion.  Corrector attempts/convergences,
    successful Krawczyk boxes and their depths, isolated-box ray-t widths,
    subdivision work, overflow, and high-water marks are observable.  The
    continuation certificate deliberately disables this new rule, preserving
    its prior depth-24 behavior.

    Three deterministic off-center, oblique sphere lines are shot in both
    directions.  All six have one implicit, legacy BREP, and prepared BREP
    partition with endpoints within model tolerance.  They produce 12
    physical events and exactly 12 Krawczyk-qualified boxes at depths 4
    through 9, with zero candidate-stage, semantic-stage, cleaned-hit,
    root/event, or final-partition differences.  Adaptive work is 294 visited
    boxes total and at most 64 per ray, versus a directly measured run with
    this one termination disabled of 1,038 total and 192 per ray at depth 24.
    That is a 71.7 percent reduction in subdivision visits for this regular-
    root fixture.  Correctors converge in 28 of 44 adaptive attempts.

    This does not weaken grazing qualification.  The analytic grazing sphere
    and 416-ray bowed Cobb matrices produce zero Krawczyk-qualified boxes;
    tangent, nearly tangent, trim-boundary, and patch-boundary candidates all
    remain on the depth-24 path.  Their root/event/partition results are
    unchanged, including the one independently proved prepared-partition
    improvement and zero regressions or ambiguous changes.  The production
    SurfaceTree still uses depth eight, and no production hit publication or
    tree density changed.

    R. E. Moore's 1977 existence test and Breiding, Rose, and Timme's modern
    interval certification treatment justify the strict Krawczyk inclusion
    rule and are recorded with primary-source links in the plan.  The current
    arithmetic is intentionally classified as shadow evidence, not a formal
    production certificate: an independent numerical audit must prove that
    every generated coefficient and function/Jacobian interval encloses the
    exact polynomial quantity on all supported orders, scales, and
    translations before production promotion.

    The final focused normal gate passes 13/13 with rt_brep_ray at 9.30
    seconds.  The focused ASan/UBSan gate passes 12/12 in 33.20 seconds;
    rt_brep_ray takes 33.15 seconds in that parallel gate.  A final direct
    ASan/UBSan rerun after the grazing no-termination hard gates passes in
    25.16 seconds with no sanitizer diagnostic.

41. Adaptive regular-root qualification now has its own similarity matrix,
    separate from the seam classifier's existing invariance corpus.  Each
    fixture constructs an exact `rt_ell_internal` sphere whose center and
    three radius vectors receive one common similarity transform, converts
    that implicit to BREP through the production conversion callback, and
    transforms the same off-center oblique lines and both ray directions.
    Model distance and squared-distance tolerances scale with the geometry.

    The matrix contains identity, pure translation, arbitrary-axis rotation
    plus translation, rotated/translated 0.01x scale, and rotated/translated
    10,000x scale.  Three lines in both directions give 30 rays and 60
    physical roots.  Every ray has one analytic, implicit, legacy-BREP, and
    prepared-BREP interval; normalization by the uniform scale recovers the
    same analytic sphere chord.  Every prepared result contains exactly two
    non-contact event groups, matches both legacy root/event classes, and
    produces one final interval without workspace, trace, or partition
    mismatch.

    All 60 roots pass strict-interior Krawczyk qualification before fixed
    depth, at depths 4 through 9.  Subdivision work deliberately is not
    required to be invariant: rays visit 27 through 67 boxes, 1,602 in total.
    This proves the relevant distinction directly.  Physical classification
    and normalized partitions are similarity invariants; ray-plane basis
    selection and floating-point interval conditioning may change traversal
    effort.  Maximum normalized analytic endpoint errors are approximately
    4.62e-14 for the implicit, 2.87e-7 for the legacy BREP path, and 2.23e-8
    for the prepared path, all below the scaled model-tolerance gate.

    Nonuniform scaling is still not claimed invariant.  It changes a sphere
    into an ellipsoid and changes normal/metric conditioning, so it belongs in
    a separate affine fixture.  Similarity success also does not replace the
    pending interval-enclosure audit or authorize production tree reduction.

    The focused normal gate passes 13/13 with rt_brep_ray at 10.14 seconds.
    The focused ASan/UBSan gate passes 12/12 in 36.26 seconds, with
    rt_brep_ray at 36.22 seconds and no sanitizer diagnostic.

42. The function/Jacobian arithmetic layer of the provisional Krawczyk test
    now has an independent enclosure audit.  Scalar double evaluation of
    F(x) plus a post hoc magnitude estimate has been removed.  Each uncertain
    Bernstein coefficient begins as an outward interval, and tensor de
    Casteljau evaluation uses outward interval scaling and addition at every
    level.  Derivative Bernstein coefficients are likewise formed as
    intervals before their convex hulls are taken.  Krawczyk matrix products
    use the same outward interval multiplication.

    Primitive interval operations now expand each computed endpoint by one
    `nextafter` step instead of adding a fixed `128 epsilon * max(1, |x|)`
    margin.  The old absolute floor was conservative but destroyed binary
    scale covariance for small coefficients.  One outward representable step
    around each finite IEEE operation encloses its exact result regardless of
    whether the active rounding mode rounded that operation up or down.  All
    nonfinite results remain rejection paths.

    A private librt test hook publishes only the computed function, Jacobian,
    and product intervals.  The independent C++ oracle represents generated
    coefficients as exact signed dyadics, evaluates their tensor midpoint by
    exact integer addition and exponent adjustment, and computes derivative
    control coefficients exactly.  It then verifies that production
    intervals contain the nominal value plus the complete declared
    coefficient uncertainty.  This avoids relying on the implementation's
    own scalar evaluator as its oracle.

    Every tensor order pair from 2x2 through the supported 16x16 maximum is
    covered at binary exponents -400, -40, 0, 40, and 400.  Mixed-sign nets
    force cancellation.  The permanent gate checks 1,125 nets, 2,250 function
    intervals, 324,000 derivative coefficients, and 45 signed interval
    products.  Maximum function interval width is 1.00001197 times the exact
    propagated coefficient-uncertainty width, well inside the 1.05 guard.

    This completes only one proof layer.  The initial homogeneous ray-plane
    coefficient construction, arbitrary sub-box de Casteljau restriction
    error, rational weight/ray-t intervals, and outside-span
    reparameterization amplification still require independent enclosure
    audits.  Until those are complete, a successful Krawczyk result remains
    shadow evidence and cannot change production hit publication or tree
    density.

    All existing physical results remain unchanged: the ordinary sphere and
    similarity matrices qualify at depths 4 through 9, both grazing matrices
    qualify zero boxes early, and prepared partitions retain one improvement
    with zero regressions or ambiguous changes.  The focused normal gate
    passes 13/13 with rt_brep_ray at 10.36 seconds.  The focused ASan/UBSan
    gate passes 12/12 in 37.29 seconds, with rt_brep_ray at 37.25 seconds and
    no sanitizer diagnostic.

43. Initial homogeneous coefficient construction, arbitrary in-span
    restriction, and rational ray-t bounding now use the same operation-level
    outward intervals as the function/Jacobian layer.  Each stored binary64
    control component, weight, ray origin, direction, and ray-plane component
    begins as a point interval.  The implementation encloses `cv - origin*w`,
    both plane dot products, the ray-direction squared norm, and the ray-t
    numerator quotient at every arithmetic operation.  The existing scalar
    coefficients remain deterministic working centers, but their one common
    error per net is derived from the full interval hull rather than a
    coefficient-magnitude heuristic.  Stored positive weights are exact
    inputs and therefore begin with zero error.

    Every scalar tensor restriction now has a parallel interval de Casteljau
    restriction.  It starts from each complete center-plus-error coefficient
    interval, encloses the local minimum/maximum ratio used by the second
    split, and derives one common output error only after all restricted
    coefficient intervals exist.  This replaces the former
    `(1 + 8*depth)` error growth and `128*epsilon*max(1,magnitude)` addition.
    The same bounded restriction is applied independently to the homogeneous
    ray numerator and weight nets.  A weight interval reaching zero rejects
    the ray-t certificate; otherwise outward interval division supplies each
    rational coefficient bound and their hull supplies the box's t range.

    The exact-dyadic oracle now restricts tensor nets independently with exact
    half splits at three multi-split sub-box locations.  It covers both mixed-
    sign coefficient patterns, all order pairs from 2x2 through 16x16, five
    binary scales from exponent -400 through 400, and positive input
    uncertainty.  All 546,750 restricted coefficients enclose the exact
    nominal restricted value plus the full original uncertainty.  Maximum
    output width is 1.00003862 times that uncertainty, below the 1.05 guard.
    A separate gate checks 162 exact initial plane/ray coefficients across
    coordinate, direction, and plane scales, and 36 positive-denominator
    interval quotients.  Together with Finding 42, the audit now performs
    873,243 permanent enclosure checks before the ray corpus.

    Tighter proven hulls reject three legacy-only candidate clusters on
    representable miss-side sphere lines which the former heuristics retained.
    All three are analytically outside the sphere, produce zero implicit and
    production segments, lie within 0.1 T in ray parameter of tangency, and
    have grazing normal dot products.  The corpus reports unmatched legacy
    clusters as raw/accepted/invalid and permits only this narrow oracle-
    proven class; any invalid unmatched cluster is a hard failure.  Prepared
    sphere candidates fall from 324 to 296 and physical clusters from 111 to
    107 without changing a final partition.  This is a rigorous exclusion of
    numerical miss-side ambiguity, not crack closure or relaxed hit handling.

    The physical similarity contract remains unchanged.  Identity,
    translation, arbitrary rotation, 0.01x, and 10,000x cases still preserve
    normalized analytic/implicit/legacy/prepared partitions, all 60 ordinary
    roots qualify at depths 4 through 9, and the 30 rays still visit 1,602
    boxes.  The grazing matrices continue to qualify zero boxes early and the
    fixed-depth sphere grazing traversal remains 12,384 boxes; the 71.7
    percent measured work reduction still applies only to regular transverse
    roots.

    This completes the interval input proof for ordinary in-span Krawczyk
    boxes.  Outside-span continuation reparameterization remains a distinct
    open audit because extrapolating Bernstein transforms are not convex
    restrictions.  No production SurfaceTree density or published hit path
    changes in this checkpoint.  The focused normal gate passes 13/13 in
    13.88 seconds, with rt_brep_ray at 12.58 seconds.  The focused ASan/UBSan
    gate passes 12/12 in 54.91 seconds, with rt_brep_ray at 54.87 seconds and
    no sanitizer diagnostic.

44. Outside-span Bernstein reparameterization now has operation-level
    enclosures and an independent exact audit.  The former implementation
    computed a scalar infinity norm from transformed basis vectors, multiplied
    inherited errors by the tensor norm, and added a magnitude-based epsilon
    estimate for transformation roundoff.  Although the norm correctly
    described exact input-error amplification, the scalar transformation
    itself had no independently proven rounding enclosure.

    The scalar transformed net remains the deterministic working center.  For
    its bound, each one-dimensional Bernstein transformation matrix is now
    constructed with outward interval de Casteljau operations, including the
    interval quotient used by both algebraic split branches.  The u/v tensor
    matrix is then applied directly to every complete source coefficient
    interval with outward products and sums.  The final common error is
    derived from all output intervals, and every transformed weight interval
    must remain strictly positive before a continuation certificate proceeds.

    Direct tensor application matters for quality as well as proof.  A trial
    that propagated uncertain controls through two sequential interval split
    nets retained false correlations: even the linear [2,3] extrapolation
    enlarged the exact 5E input uncertainty to 7E, and the factor could
    compound with degree.  Building interval transformation matrices from
    exact basis vectors and applying the tensor operator once removes that
    dependency blow-up while still enclosing matrix-construction roundoff.

    The independent oracle performs exact-dyadic de Casteljau transforms and
    obtains exact input-uncertainty bounds from absolute u/v operator row
    sums.  Four large cases cover extension below zero, above one, the
    alternate minimum-equals-one split branch, an interval wholly above one
    with a negative intermediate denominator, and identity in one direction.
    They exercise both mixed-sign nets, all 2x2 through 16x16 order pairs, and
    exponents -400, -40, 0, 40, and 400.  A fifth seam-sized
    [-1/1024,1] x [0,1025/1024] case covers exact combined degrees through six;
    larger exact dyadic denominators exceed the fixed 64-bit oracle workspace,
    while the large integer-parameter cases continue to cover every supported
    order.

    All 730,550 transformed coefficient checks contain the exact nominal
    value plus its complete operator-amplified input uncertainty.  Maximum
    common-error width is 1.01298951 times the exact maximum uncertainty,
    below the 1.05 efficiency guard.  Combined with Findings 42 and 43, the
    permanent arithmetic audit now contains 1,603,793 enclosure checks.

    All 15 authorized bowed-Cobb continuation repairs remain certified and
    match the implicit sphere oracle.  Certificate maximum work remains 93
    boxes with stack high-water 20; maximum t-cluster width changes only from
    approximately 3.46262659e-5 to 3.46262648e-5 mm.  Identity, rigid,
    0.01x, and 10,000x classification and published partitions are unchanged.
    Ordinary similarity roots still qualify at depths 4 through 9 and grazing
    roots still qualify zero boxes early.  No SurfaceTree density changes in
    this checkpoint.  The focused normal gate passes 13/13 in 25.64 seconds,
    with rt_brep_ray at 24.37 seconds.  The focused ASan/UBSan gate passes
    12/12 in 93.19 seconds, with rt_brep_ray at 93.15 seconds and no sanitizer
    diagnostic.

45. Conservative Bernstein clipping now reduces ordinary shadow isolation
    work without changing published hits or SurfaceTree density.  For each
    parameter direction, each ray-plane equation is reduced to lower and
    upper univariate coefficient envelopes over the other parameter.  A root
    must lie where the lower Bernstein curve can be nonpositive and the upper
    curve can be nonnegative.  Fixed control-polygon halfspace bounds include
    every eligible vertex and every pairwise zero crossing using outward
    interval arithmetic.  Scanning non-hull chords may enlarge the parameter
    range but cannot remove a valid root.

    The ranges from both equations are intersected in U and V.  Empty or
    inconclusive results do not exclude a box in this checkpoint.  A proposed
    contraction is re-restricted from the complete prepared coefficient net;
    restriction failure or a contradictory hull result restores the original
    box.  No clipping path allocates.  Trace accounting reports attempts,
    accepted U/V contractions, inconclusive results, restriction failures,
    and maximum removed width.

    Two numerical guards were required.  Each clip retains at least half of
    each parameter width and ignores removal below 1/16.  A contraction is
    charged one or two subdivision levels according to retained parameter
    area and capped at depth 24.  Without depth charging, repeated clipping
    narrowed boxes far beyond the old depth-24 resolution, outran corrector
    accuracy, and exposed non-progressing midpoint subdivision.  If neither
    midpoint is representably interior, the box now terminates conservatively
    rather than generating duplicate children.

    Continuation certificates intentionally retain the unclipped isolator.
    Trial clipping covered the intended continuation root but also retained
    extra conservative terminal boxes, violating the certificate's stronger
    requirement that its bounded region contain only that root component.
    This is an explicit promotion boundary rather than a loosened seam check.

    A private arithmetic gate covers 2,025 affine bivariate systems with known
    roots, positive coefficient uncertainty, every order pair from 2x2 through
    16x16, and binary scales 2^-200, 1, and 2^200.  Every clipped range contains
    the known root and contracts.  The complete 1,603,793 prior arithmetic
    gates and the new clipping gate pass before the geometry corpus.

    On the 30-ray similarity matrix, all 60 ordinary roots still qualify at
    depths 4 through 11 and normalized analytic/implicit/legacy/prepared
    partitions are unchanged.  Subdivision visits fall from the recorded
    no-clipping 1,602 boxes to 964 (39.8 percent).  The complete signed sphere
    grazing sequence, exact tangent, mirrored miss-side sequence, and bowed
    Cobb seam matrix pass with zero clipping restriction failures; grazing
    boxes receive no Krawczyk early termination.  The full direct ray corpus
    passes.  The focused normal gate passes 13/13 in 25.30 seconds, with
    rt_brep_ray at 24.07 seconds.  The focused ASan/UBSan gate passes 12/12 in
    91.04 seconds, with rt_brep_ray at 91.00 seconds and no sanitizer
    diagnostic.  Production ordinary hits still come only from the
    depth-eight SurfaceTree path.

46. Completely certified ordinary prepared partitions now have a production
    publication path.  It is deliberately fail-closed: the BREP must be a
    solid and not a plate; every face and span must be supported and fully
    accounted; every retained box must be stored, Krawczyk-certified, and
    covered by a corrected root from the same face/span; and no surface,
    clipping, root, trim, cluster, or hit workspace may fail or overflow.
    Reconstructed events must be unambiguous ENTERING or LEAVING events and
    must clean to a finite, alternating, even partition with no sub-tolerance
    segment.  Clean trimmed misses are allowed; contacts, tangencies, odd
    partitions, and all incomplete evidence fall back.

    Thirteen observable outcomes distinguish selection from non-solid, plate,
    unsupported-surface, surface-workspace/restriction, surface-box-capacity,
    uncertified-root, local-workspace/trim, root-coverage, event-class,
    hit-construction, hit-workspace, and final-partition fallback.  Production
    returns a qualified fixed-workspace partition before SurfaceTree traversal.
    Otherwise it executes the existing dense-tree solver unchanged.  Plate,
    non-solid, and unsupported-face checks occur before prepared
    isolation, so those object modes do not pay for a solve merely to report
    their fallback.  Trace mode intentionally runs the legacy solver even
    after qualification so all prior event and partition comparisons remain
    live, then returns the prepared answer.  A private test entry forces legacy
    dispatch and prevents the promoted path from serving as its own oracle.

    Six oblique interior sphere rays qualify and match the analytic implicit,
    promoted production, forced legacy, and reconstructed prepared paths to
    the model tolerance.  The 30-ray similarity gate applies identity,
    translation, arbitrary-axis rotation, 0.01x scale, and 10,000x scale.  All
    60 roots qualify, all 30 partitions are selected, and the maximum
    normalized errors are 4.62e-14 implicit, 1.08e-8 production, 2.87e-7
    forced legacy, and 1.08e-8 reconstructed prepared.  Thus classification
    and normalized hit locations are invariant for tested similarities when
    model-space tolerances scale as lengths.  Finding 47 supplies the separate
    nonuniform affine ellipsoid qualification.

    The signed grazing matrix and exact tangent do not qualify: they report
    the explicit uncertified-root fallback and retain the legacy result.  In
    accumulated trace observations, the sphere interior selects 6/6 attempts,
    the broader sphere matrix selects 58/119 with 61 uncertified fallbacks,
    and the bowed Cobb matrix selects 102/416 with 314 uncertified fallbacks.
    The complete Crofton, CSG-leaf, similarity, classifier, metadata, and Cobb
    corpus passes without a partition delta.

    This is a correctness checkpoint, not yet a performance win.  The normal
    focused gate passes 13/13 with rt_brep_ray at 68.99 seconds; the previous
    shadow-only checkpoint took 24.07 seconds.  Some increase is independent
    test duplication, but production now also attempts prepared isolation on
    every BREP ray and grazing rays pay for that attempt before fallback.  The
    ASan/UBSan gate passes 12/12 in 361.99 seconds with rt_brep_ray at 361.95
    seconds and no diagnostic.  Do not lower SurfaceTree density or claim a
    throughput improvement until selection/fallback profiling supplies a
    lower-cost dispatch strategy or an adequate replacement for dense-tree
    fallback.

47. Ordinary prepared-root publication is now qualified under nonuniform
    affine sphere-to-ellipsoid transforms, with the high-condition fallback
    boundary retained as a permanent ratchet.  Each fixture applies
    A = R diag(sx, sy, sz) plus translation to a known sphere and its ray.  A
    transformed ray is normalized for shooting, and its analytic entry/exit
    parameters are the original chord parameters multiplied by |A d|.  This
    gives a direct oracle rather than a second numerical ellipsoid solve.

    The implicit ELL is formed from the three transformed radius vectors and
    independently converted to BREP.  Both ray directions compare the exact
    chord, implicit ELL, promoted production BREP, forced legacy BREP, and the
    reconstructed prepared physical partition.  Model distance tolerance is
    scaled by the minimum principal scale, which is the conservative scalar
    choice for the thinnest ellipsoid direction; endpoint errors are normalized
    by |A d|.  The six transforms include oblate and triaxial forms, arbitrary
    rotation and translation, principal scales from 0.001 through 2,500, and
    singular-value condition numbers from 1.67 through 1,000.

    All 36 production and forced-legacy rays produce the same one-segment
    implicit-oracle partition.  Twenty-eight rays publish the prepared result.
    At condition 100, one of three directions reaches the conservative depth
    limit in both ray directions and reports uncertified fallback.  At
    condition 1,000, two directions exceed the 64-box prepared trace capacity
    and one remains uncertified, again in both directions.  The latter middle
    direction also intersects 197 dense SurfaceTree leaves: trace mode records
    the 128-leaf fixed-workspace overflow and allocating-list fallback, while
    production remains identical to the forced legacy and implicit answers.
    Thus neither prepared nor legacy fixed capacity silently truncates an
    extreme ellipsoid.

    Across the 36 rays, all 72 intended roots receive a Krawczyk certificate
    somewhere in the retained subdivision, at certificate depths 5 through
    19.  Total subdivision visits are 16,860, with 15 minimum and 3,096 maximum
    per ray.  Maximum normalized endpoint errors are 2.20e-12 implicit,
    5.94e-8 production, 9.61e-8 forced legacy, and 5.94e-8 selected prepared.
    The summary hard-gates 28 selections, four surface-box fallbacks, and four
    uncertified fallbacks.  A new `--affine-only` mode runs this matrix directly
    for fast conditioning work.

    The focused affine gate passes in both optimized and ASan/UBSan builds with
    no sanitizer diagnostic.  The complete normal gate passes 13/13 in 69.56
    seconds, with rt_brep_ray at 69.52 seconds.  A full ASan/UBSan 12/12 run
    before extending the condition sweep passed in 359.82 seconds; the final
    condition-1,000 additions are covered by the focused sanitizer run.  This
    finding does not claim affine grazing or seam invariance: those need a
    separately mapped tangent-limit trend with anisotropic tolerance analysis.

48. The mapped affine grazing trend is now an executable ratchet, and it
    proves that the current classifiers are not yet fully affine invariant at
    the tangent limit.  The gate reuses the analytic sphere line and maps both
    its closest point and direction through A = R diag(sx, sy, sz) plus
    translation.  It shoots the triaxial, condition-100, and condition-1,000
    ellipsoids in both directions at signed base-sphere clearances
    h/T = 100, 1, 0.01, 1e-4, 1e-6, 1e-8, 0, and the six mirrored negative
    values.  This is 78 directed rays.  The exact base chord is multiplied by
    |A d| and divided by the model tolerance scaled by the smallest principal
    scale; endpoint errors are normalized back by |A d|.  Thus the oracle
    measures physical material at the thinnest modeled scale rather than a
    fixed angle or an untransformed parameter distance.

    The first run found a classification defect after successful root solve.
    At increasingly anisotropic grazes the legacy solver had two clean,
    correctly ordered roots, but both normal dot products fell below the fixed
    BREP_GRAZING_DOT_TOL.  Cleanup deleted the roots even when their physical
    chord was tens, hundreds, or thousands of model tolerances long.  A fixed
    angular cutoff is not affine invariant: an affine distortion can make the
    endpoint normals arbitrarily close to perpendicular without eliminating
    the material between the endpoints.

    Grazing cleanup now distinguishes an isolated contact from a resolved
    solid interval.  A nearly perpendicular hit is retained when it belongs
    to an adjacent, finite, untrimmed-or-authorized, ordered ENTERING/LEAVING
    pair whose ray-parameter separation is at least
    rti_tol.dist/|ray direction|, with only a scale-aware floating-point slack.
    Invalid trimmed and out-of-bounds candidates are still removed.  The same
    rule is used by the allocation-free cleanup, the legacy list cleanup, the
    prepared-event shadow, and prepared production qualification, so fixed and
    allocating paths remain equivalence-gated.  Exact contacts and
    sub-tolerance slivers may still collapse.  This change restores 16 of the
    initially missing directed affine grazing partitions.

    The resulting trend has no reversal mismatch and no hit/miss restart as h
    approaches zero.  All six transformed rays hit at h/T = 100, 1, 0.01, and
    1e-4.  Four of six hit at 1e-6 and zero hit at 1e-8.  The corresponding
    analytic chord ranges, in transformed model tolerances, are
    4.24e4--3.05e6, 4.25e3--3.05e5, 425--3.05e4, 42.5--3.05e3,
    4.25--305, and 0.425--30.5.  The triaxial condition-13.3 case is resolved
    through h/T = 1e-6 and its next miss has only a 0.425-T chord.  The
    condition-100 case still misses both directions at h/T = 1e-8 despite a
    3.08-T chord.  The condition-1,000 case still misses both directions at
    h/T = 1e-6 and 1e-8 despite 305-T and 30.5-T chords.  Those six resolved
    misses occur before hit construction: the old solver stores no roots and
    the prepared path reaches hundreds of uncertified/overflowed boxes without
    a publishable local root.  They are a solver/isolation problem, distinct
    from the corrected angular-cleanup problem.

    The gate therefore enforces monotone capability floors of h/T = 1e-6,
    1e-6, and 1e-4 for conditions 13.3, 100, and 1,000, respectively, while
    allowing closer cases and the aggregate six-resolved-miss count only to
    improve.  Production and forced legacy topology must match, every retained
    endpoint must satisfy the analytic tolerance, both ray directions must
    agree, negative clearances must miss, and prepared overflow/fallback must
    remain explicit.  This records a bounded known gap without blessing the
    gap as the desired final answer.

    Exact transformed tangency also revealed that the implicit ELL is not a
    reliable binary oracle exactly on the discriminant: three of six directed
    shots return tiny, direction-dependent contact slivers from affine
    floating-point cancellation, while the analytic oracle and BREP both
    classify all six as no material.  Positive and negative clearances still
    use the implicit result as an independent comparison, but exact contact is
    judged analytically.  This is relevant to later Cauchy-Crofton work:
    tangencies have measure zero in the estimator, but directed tangent tests
    must not silently treat an implicit numerical artifact as ground truth.

    The optimized `--affine-only` gate, the complete `rt_brep_ray` corpus, and
    the focused ASan/UBSan `--affine-only` gate pass.  The full normal corpus
    ends with `BREP ray correctness corpus: PASS`; the sanitizer run uses
    halt-on-error and reports no diagnostic.  The focused implicit-to-BREP and
    NURBS regression gate passes 4/4.  The test output deliberately reports
    `resolved-gaps=6` rather than claiming complete affine grazing invariance.

49. Structured prepared-corrector outcomes now identify why the six affine
    grazing gaps remain.  Every bounded correction attempt records invalid
    input, evaluation failure, nonfinite arithmetic, degenerate normal,
    singular Jacobian, no improvement, iteration limit, or convergence.  A
    finite nonconverged local attempt also records its final projected
    residual divided by the same scale-aware acceptance bound used by the
    solver.  Trace consistency requires exactly one outcome per attempt,
    convergence counters to agree, and every recorded failed ratio to exceed
    one.  This instrumentation does not change a hit decision.

    With the normal 64-box trace capacity, all 576 local seeds for each missed
    affine case end in no improvement; none reaches the 24-iteration limit.
    The surface-stage attempts show the same pattern, plus a small number of
    singular Jacobians.  Controlled diagnostic builds established the
    following boundaries and were then restored to production constants:

      * Retaining 1,024 boxes instead of 64 lets the condition-100 case reach
        one of its two roots.  The other local basin stops with a residual
        about 3.2 times its acceptance bound.  The condition-1,000 cases retain
        all 546 terminal boxes but find no root; their closest failed residuals
        remain about 19 through 37 times the bound.

      * Raising subdivision depth from 24 to 32 and 40 does not recover a
        partition.  The extreme case grows from roughly 2,900 visits to 11,900
        and then 49,400 visits.  More isotropic subdivision is therefore an
        expensive repetition of the same ill-conditioned solve.

      * Increasing numerical acceptance from 32 to 1,024 evaluation ULPs, or
        similarly relaxing the old Utah solver, creates false outside/contact
        candidates and endpoint errors around 1e-4 where the analytic gate is
        around 1e-6.  Bypassing the Krawczyk requirement does not qualify the
        prepared answer: the independent complete-root-coverage gate rejects
        it.  Tolerance inflation is not a valid repair.

      * Adding deterministic seeds along the smallest-singular-vector
        direction to the old solver does not recover the pair.  A generic
        closest-point query also enters the wrong local basin on these
        ellipsoids, so neither experiment supplies a completeness argument.

    The evidence points to a fold rather than an iteration-budget problem.
    Near a generic tangent the weak reduced equation has the leading form
    a(q-q0)^2-epsilon: root separation, Jacobian conditioning, and normal/ray
    dot approach zero like sqrt(epsilon).  The next solver step should isolate
    the well-conditioned equation across bounded strips, bracket the weak
    direction on both sides of its fold, correct the roots in disjoint boxes,
    and use outward interval/Bernstein exclusion plus Krawczyk inclusion to
    account for the whole parent box.  Samples and an SVD may guide traversal
    but cannot authorize exclusion.  Exact contact, incomplete coverage, or
    fixed-workspace exhaustion must remain explicit conservative fallbacks.

50. A bounded diagnostic prototype gives positive evidence that the broader
    solver goal is tractable under the supported-patch, model-tolerance, and
    explicit-fallback contract.  Mapping the analytic root t values back into
    all retained boxes showed that the missing affine pair is not hidden in an
    arbitrary interior singularity.  Its two roots approach the ellipsoid's
    equatorial Bezier-span boundary from opposite sides: the relevant boxes
    are spans 6 and 7 meeting at v=0.5.  With unrestricted diagnostic storage,
    they appeared at box indices 64/65 of 104 for condition 100 and 206/207 of
    546 for condition 1,000.  The ordinary 64-box observation limit therefore
    did not even retain the relevant boxes before local correction.

    A locally invertible rotation of the two ray-plane equations, aligned to
    the dominant projected surface derivative, was then applied only as a
    conservative coefficient-hull exclusion test.  Each rotated coefficient
    was formed with the already outward interval addition/scaling operations.
    This reduced the false terminal ambiguity corridor from 104 and 546 boxes
    to exactly the two boundary-adjacent boxes.  Because F=0 implies M F=0 for
    any nonsingular finite 2x2 matrix M, the idea has a straightforward
    no-false-exclusion argument; at the prototype stage, a permanent
    implementation still needed a direct exact-dyadic enclosure gate for the
    new coefficient combinations and explicit telemetry before promotion.

    A second prototype solved the dominant rotated equation for the regular
    parameter at fixed values of the weak parameter, then bracketed the signed
    weak equation independently on each side of the span boundary.  It
    recovered two local roots in all four previously missed condition-1,000
    directed shots, including both h/T=1e-8 shots whose transformed analytic
    chord is 30.5 model tolerances.  Thus the roots are numerically recoverable
    without deeper global subdivision or tolerance inflation.  The
    condition-100 case produced three raw local observations because one
    existing Newton result and the two one-sided span results were not yet
    seam-deduplicated.

    The prototype was intentionally reverted after measurement.  Its roots
    were not published: strict Krawczyk inclusion on the original terminal
    boxes still fails as the roots approach their shared box boundary, and the
    production completeness gate correctly reports uncertified fallback.
    Promotion therefore requires smaller one-sided root boxes, conservative
    exclusion of every remaining rotated box, separate certification of each
    simple root, and topology-aware cross-span event deduplication.  Exact
    contact must remain ambiguous/fallback because strict regular-root
    certification is expected to fail at the double root.

    This narrows the tractability boundary.  Correct, substantially cheaper
    raytracing is a realistic engineering target for valid positive-weight
    patches within supported order, explicit model tolerances, fixed-workspace
    limits, and conservative fallback.  A universal exact, fixed-time,
    fixed-memory classifier for arbitrary rational surfaces, degenerate
    trims, and unspecified modeling intent is not a realistic target.  The
    project should continue to state the former contract explicitly rather
    than accidentally promising the latter.

51. The conditioned equation-hull exclusion from Finding 50 is now a
    permanent, allocation-free part of prepared surface isolation.  After the
    ordinary per-equation Bernstein hull retains a box, the implementation
    forms interval derivative hulls for both projected ray-plane equations.
    The midpoint column with the greater Euclidean magnitude supplies a local
    orthonormal equation basis.  Every transformed Bernstein coefficient is
    then formed from outward interval scaling and addition, including the
    complete inherited coefficient uncertainty.  A box is excluded only when
    one transformed coefficient hull proves that zero is absent.  A bad or
    nonfinite conditioning hint is inconclusive and retains the box; it cannot
    authorize exclusion.

    The safety property does not depend on the midpoint derivative being an
    accurate Jacobian estimate.  For every finite 2x2 matrix M, F=0 implies
    M F=0, so an outward enclosure of a transformed component that omits zero
    proves that the original system has no root in the box.  The derivative
    column only selects a useful search basis.  This deliberately gives the
    rotation less authority than the interval coefficient arithmetic.

    A private exact-dyadic gate now audits arbitrary two-equation linear hulls
    at every supported tensor order from 2x2 through 16x16.  Four signed
    integer matrices, transform exponents -100, 0, and 100, and coefficient
    exponents -400, -40, 0, 40, and 400 exercise 2,187,000 transformed
    coefficients.  Each observed lower and upper bound must enclose the exact
    nominal value plus declared uncertainty, and an observed exclusion may
    occur only when the exact hull also excludes zero.  A new
    `--interval-only` test mode runs this arithmetic gate without constructing
    the ray corpus.

    The permanent exclusion reproduces the diagnostic localization: each of
    the six unresolved high-condition affine grazing shots now retains at
    most two terminal boxes and performs at least one rotated-hull exclusion.
    It does not publish an uncertified root, and the production hit results
    remain unchanged.  On the 36 ordinary affine shots, all 36 now select the
    prepared result with no fallback through condition 1,000.  Surface-box
    visits fall from 16,860 to 970, a 94.2 percent reduction, with 446
    exclusions in 920 rotated-hull attempts and no inconclusive arithmetic.
    The normal sphere, seam, crack, trim, Cobb, affine-grazing, and
    Cauchy-Crofton gates preserve their prior classifications and partitions;
    the complete corpus still ends with `BREP ray correctness corpus: PASS`.
    Focused optimized and ASan/UBSan `--affine-only` and `--interval-only`
    runs pass without a sanitizer diagnostic.  Certification, full local
    coverage, and cross-span deduplication remain prerequisites for promoting
    the recovered fold roots.

52. A second bounded fold experiment narrows the remaining proof and
    precision requirements without changing production behavior.  On each of
    the two boundary-adjacent boxes for every one of the six unresolved affine
    rays, a nine-sample regular/weak decomposition found a scalar sign bracket
    and converged it with fixed bisection.  All twelve expected local
    candidates were recovered with transformed residuals between zero and
    about 2e-16.  Their nearest parent-boundary distances were 0.00176 through
    0.0257 of the relevant box width, so the candidates were not numerical
    artifacts placed exactly on the shared span boundary.

    Strict Krawczyk trials on candidate-centered anisotropic boxes exposed the
    actual precision limit.  The normalized midpoint-Jacobian determinant
