    Corrected scalar root representatives no longer have to bracket the edge
    projection.  The initial version did so with a binary64 roundoff margin;
    the 0.01x reverse-similarity case placed one representative about 1.6e-12
    past the projection while its certified terminal box still enclosed it.
    Representative ordering is not a proof.  The final rule uses the outward
    terminal boxes for edge-t containment and retains strict representative
    ordering only against the outer material endpoints.  This restores ray-
    reversal and scale invariance without introducing a model-tolerance t
    merge.

    Contact roots emit no transition.  Only after the full transaction and an
    event-capacity check do their boxes become `RESOLVED_CONTACT`; outer source
    boxes become `RESOLVED_BOUNDARY`.  The two real endpoints receive distinct
    `SEAM_CONTACT_EXISTING` and `SEAM_CONTACT_CONTINUATION` certificates.  The
    trace records one contact pair, the actual box count, and exactly two
    roots.  Publication eligibility requires the matched certificate pair,
    contact counters 1/>0/2, four direction checks, no old tangent-witness or
    edge-only evidence, and matching near-trim box accounting.  Ordinary seam
    pairs conversely require zero contact counters and their original witness
    accounting.  Moving the event-capacity check ahead of disposition changes
    also closes a latent non-atomic overflow path in the older seam subtype.

    The immutable test validator independently reconstructs both contact
    roots, their ordering, trim state, normal state, source/contact box roles,
    root coverage, UV/t containment, contact-box hull, and the two outer
    analytic sphere endpoints.  Its Möbius-reparameterized Cobb oracle applies
    the exact fixture mapping rather than calling production mapping logic.
    The 416-ray matrix changes from 16 to 22 certified seam pairs, 32 to 44
    seam events, 118 to 124 selected prepared partitions, and six ownership/
    witness failures to zero.  The new aggregate is six contact pairs, twelve
    boxes, and twelve roots.  Implicit-oracle differences fall from 268 to 264;
    all 208 tolerance-band differences and 56 outside-band differences remain
    explicitly classified, with zero below-envelope leaks in this endpoint-
    preserving family.

    The existing classifier similarity gate now adds the exact contact state
    to identity, nonlinear trim reparameterization, translation, cyclic and
    arbitrary-axis rotations plus translation, 0.01x scale, and 10,000x scale
    with large translation.  Both directions pass in all seven forms: fourteen
    contact certificates preserve the analytic normalized sphere chord.  The
    adaptive surface isolator legitimately represents a contact pair with one
    or two terminal boxes depending on transform; physical ownership and the
    final interval remain invariant.

    A first endpoint-moving family now moves the complete rational boundary
    row while preserving weights, measures the resulting target/adjacent-edge
    and endpoint-vertex lift discrepancies, and sets each affected declared
    tolerance from that independent measurement.  It covers g/T = 0.25, 0.9,
    and 1.1,
    inward/outward signs, h/T = 2.0, 0.9, 0.25, 0, and -0.25, and reversal: 60
    rays total.  All target edges retain supported correspondence but report
    `SEAM_GAP_UNAVAILABLE` before adaptive gap traversal (zero cells and no
    exhaustion), so the new contact subtype publishes none.  The baseline has
    16 legacy one-hit cases, eight unrelated ordinary prepared selections, 48
    implicit/variant differences all inside the explicit uncertainty band,
    and two below-envelope no-partition leaks.  `rt_brep_ray_endpoint` makes
    this a parallel monotone ratchet: those counts may improve but may not
    increase, and any difference outside the uncertainty band fails.  This is
    preserved evidence of the endpoint/vertex correspondence gap, not a claim
    that endpoint-moving crack handling is correct.

    Optimized verification after the final segment and representation gates:

      cmake --build .build --target rt_brep_ray -j6
      ctest --test-dir .build --parallel 7 \
        -R '^rt_brep_ray($|_)' --output-on-failure

    This checkpoint resolves one exact two-root incident-face contact shape,

70. Endpoint-moving discrepancy now has a topological boundary certificate,
    and seam dispatch no longer depends on which solver isolated the root.

    Finding 69's endpoint family stopped before its first discrepancy-bound
    cell even though the shared-projection correspondence theorem succeeded.
    The failure was a stronger assumption than BREP topology provides:
    `brep_bound_edge_trim_discrepancy` used a geometric nearest-point solve for
    the edge-domain endpoints and then required the resulting trim parameters
    to equal the trim domain endpoints to binary64 parameter roundoff.  Once a
    complete boundary row and its surface-lifted endpoints move within declared
    tolerance, the trim endpoint remains topologically paired to the edge
    vertex but need not be the nearest lifted point to the edge endpoint.
    Consequently every target returned `SEAM_GAP_UNAVAILABLE` with zero cells;
    this was a parameter-boundary assumption, not a failure of the global
    injectivity/orientation certificate or the rational Hausdorff bounds.

    Prep now certifies each of the four edge/trim endpoint incidences before
    bounding discrepancy.  For edge endpoint e, trim endpoint
    `m_bRev3d ? 1-e : e` must name exactly `edge.m_vi[e]`.  The 3-D edge endpoint
    and the surface lift of that exact trim-domain endpoint must both be within
    the same `ON_BrepVertex` point by the maximum of the BRL-CAD model distance
    tolerance and that vertex's valid declared tolerance, plus a coordinate-
    scaled 512-epsilon/`ON_ZERO_TOLERANCE` roundoff envelope.  The test follows
    the OpenNURBS tolerance contract directly and reconstructs these distances
    without calling production code.  A large edge tolerance or the other
    endpoint's tolerance cannot satisfy this per-vertex proof.

    The two certified topological trim parameters now seed the complete gap
    partition.  The first cell starts at the oriented trim-domain endpoint;
    each following Bezier edge span inherits the preceding trim endpoint
    exactly; and the last cell ends at the other topological endpoint.  Only
    interior span boundaries use the geometric nearest-point map, constrained
    to the remaining directed trim interval.  Adaptive midpoint solves remain
    constrained to their parent interval.  This covers both complete loci,
    avoids a boundary hole, and preserves the earlier nonlinear Möbius trim-
    reparameterization theorem.  The same positive-weight control hulls and
    strict gap comparison remain in force.  A new
    `discrepancy_endpoints_certified` record/trace field is required explicitly
    by closure and seam consumers; unavailable, ambiguous, outside, and
    exhausted proofs remain fallback.

    The first transformed endpoint check then exposed a second independent
    representation dependency.  Identity and pure translation produced one
    ordinary seam pair, but arbitrary rotation or uniform scaling isolated the
    one surviving surface root with Krawczyk.  Physical dispatch equated "all
    boxes Krawczyk-qualified" with "regular solid boundary stream" and never
    attempted seam completion, even though the single clean ENTER or LEAVE was
    unbalanced.  Krawczyk proves local surface-root uniqueness, not completeness
    of a closed solid's physical transitions.  Dispatch now counts retained
    clean/near non-tangent ENTER and LEAVE roots before mutation.  A direction
    imbalance lets the unchanged seam theorem run regardless of the root-box
    solver; balanced all-Krawczyk streams still go directly to the regular
    theorem.  The existing endpoint/continuation, edge-sector, root/box
    ownership, event-capacity, and global material-state gates are unchanged.

    The primary endpoint-moving matrix still covers 60 rays: g/T = 0.25, 0.9,
    and 1.1; inward/outward displacement; h/T = 2.0, 0.9, 0.25, 0, and -0.25;
    and reversal.  All 60 independently satisfy their topological endpoint
    contract and now report a complete strict INSIDE discrepancy proof, with a
    maximum 1,598 cells and no exhaustion.  The original narrow seam theorem
    certifies the inward g/T=0.9, h/T=0.25 case in both directions using the
    standard existing/continuation certificates, not Finding 69's two-root
    contact-lobe certificates.  Both immutable event enclosures contain the
    implicit-sphere endpoints and their representatives agree within 2.5e-4 T.
    The two former below-envelope no-partition leaks disappear.  The new
    monotone baseline is 16 legacy one-hit cases, at least two independently
    oracle-validated certified pairs, ten current prepared selections, at most
    46 implicit/variant differences all within the declared uncertainty band,
    zero outside-band differences, and zero below-envelope leaks.

    A separate endpoint-specific similarity gate repeats that repaired state
    forward and backward under translation, arbitrary-axis rotation plus
    translation, 0.01x scale, and 10,000x scale with large translation.  All
    eight cases reconstruct the endpoint contract, obtain strict INSIDE gap
    proofs (1,278 cells in the current fixtures), publish the standard seam
    pair, contain the scaled implicit endpoints in their certified t boxes,
    and preserve representative accuracy.  Two in-memory g/T=2 controls lower
    one endpoint tolerance at a time and are rejected by the independent
    endpoint oracle.  They are deliberately not sent through BREP prep because
    the metadata is invalid; malformed-model prep robustness is separate from
    valid-model ray classification.

    The endpoint shard passes with this new 60-ray ratchet, eight transformed
    directions, and two metadata controls.  The full seam shard also passes;
    its endpoint-preserving bowed matrix improves from 264 to 262 implicit
    differences and from 208 to 206 uncertainty-band differences, while the
    56 outside-band cases, 22 certified pairs, and zero below-envelope leaks
    remain unchanged.  This checkpoint changes prep-time edge certificates and
    allocation-free shot dispatch only.  It does not change SurfaceTree
    density, cache serialization, fixed shot workspace sizes, or add shot-time
    allocation.

    This proves complete endpoint association for an edge discrepancy
    corridor; it is not a vertex-neighborhood transition theorem.  Rays whose
    relevant edge parameter lies in a vertex neighborhood, curved vertex fans,
    arbitrary endpoint overlap graphs, oblique contact intervals with varying
    edge frames, sector-boundary tangency, and non-manifold topology remain
    fail-closed targets.

71. The first bounded oblique contact-lobe subtype is now a qualified
    production path.  It preserves Finding 69's exact two-root incident-face
    topology and outer-event publication, but replaces the perpendicular
    branch's frozen edge UV, ray t, and material frame with a complete local
    interval certificate.  The perpendicular branch and its original tests
    remain unchanged.

    Prep retains the terminal cells from each strict edge/trim discrepancy
    proof.  Each `brep_edge_trim_cell` records the exact edge interval,
    constrained trim interval, prepared Bezier edge span, and trim index.
    Cells are installed only for a completed strict `SEAM_GAP_INSIDE` edge
    whose two correspondences passed the exact isoparametric theorem.  Generic
    injective/oriented correspondence, unavailable/ambiguous/outside gap
    proofs, and exhausted budgets retain no frame-interval authority.  The
    target bowed-Cobb edge currently exposes 566 two-sided terminal cells.
    Cached prep rebuilds this process-local vector without changing the cache
    format.

    Shot-time proof is fixed-work and transactional.  For every unresolved
    zero-determinant contact box, it intersects the paired prepared partitions
    and locally remaps both trim intervals inside their parents.  Adjacent
    rootless unresolved zero-determinant boxes are attempted only when they
    share the already selected contact face and surface span.  A complete trim
    UV interval on that incident face must be contained in each box's UV
    rectangle.  The complete box t interval must be inside every material
    sector frame on the interval.  Every box, including a rootless corridor
    candidate, needs one certificate; linked box t intervals must cover the
    contact hull, and the edge tangent dot ray must have one nonzero consistent
    sign.  Rootless boxes on the source outer face or another span are not
    candidates.  Vertex neighborhoods, exact/interval parallelism,
    unsupported spans, mapping failures, and every work/depth/capacity limit
    return whole-ray fallback before any box or event mutation.  The proof
    uses a 32-cell stack, depth 20, at most 8,192 visited cells, fixed coverage
    storage, and insertion sorting; it performs no shot-time allocation.

    Edge points, tangents, both surface derivative frames, outward normals,
    and interior conormals are outward interval bounds over the complete cell.
    The rational derivative quotient-rule numerator is now formed as
    Bernstein product coefficients before taking a hull.  This preserves the
    shared `X'W-XW'` cancellation that independent numerator/weight hulls lost
    and avoids amplifying that dependency error when a small restriction is
    rescaled.  Surface derivative evaluation uses a small containing
    parameter neighborhood to keep outward arithmetic stable; widening cannot
    exclude the actual UV interval.

    Sector classification uses homogeneous determinant signs and the same
    edge-normal projection as the fixed-frame theorem.  For each fixed edge
    parameter, a boundary determinant is affine in ray t, so strict interval
    signs at both box endpoints prove the complete segment.  Convex sectors
    require both boundary predicates throughout; reflex sectors require one
    predicate throughout, preventing the segment from crossing the
    complementary convex cone.  A turn interval containing zero is accepted
    only as a smooth pi sector when the projected conormal dot is strictly
    negative and both boundary predicates pass.  Every determinant containing
    zero otherwise fails closed.

    The first exploratory projection-linked implementation was removed.  It
    could associate a box with an edge point that merely had the same ray
    projection while lying physically far along the seam.  A second blind
    low-parameter-first search exhausted its budget before reaching the
    prepared closest-edge witness.  The final proof links by incident-face UV
    containment and only prioritizes the witness-containing child as a search
    order; all interval obligations remain unchanged.

    Tracing records `frame_interval_supported/cells` on the edge plus one
    oblique pair, certified frame-cell count, and box-link count.  Production
    eligibility requires one oblique pair, at least one frame cell, and links
    equal to contact boxes.  Perpendicular contact and ordinary seam pairs
    require all oblique counters to be zero.  The immutable validator accepts
    oblique ownership only with that evidence and still independently checks
    roots, boxes, outer events, and the implicit sphere chord.  The global
    root-coverage gate normally rejects any rootless terminal box.  Its sole
    exception is a box already resolved by a completed oblique transaction
    whose one-pair ledger links every contact box.  A rootless box cannot gain
    production authority merely from its location, determinant, or proximity.

    At this checkpoint the permanent trend used seam-tangent components 1e-4,
    1e-3, 0.01, 0.05,
    0.25, 0.5, and 1 in both ray directions: all fourteen require production
    certification and analytic chord agreement.  The 1e-3 and 0.05 cases
    require certification of one or two contact boxes despite only one box
    owning a nominal surface root.  Components 1e-6, 10, and 1e6 require six
    safe fallbacks.  Component 0.1 is observational in both directions and
    remains fallback because its extra unresolved box is on the source outer
    face, where the contact theorem cannot prove surface-root completeness.  A
    separate 14-case gate repeats component 1e-3 forward/reverse under
    identity, nonlinear trim reparameterization, translation, cyclic and
    arbitrary-axis rotation, 0.01x scale, and 10,000x scale.  Every case
    selects production and contains the scaled analytic sphere endpoints.

72. A connected source-root union now supplies the missing surface-root count
    theorem for the component-0.1 oblique Cobb cases.

    The forward case had three terminal boxes.  Contact face/span 0/0 owned
    one unresolved contact box.  Source face/span 1/1 had a depth-20 regular
    Krawczyk box containing the analytic outer sphere root at approximately
    UV (0.0070216, 0.499223), plus a rootless depth-24 zero-determinant box
    immediately below it.  Their V intervals met at 0.4992098714, their U
    intervals overlapped, and their outward t intervals overlapped.  Reverse
    had the same two source boxes plus two separately certified contact boxes.
    The contact material-sector theorem could prove that contact boxes hide no
    transition, but said nothing about a second root on the outer source
    surface.  The former whole-ray fallback was therefore correct.

    The new `brep_trace_seam_source_union` proof begins only inside a qualified
    finite nonparallel oblique contact transaction.  The source face/span must
    differ from the contact face/span.  Every source-root-owning box must
    already be `RESOLVED_REGULAR` with one nonzero consistent determinant
    sign.  Additional candidates must be unresolved, zero-determinant,
    rootless boxes on exactly that source face/span.  Fixed boolean arrays grow
    only the component connected to a root box by closed UV-rectangle
    intersection and outward t-interval overlap, with a 256-epsilon roundoff
    allowance.  A disconnected box or a box containing any local/fold root is
    never considered owned.

    The proof takes the component's complete rectangular UV hull, maps it into
    the prepared Bezier span, widens its normalized boundaries outward by one
    binary64 step, reconstructs the two ray-plane Bernstein systems from the
    positive-weight control net, and restricts them with the existing audited
    outward arithmetic.  Strict Krawczyk inclusion over that entire hull proves
    existence and uniqueness of one surface root.  Because the regular source
    box already contains a root, every connected rootless box is redundant
    isolator coverage rather than another solid transition.  All arrays and
    coefficients are fixed stack storage; there is no shot-time allocation.

    Publication remains atomic.  A successful seam event keeps a root-owning
    box as its representative, uses all component boxes for its outward t
    interval and source-box count, and records
    `physical_event_seam_source_union_certified/root_boxes/boxes`.  Eligibility
    requires either all three fields zero or exactly one certificate with
    `0 < root_boxes < boxes` and `boxes == event.source_box_count`.  The global
    root-coverage gate waives a rootless resolved-boundary box only after the
    complete seam eligibility check and that ledger.  The immutable validator
    independently checks representative ownership, determinant roles, the
    same UV/t connectivity graph, every root and box, and the analytic sphere
    chord.

    Component 0.1 now requires production in both directions with a `1/1/2`
    source-union ledger, raising the trend to sixteen required certifications,
    six fail-closed threshold controls, and no observational cases.  The
    similarity/reparameterization/reversal gate retains the fourteen
    component-1e-3 contact-corridor cases and adds fourteen component-0.1
    source-union cases.  All 28 select the same analytic sphere partition.
    Isolation topology needs the union proof in eight of those cases; the
    other six component-0.1 cases have no rootless source box and correctly
    carry an all-zero ledger.  Thus physical classification is similarity
    invariant without requiring solver box topology itself to be invariant.
    Each identity direction also copies its valid trace into three negative
    controls.  The immutable validator rejects a UV-disconnected rootless box,
    `root_boxes == boxes` ledger corruption, and selection of the rootless box
    as the event representative.

73. A solver-level source-union corpus now tests the root-count theorem
    independently of Cobb geometry and adaptive box topology.

    `brep_source_union_component` is shared by production and the private test
    hook.  It validates finite ordered UV/t boxes on exactly one face/span,
    seeds only boxes already known to own the selected root, grows through the
    production closed-UV plus outward-t connectivity predicate, and computes
    the complete rectangular hull.  Production still derives root and
    candidate roles from immutable root/box provenance.  The synthetic hook
    supplies roles directly, but then uses the same one-step outward hull
    widening, bounded Bernstein restriction, and strict production Krawczyk
    inclusion.  A disconnected eligible candidate now stops the production
    helper before restriction; the former path reached the global unowned-box
    rejection instead, so both behavior and authority remain fail-closed.

    Five exact systems are permanent:

      * cubic `F=(u-1/2)+(u-1/2)^3/4`, `G=v-1/2` over a connected two-box
        hull certifies one root;
      * UV-disconnected and t-disconnected copies each retain only the root
        box and do not attempt Krawczyk;
      * `F=(u-3/8)(u-5/8)`, `G=v-1/2` has one selected root and a second root
        in the connected candidate box, so complete-hull Krawczyk fails;
      * singular `F=(u-1/2)^2`, `G=v-1/2` uses rootless boxes on both sides of
        the root box and fails certification over its symmetric hull.

    A sixth invalid-input control marks a box as root-owning while supplying
    a nominal root outside it; provenance validation rejects it before graph
    or Krawczyk work.  The fast `rt_brep_ray_source_union` shard passes in
    0.02 seconds optimized and 0.15 seconds under ASan/UBSan.  The final exact
    optimized eight-shard matrix passes in 154.89 seconds, with the real seam
    and interval paths at 119.61 and 154.84 seconds.  The corpus is also called
    by the interval and unsharded complete tests.  The exact-code ASan/UBSan
    seam companion passes in 745.40 seconds with no diagnostic; it overlapped
    an optimized rebuild and is not a performance baseline.  All graph storage
    remains fixed-capacity stack memory.

74. Oblique material-sector frames now support globally certified curved
    non-isoparametric trims through complete retained rational intervals.

    Prep no longer discards frame authority merely because a trim is not
    W/E/S/N isoparametric.  The existing global correspondence theorem must
    still certify the edge and both trims as injective and consistently
    oriented.  The strict discrepancy transaction now also retains each
    authorized trim's immutable positive-weight rational Bezier spans.
    Installed `brep_edge_trim_cell` records identify the complete side-local
    span range.  Cells and spans are appended only after both endpoint
    contracts, both correspondences, and both strict gap bounds complete, so
    no partial span data can acquire production authority.  Cached prep
    reconstructs the process-local spans from the BREP; cache serialization is
    unchanged.

    At shot time, `brep_interval_trim_cell_geometry` intersects a mapped trim
    cell with every retained span and restricts homogeneous numerator and
    weight controls with the existing outward interval arithmetic.  Positive
    weight division encloses the complete UV locus.  Coefficient-wise
    `X'W-XW'` rational derivatives enclose both UV differential components.
    The union must cover the complete trim parameter cell, and its UV hull must
    fit exactly one prepared surface span.  Missing spans, incomplete
    coverage, a surface-span crossing that local subdivision cannot isolate,
    nonfinite arithmetic, unsupported order, or any work limit fails the
    whole ray closed.

    The generic loop tangent is the interval chain rule
    `L=Su*(du/ds)+Sv*(dv/ds)`.  The local span parameter differs from the
    original trim parameter only by a positive scale, so every homogeneous
    orientation and material-sector sign is preserved.  All prior outward
    normal, interior conormal, edge-tangent, complete ray-t sector, monotone
    sweep, box ownership, source-union, immutable-event, and atomic
    publication obligations remain unchanged.  Exact isoparametric trims keep
    their endpoint/fixed-coordinate fast path.  Generic contact-face box
    ownership uses the complete rational UV hull rather than endpoints.

    A new valid Cobb fixture bows the target surface seam by exactly model
    tolerance and replaces the opposite incident trim with an
    endpoint-preserving quadratic Bezier bowed toward its face interior.  Its
    measured maximum 3D trim-lift change is `0.05*T`; the curve is required to
    be genuinely non-isoparametric, endpoint preserving, and BREP-valid.
    The maximum separation between the two lifted trims is `1.00125*T`, while
    their separate distances from the shared edge remain `T` and `0.05*T`;
    the declared edge tolerance is `1.01` times the two-lift separation.  This
    keeps model tolerance, edge metadata, and the geometric discrepancy facts
    explicit rather than using one number for all three roles.
    Seam-tangent components 0.001 and 0.1 in both directions require the
    analytic sphere chord, production selection, strict correspondence/gap
    evidence, a nonempty frame-cell proof, and the complete oblique ledger.
    Identity component 0.1 additionally requires the `1/1/2` connected
    source-union ledger.

    The same four rays pass after arbitrary-axis rotation plus translation,
    0.01x similarity, and 10,000x similarity with large translation: 16/16
    classifications select the same analytic partition.  As in Finding 72,
    transformed adaptive box topology may use either the ordinary source-root
    proof or a valid source-union proof; identity retains the exact union
    ratchet.  The existing hidden-reversal degree-15 trim remains a required
    correspondence/discrepancy fallback in the seam suite.  The focused
    `rt_brep_ray_nonisoparametric` shard isolates this matrix and runs in
    parallel with the larger ray tests.

    This adds prep memory proportional to retained trim Bezier spans only on
    strictly authorized seam edges.  It adds no shot-time allocation and does
    not change SurfaceTree density or fixed workspaces.  A trim perturbation on
    the contact-root face can change which roots the earlier closure stage
    admits; this checkpoint does not invent those roots or replace that stage
    with a tolerance tube.  Changed contact-root topology, unsupported
    rational data, arbitrary faces/spans/root counts, exact or sector-boundary
    tangency, vertex neighborhoods, and non-manifold topology remain
    fail-closed.

75. The production rational trim restriction now has an independent
    solver-level corpus, and complete span coverage is an interval-union proof
    rather than a length sum.

    A private `_rt_brep_trim_interval_test` hook accepts at most four fixed
    rational Bezier spans, constructs a test-local `brep_specific`, and calls
    `brep_interval_trim_cell_geometry` directly.  It does not copy or
    approximate the production interval algorithm.  Test construction may
    allocate, but the helper exercised by production remains fixed-storage and
    allocation free.  Invalid interface data is distinct from unavailable
    theorem data: malformed/nonfinite requests return false, while missing
    provenance, incomplete coverage, nonpositive weights, or an OpenNURBS
    construction refusal return a valid result with `available=0`.

    The former shot and prep coverage checks summed all positive
    span-intersection lengths.  That was not a proof of a complete locus:
    duplicate or overlapping spans could contribute the same parameter
    interval twice and hide a gap.  Both stages now share
    `brep_trim_spans_cover`, which grows a covered prefix from the cell minimum
    by repeatedly selecting the farthest reachable span endpoint.  It succeeds
    only when that interval union reaches the cell maximum.  The scan is
    bounded by the retained span count, assumes no span ordering, and
    allocates no storage.  Boundaries retained from the same knot vector must
    meet exactly; any representable parameter gap fails closed rather than
    becoming geometric authority through a numerical allowance.  UV and
    derivative hulls still include every positive-length intersection;
    coverage cannot excuse an invalid weight, order, or interval operation.

    Fourteen deterministic requests cover a positive rational quadratic on a
    monotone subinterval, two joined spans in both storage orders, reversed
    control direction, an exact span-boundary cell, missing and zero-count
    provenance, incomplete coverage, a deliberately overlap-masked gap, zero
    and negative weights, a rational turning derivative containing zero, and
    nonfinite controls and domains.  The rational quadratic is independently
    evaluated at 65 points.
    Every UV value and quotient-rule derivative lies inside the production
    result; derivative comparison includes the positive local
    reparameterization scale introduced by interval restriction.  Strict
    derivative signs are required where mathematically valid and zero must
    remain enclosed at the turning point.

    The focused optimized `rt_brep_ray_trim_interval` shard passes in 0.01
    seconds and reports fourteen cases, six available enclosures, six valid
    fail-closed results, and two invalid-input rejections.  The exhaustive
    `rt_brep_ray_interval` shard, which now includes the corpus, passes in
    143.15 seconds in the final ten-process matrix.  Full parallel and
    sanitizer matrices are recorded in the verification log below.

    This checkpoint qualifies complete rational trim UV and derivative
    enclosures only.  It does not change SurfaceTree density, root isolation,
    contact-face ownership, seam tolerances, or publication authority.  A
    contact-face trim perturbation can still alter which exact surface roots
    the earlier closure stage admits.  Extending that behavior requires an
    allocation-free local surface/trim/edge tube plus a separate root-ownership
    theorem; proximity samples and tolerance-manufactured roots remain
    forbidden.

76. A contact-face trim may now contribute one explicitly tube-qualified
    classifier miss to the narrow incident-face contact-lobe theorem.

    Contact-face perturbation isolated the earlier failure stage.  With a
    `0.05*T` trim lift, the contact face still reports two mathematical trim
    hits.  At `0.1*T`, the same three ray/surface roots, three terminal boxes,
    correspondence proof, closure candidate, continuation, and material
    ordering remain, but the exact edge-coincident root changes from
    `CLEAN_HIT` to `CLEAN_MISS`.  Hard model-tolerance closure also rejected
    the complete discrepancy `1.00498*T` even when the edge explicitly
    declared a larger accuracy envelope.

    Ordinary and legacy closure still use the hard model tolerance `T`.
    Declared edge metadata must not broaden that classifier: the bowed-seam
    matrix deliberately requires every complete discrepancy above `T` to
    report zero ordinary closure candidates, even when the edge declaration
    is larger.

    A separate provisional classifier exists only inside the prepared
    contact-event transaction.  After hard closure fails, it may compute
    `C=max(model_tolerance, declared_edge_tolerance)` only when the edge
    declaration is valid, explicit, and strictly larger than `T`.  A cheap
    immutable-ledger screen first requires exactly two transverse roots on the
    other incident face/span, ordered ENTER/LEAVE with exactly one trim hit and
    one trim miss.  Only then is the continuation solve attempted.  The
    provisional state remains private and its prepared candidate counters are
    deferred until the authoritative contact theorem proves the same one-miss
    pattern.  A zero-miss contact, arbitrary miss layout, or failed later proof
    discards it without changing legacy closure or publishing a transaction.

    This separation is a tested correction, not just documentation.  The
    first implementation put `C` directly in the general closure classifier.
    It caused 31 failures in the exhaustive seam shard by activating bowed
    discrepancies at `g/T=1.1`, `2`, and `10`; aggregate certified seam pairs,
    prepared selections, and seam events moved from `22/124/44` to
    `56/158/112`.  Restoring hard `T` closure and screening the private
    declared-tolerance path before continuation restores the exact
    `22/124/44` ratchets while retaining all sixteen intended contact-face
    certifications.

    The explicit path does not use the prep record's effective tolerance,
    because that value can contain vertex metadata and, for an unset edge
    tolerance, the measured discrepancy itself.  Inferred tolerance therefore
    remains useful for candidate observation but cannot authorize closure or a
    trim miss.  Invalid model tolerance fails closed, and an invalid/unset edge
    declaration contributes no expansion beyond model tolerance.

    The contact-root theorem still requires exactly two transverse roots on
    one other incident face and span, ordered ENTER then LEAVE strictly inside
    the outer source/continuation segment.  Both must be exact ray/surface
    solver results.  At least one must remain a clean/near trim hit; at most
    one may be a clean/near trim miss.  For that miss, the production code maps
    the certified shared-edge parameter to the contact trim and evaluates the
    shared edge point, corresponding surface-lifted trim point, and miss-root
    surface point.  The root point must lie within `C` of both model-space
    references.  No root is fabricated, reflected, merged by t distance, or
    moved to the edge.

    The new point witness only associates an already solved root with the
    authorized seam corridor.  It cannot publish an event independently.
    Complete source and contact box ownership, connected source-union where
    needed, complete rational trim-cell coverage, outward material-sector
    intervals over every contact box, monotone edge sweep, event order, and
    minimum material segment remain mandatory.  The miss count is published
    atomically with the two hidden contact roots only after that full
    transaction succeeds.  All older seam/contact tests explicitly require
    zero misses.

    The focused `rt_brep_ray_contact_trim` shard bows the valid
    endpoint-preserving quadratic trim on the contact face by `0.1*T`.  It
    requires the analytic sphere chord, production selection, the complete
    oblique ledger, and exactly one miss for tangent components `0.001` and
    `0.1`, both ray directions, and identity, rotated/translated, `0.01x`, and
    `10,000x` similarities.  Direct output reports 16/16 certifications, 16
    miss roots, two identity source unions, lift `0.1*T`, and gap
    `1.00498*T`.  Every positive ray now also requires zero ordinary closure
    candidates and one prepared closure/continuation candidate.  Six
    forward/reverse controls with a model-only declaration, an exact-gap
    declaration without strict proof margin, and an unset/inferred declaration
    require zero ordinary and prepared closure/continuation candidates and
    remain on fallback with no published seam transaction.

    At this checkpoint, a stronger interval-root tube had been explored and
    removed rather than being allowed to create transform-dependent authority.
    The existing terminal root box had a complete surface hull about `0.00335`
    from the shared edge against `C=0.0005075`, and the first strict-interior
    Krawczyk variants certified only four of sixteen transformations.  Finding
    77 supersedes that point-only limitation with a boundary-capable local
    contraction and complete rational image proof; the historical failure is
    retained because it explains why terminal boxes and ordinary Krawczyk are
    not suitable authority.

77. Declared contact-face repair now requires a unique local analytic root
    whose complete rational image is inside both model-space tubes.

    Inspection of the actual identity/component-0.001 coefficient system
    showed that the miss root is exactly at normalized support boundary
    `u=1`, while the neighboring hit is about `1.7e-4` away in normalized
    infinity norm.  Both roots occupy the same terminal surface box.  A proof
    requiring strict parameter-domain interior, or the complete terminal box
    to fit the seam tube, therefore rejects the intended geometry for reasons
    unrelated to root existence.

    The new fixed-work solver converts both homogeneous ray-plane Bernstein
    equations into complete finite Taylor expansions at the already solved
    miss.  Bernstein finite differences, derivative evaluation, fixed-point
    images, and derivative bounds use outward fixed-expansion arithmetic;
    factorial scaling successively divides by exactly representable small
    integers with outward intervals.  A binary64 midpoint Jacobian inverse is
    only a preconditioner.  Starting at the larger of four times the correction
    bound and `1024 eps` of root scale, the solver tries at most 48 doubling
    radii.  It accepts only strict self-map inclusion with a `512 eps r`
    margin and infinity-norm contraction below one.  Production caps the
    radius below one quarter of the neighboring contact-root separation.
    Singular, nonfinite, expansion-capacity, inclusion, contraction, or radius
    failures leave the complete ray on legacy fallback.

    This contraction proves exactly one root of the analytic Bezier extension,
    but crossing a span boundary is not automatically geometric authority.
    A second theorem forms each homogeneous coordinate difference
    `X_i-P_i W` relative to the nominal rational point and encloses all three
    difference polynomials plus `W` over the complete symmetric root box with
    the same Taylor arithmetic.  The weight lower bound must remain strictly
    positive.  After outward rational division and Euclidean combination, the
    resulting model-image displacement `B` must satisfy

        |P-E| + B <= C + coordinate roundoff
        |P-L| + B <= C + coordinate roundoff.

    Thus the whole unique-root image, including its analytically extended
    portion, lies inside both the certified shared-edge and lifted-trim tubes.
    The nominal point checks remain useful screening evidence but no longer
    supply the final authority.

    The production transaction records declared-contact pairs, root attempts,
    availability, uniqueness, boundary extension, radius, image displacement,
    both tube upper bounds, tolerance, roundoff, and tube failures.  A selected
    declared repair requires exactly one attempt, one available unique root,
    no tube failure, and the unchanged full box/source/material/event theorem.
    Ordinary model-tolerance and zero-miss paths require an empty local-proof
    ledger.  The proof performs no allocation during ray tracing.

    The focused C/C++ arithmetic corpus now has thirteen classified systems.
    The original layer has three affine boundary roots and three nearby
    quadratic root pairs at equation scales `2^-100`, `1`, and `2^100`, plus
    multi-root, singular, and uncertain interval systems.  The conditioning
    layer adds a degree-elevated order-9-by-9 affine system, a coupled
    degree-15-by-7 boundary root, and nearly singular affine systems with
    Jacobian determinants `2^-20` and `2^-40`.  Ten cases certify uniquely in
    one attempt and the three ambiguous/singular cases fail closed.
    Invalid-input and explicit tube pass/reject controls are additional gates.

    Three actual prepared rational bilinear surfaces independently exercise
    coefficient extraction and the complete model-image theorem: equal
    weights; a `2^20` control-weight ratio with ray reversal; and the same
    ratio after `10,000x` scale and a large translation.  All three certify a
    support-boundary-crossing root with positive weight and finite complete
    image.  Maximum image displacement is `2.27e-9`; root and model expansion
    high-water are both 2.  A `2^400` ratio is now retained as a required prep
    rejection: its parameter derivative is numerically singular under the
    current OpenNURBS normal threshold.  Finding 79 makes that rejection
    fail-closed instead of aborting the process.  It remains a prep robustness
    case, not a local-certificate failure and not a reason to relax the
    theorem.

    The real Cobb test independently replays the actual prepared coefficient
    intervals for all sixteen transformations and reversals.  All sixteen
    certify in one attempt, all sixteen root boxes cross the analytic support
    boundary, all exclude the neighboring root, and all have positive weight
    and complete image containment.  Maxima are radius `9.13e-11`, correction
    `2.28e-11`, contraction `2.54e-6`, model image `2.16e-5`, and
    fixed-expansion high-water 26.  The image maximum is from the `10,000x`
    similarity and scales as model-space length.

78. Real near-grazing sphere roots now ratchet the local certificate down to
    one tenth of model tolerance without granting general grazing authority.

    The existing signed sphere matrix already sweeps hit-side clearances from
    `2^-1` through `2^-50`, tolerance-targeted chords, exact tangency, mirrored
    misses, reversal, and similarity; the affine matrix separately covers
    anisotropic conditioning.  What was missing was a direct bridge from real
    close curved-surface roots into the fixed-expansion uniqueness and
    rational-image theorem introduced by Finding 77.

    The focused bridge chooses the generic unit radial `(2,3,6)/7` and unit
    tangent `(3,-2,0)/sqrt(13)`, away from the converted sphere's seams and
    span boundaries.  In both ray directions it constructs analytic chords of
    `100`, `10`, `2`, `1.1`, `1`, `0.9`, `0.5`, `0.1`, and `0.01` times model
    distance tolerance.  Each raw local root is matched to
    `2R +/- chord/2`, and its measured absolute normal dot is compared to
    `chord/(2R)`.  Both roots must be on one prepared face/span.  The test
    first obtains their normalized UV separation, then reruns each complete
    root/model-image certificate with a downward-rounded radius cap below one
    quarter of that separation.  Every accepted root box must remain strictly
    inside the analytic span with positive rational weight.

    All ten resolved directed pairs through one tolerance certify.  Six more
    sub-tolerance pairs at `0.9T`, `0.5T`, and `0.1T` certify.  At `0.01T` the
    two candidate pairs are unavailable, with no later restart.  Both exact
    tangent directions retain a raw contact observation but correctly refuse
    a unique-root certificate, and all eighteen mirrored miss-side rays have
    no local root or final segment.  Minimum normalized root separation is
    `5.92e-6`; maximum certificate-radius/separation is `0.00239`, contraction
    `0.0258`, model-image displacement `3.87e-7`, normal-dot error `3.47e-9`,
    and fixed-expansion high-water 10.  Every certificate takes one attempt.

    This result proves that the new local theorem remains effective as two
    real rational-surface roots converge and become ill-conditioned.  It does
    not prove candidate completeness over the parent region, absence of other
    roots, material pairing, or exact-double-root classification.  Those are
    independent obligations for the intended production grazing pipeline.
    The focused `rt_brep_ray_grazing_root` CTest shard makes this distinction a
    permanent, independently parallelizable ratchet.

79. Numerically unavailable SurfaceTree faces now fail complete BREP prep
    instead of aborting the process or permitting a partial acceleration tree.

    `initialBBox` previously called `bu_bomb` when `surface_EvNormal` could not
    produce the estimate point and normal.  The positive-weight rational
    bilinear diagnostic with weights `2^-200` and `2^200` reached this path:
    its geometry and homogeneous controls are finite, but the parameter
    derivative is below the OpenNURBS normal threshold at the midpoint.  A
    single difficult input could therefore terminate conversion, rendering,
    or a test process before librt could report prep failure.

    `initialBBox` now evaluates the midpoint of its actual local `u,v` box and
    returns no node when the normal is unavailable.  Surface subdivision
    treats every missing expected child as failure of the complete subtree,
    deletes every sibling built for that subdivision, and propagates failure
    to the root.  After parallel face construction joins, `brep_build_bvh`
    validates every SurfaceTree before releasing any root or CurveTree.  One
    invalid face deletes all temporary builders and makes `rt_brep_prep`
    return failure.  No face can disappear from an otherwise successful solid
    BVH.

    An invalid SurfaceTree is now an ordinary observable builder state:
    depth, closest-estimate, leaf, and surface-point queries are null-safe, and
    the libged and librt plotting helpers skip it.  Existing callers that use
    `Valid()` continue unchanged.  Successful prep retains the prior ownership
    order: every CurveTree is transferred only after all faces validate, the
    temporary builders are destroyed, and the top-level BVH owns all released
    roots.

    The weighted focused corpus now has four surfaces.  Equal weights and the
    two `2^20` weight-range/reversal/similarity cases still prep and certify;
    the `2^400` case must return prep failure without invoking the local-root
    hook.  This is deliberately whole-solid fail-closed behavior.  A future
    algorithm may support more extreme parameterizations, but it may not
    recover by raytracing an incomplete face set.

VERIFICATION LOG
----------------

Ownership implementation:

  * include/brep/surfacetree.h and src/libbrep/opennurbs_ext.cpp now provide
    releaseRootNode() and releaseCurveTree().

  * brep_specific is now constructed/destructed as a C++ object and owns a
    vector of CurveTrees.  Teardown order is BVH, CurveTrees, then ON_Brep.

  * brep_build_bvh transfers each builder's CurveTree and BBNode root, destroys
    the temporary SurfaceTree immediately, and no longer writes
    ON_BrepFace::m_face_user.p.

  * Cache deserialization records each new CurveTree in the same owner vector.

Verification commands and results:

  cmake --build .build --target librt rt_primitive_brep -j8
    PASS

  ctest --test-dir .build -R '^rt_primitive_brep$' --output-on-failure
    PASS: 1/1

  cmake --build .build --target rt_cache -j8
    PASS

  ctest --test-dir .build -R '^rt_cache_' --output-on-failure
    PASS: 7/7, including serial/parallel, repeated identical/different BREP,
    and hierarchy cache cases.

  ctest --test-dir .build \
    -R '^regress-nurbs_(NIST03_MISS_01|SPH_SOLID_HIT_LEN_01|BOX_PLATE_HIT_LEN_01)$' \
    --output-on-failure
    PASS: 3/3

Newton hardening:

  * The solver returns explicit converged, duplicate, outside-domain,
    singular-Jacobian, stalled, iteration-limit, evaluation, non-finite, and
    capacity outcomes.

  * An initial positional root is accepted before testing the Jacobian.  A
    singular parameterization uses surface_EvNormal for a finite unit normal.

  * EvPoint/Ev1Der results, residuals, Jacobian columns, Newton steps, root t,
    and normals are checked for failure and non-finite values.  The random
    singular-Jacobian jitter was removed.

In-memory correctness corpus:

  * src/librt/tests/brep_ray.cpp constructs one analytic rt_ell_internal,
    converts that exact internal with ft_brep, preps both paths, and shoots
    identical rays without generating .g files.

  * Exact north-pole, south-pole/reverse, and two regular-axis rays compare
    ordered segment endpoints and validate converted-BREP normals over 16
    identical repetitions.

  * Seam-boundary inward rays and rays beginning inside are hard paired gates.
    The outward surface-start discrepancy is retained as a known-defect
    ratchet.  Additional fixtures repeat pole, seam, and inside checks on a
    translated 0.01 mm sphere and on a radius-10,000 mm sphere translated to
    million-unit coordinates.

  * A logarithmic and target-chord positive-h sweep enforces deterministic,
    non-restarting, non-growing BREP interval behavior.  Chords at least the
    model distance tolerance must resolve with analytic endpoint agreement;
    smaller chords may collapse to a contact-sized segment or miss but may not
    reintroduce material larger than the minimum size.  Exact tangency and all
    representable mirrored negative-h samples must be misses in both paths.

  * A 20,000-line Cauchy-Crofton check estimates area and volume independently
    on the implicit sphere and its converted BREP.  Both must be within 3% of
    analytic 4*pi*R^2 and 4*pi*R^3/3, and the two representations must agree
    within 1%.  This is the first aggregate ground-truth gate; exact shared
    bounding sphere and per-line differences remain to be implemented.

  cmake --build .build --target rt_brep_ray -j8
    PASS

  ctest --test-dir .build -R '^rt_brep_ray$' --output-on-failure
    PASS: 1/1

  .build/src/librt/tests/rt_brep_ray --grazing-report
    REPORT: BREP follows the analytic square-root chord trend through a chord
    of one model distance tolerance, reports a contact-sized segment at 0.9
    tolerance, and is a miss by 0.61 tolerance.  Exact tangency and every
    sampled negative clearance are misses.  The raw solver/contact status is
    not yet instrumented, so CONTACT here is the test's segment-length class.

Conditioned root convergence:

  * The two ray-plane residuals are evaluated relative to the ray origin.  A
    root must satisfy both the 1e-7 perpendicular ceiling and the corresponding
    first-order ray-distance estimate based on |N dot D|/|D|.  A 1e-13 floor
    prevents an exact double root from requiring literal floating-point zero.

  * Simply reducing the fixed residual threshold to 1e-9 or 1e-12 repaired the
    R=10 tangent but lost the million-unit translated north-pole root.  A fixed
    1e-8 happened to pass the current corpus but retained a smaller tangent
    false interval.  Those rejected experiments demonstrate why the
    conditioning term and translated fixture are both necessary.

  cmake --build .build --target \
    librt rt_primitive_brep rt_brep_ray rt_cache -j8
    PASS

  ctest --test-dir .build -j4 \
    -R '^(rt_brep_ray|rt_primitive_brep|rt_cache_.*|regress-nurbs_(NIST03_MISS_01|SPH_SOLID_HIT_LEN_01|BOX_PLATE_HIT_LEN_01))$' \
    --output-on-failure
    PASS: 12/12

Shared-line Crofton and final partitions:

  * The test now serializes the implicit sphere and its converted BREP into
    separate in-memory databases, prepares normal rt_i instances, and shoots
    each line through rt_shootray.  Fixed-capacity records retain every final
    partition endpoint and post-Boolean entry/exit normal.

  * One fixed-seed 32,000-line set is generated from an explicitly shared
    bounding sphere using the production Crofton point-pairing algorithm.
    Nested checkpoints at 2,000, 8,000, and 32,000 rays report independent
    area/volume estimates and sample standard errors.  A four-standard-error
    confidence band guards both implicit and BREP convergence to the analytic
    sphere values.

  * All 32,000 final partition lists agree, with no tangent-band exceptions.
    Maximum paired endpoint error is approximately 9.69e-8 mm.  Signed and
    absolute total chord differences are approximately 6.74e-5 mm and
    8.15e-5 mm respectively over the full set; both aggregate representations
    remain far inside the paired 0.1 percent gate.

  * The harness is generalized to any ft_brep-capable implicit internal with
    explicit shared bounds.  Five additional fixtures each retain 8,000 shared
    lines: a translated oblate ellipsoid, translated ARB8 box, RCC, truncated
    cone, and torus.  Their analytic surface areas and volumes are confidence
    gates, not merely paired comparisons.

  * Exact directed final-partition gates cover ellipsoid pole and periodic
    seam, ARB8 forward/reverse faces and opposite vertices, cylinder/cone axes
    and periodic seams, and torus forward/reverse two-interval rays, central
    hole miss, and tube-vertical hit.  Entry/exit normals must be finite, unit,
    and correctly oriented after Boolean processing.

  * Across 72,000 shared lines for all six fixtures there are zero differing
    partition lists and zero tangent-band exceptions.  Every maximum BREP/
    implicit endpoint delta is below 1e-7 mm; the planar ARB8 maximum is about
    5.68e-14 mm.

BREP-leaf CSG hierarchy corpus:

  * Each implicit region is duplicated without changing its Boolean tree.
    Only the two leaf objects are replaced with their exact ft_brep exports in
    the comparison database.  Normal rt_shootray Boolean weaving therefore
    sees identical operators, transforms, region semantics, and tolerances.

  * The current hard fixtures are disjoint sphere union, overlapping sphere
    union, equal-sphere intersection lens, concentric sphere subtraction, and
    box minus a through cylinder.  They cover disjoint/merged intervals,
    intersection, internal cavity orientation, planar and curved leaves, and
    subtraction-generated final partitions without depending on ON_Boolean.

  * All 40,000 shared CSG lines have identical final partition lists and no
    tangent-band exceptions.  Directed forward/reverse multi-component,
    merged-union, lens, cavity, cross-hole, through-hole miss, and solid-wall
    rays also match analytic endpoints.  Maximum paired endpoint error remains
    below 1e-7 mm.

Cobb sphere baseline:

  * include/brep/cobb.h and src/libbrep/cobb.cpp now own the reusable Cobb
    support definition.  The proc-db generator calls the library helper rather
    than retaining its original second copy of the 25 homogeneous control
    points.  brep_invalid.cpp still has a duplicate to remove when that invalid
    fixture is migrated.

  * ON_Brep_CobbSphereUnsewn retains the 6-face/24-vertex/24-edge plate-mode
    representation.  ON_Brep_CobbSphereSewn creates a valid, solid, oriented
    6-face/8-vertex/12-edge manifold, with two trims on every edge.  Corner
    identity uses a scale-aware numerical roundoff bound, not modeling or ray
    tolerance.

  * A 33 by 33 grid on each support surface measures maximum radial error of
    approximately 8.88e-15 mm at radius 10 mm for both forms.  Small translated
    (R=0.01) and million-coordinate translated (R=10,000) sewn topology and
    radial error are also hard gates.

  * Directed rays hit face centers, an edge seam, and a three-face vertex.
    All 8,000 shared Crofton lines have identical implicit/Cobb partition lists
    and no tangent-band exceptions.  Maximum endpoint difference is about
    9.69e-8 mm.  The shared corpus now contains 120,000 lines.

  * The bowed-seam fixture preserves both edge endpoints, modifies homogeneous
    CVs without changing their weights, measures the resulting discrepancy
    from both trim lifts at 257 samples, and iterates the requested control-net
    displacement until measured g is within 0.1 percent of its target.  The
    observed maximum calibration error is approximately 3.5e-15 mm.

  * The signed matrix covers g/T = 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 0.9, 1, 1.1, 2, and 10;
    h/T = 100, 10, 2, 1.1, 1, 0.9, 0.5, 0.1, 0, -0.1, -1, -10, and -100;
    and both directions for every line.  It checks repeatability and emits a
    CSV table with root separation, partition counts, chord lengths, endpoint
    error, validity, and uncertainty classification under --cobb-report.

  * The current 416-ray baseline has 284 oracle differences (including the
    expected tangency-amplified endpoint changes of a deliberately distorted
    surface), 15 below-envelope whole-interval leaks, one reversal
    inconsistency, and 32 zero-chord h=0 contact records.  No below-envelope
    leak occurs at h greater than 1.01g; regression beyond that bound fails.

  * Private in-librt shot tracing records intersected tree leaves, structured
    solver outcomes, every converged candidate root, trim classification,
    normal orientation, and hit counts after each legacy cleanup stage.  It
    has a fixed 64-root capacity, reports overflow, and is enabled only by the
    correctness test entry point; normal production shots retain the same
    path with a null trace pointer.

  * Trace accounting proves that 14/15 below-envelope leaks have fewer than
    two unique candidate t values, zero are lost during trim classification,
    and one reaches cleanup with at least two unique candidates.  In the
    representative inward g/T=1, h/T=0.9 case, both candidates are clean hits
    from face 1 at the same root to about 7e-14 mm.  They are independent tree
    seeds, not the two intended crossings.  The CSV report includes each
    candidate, solver-status histograms, unique-root count, and every cleanup
    stage so future seam work can ratchet the actual failure mechanism.

  * Normal and cached prep now derive compact records for each two-trim edge.
    Exact-parameter NURBS forms are split at every nonempty knot span and
    stored as positive-weight rational Beziers with Euclidean control-hull
    bounds.  The records are rebuilt from the cached BREP, so serialized BVH
    cache version zero remains valid and unchanged.

  * The trace path uses a conservative infinite-line test against each span
    box expanded by the declared edge tolerance.  It separately records a
    fixed 17-point/32-step bounded closest-point estimate, edge index, both
    incident faces, edge and ray parameters, exact distance, tolerance, and
    candidate-span count without allocating per ray.  The estimate is an
    observation; the box test is the conservative exclusion result.

  * Every one of the 15 below-envelope leaks identifies the intended target
    edge inside its declared tolerance.  Across the complete signed and
    reversed matrix, target-edge distance differs from analytic |h| by at
    most about 3.59e-15 mm and no target observation beyond the edge envelope
    is marked eligible.  Each eligible target also has a conservatively
    selected prepared span.  The Cobb fixture has 12 eligible edges and 12
    prepared spans.

  * For tolerance-qualified edges, the trace maps the closest edge fraction
    to both incident trims, applies trim reversal, evaluates both surface
    lifts, and constructs the local material sector from loop-interior
    conormals and oriented face normals.  It records lift distances, face/ray
    normal dots, ray/edge alignment, sector validity, and whether the closest
    ray point is inside, on, or outside that sector.

  * Three independent sector fixtures now cover a converted ARB8's sharp
    90-degree convex edge, a capped L-shaped extrusion's 270-degree re-entrant
    edge, and a converted sphere's two-trim periodic same-face seam.  Offsets
    at +/-T/2 and exact contact all classify correctly in both ray directions.
    On the bowed Cobb sweep, all 15 leaks are authorized, span-selected, valid
    inside-sector observations; the initial effective-envelope policy expands
    qualified target states to 152 inside, 32 contact, and 68 outside.  Maximum
    error in the measured target lift
    discrepancy is about 2.51e-15 mm.

  * Prepared edge records now distinguish raw declared tolerance, model
    tolerance, inferred status, sampled maximum edge/trim-lift discrepancy,
    effective envelope, and authorization.  Edge span expansion and all later
    sector/closure work require the discrepancy-authorized bit.  A five-case
    g=2T Cobb metadata fixture permanently distinguishes correct, unset,
    explicit-zero, half-discrepancy, and double-discrepancy declarations.
    Similarity fixtures require all length-valued fields to scale together.

  * Cache deserialization rebuilds edge records with the active rti tolerance
    and rebuilds prepared surface spans before using deserialized trees.  The
    derived records are still not serialized, so cache version zero remains
    compatible.

  * The closure pass runs after direction cleanup and only
    considers solid, non-plate BREPs with one unmatched final hit.  A candidate
    must be a tolerance-qualified valid inside sector, share the hit's face,
    and have the t ordering required for the opposite event direction.  The
    bowed matrix hard-gates one and only one target-edge candidate for every
    legacy below-envelope leak.  Production activation additionally requires
    the measured face/edge discrepancy to be no larger than rti_tol.dist;
    both incident trim lifts at the actual event parameter must independently
    be within rti_tol.dist.  Imported topology tolerances larger than the
    model tolerance cannot silently broaden closure, and the sampled prep
    discrepancy is not treated as sufficient proof by itself.

  * A dedicated similarity corpus transforms the representative inward
    g/T=1, h/T=0.9 bowed case and its contact/exterior companions.  Identity,
    rigid rotation/translation, translated 0.01x scale, and translated 10,000x
    scale preserve the target edge, candidate span, inside/contact/outside
    state, unique closure count, missing enter/leave direction, and event
    ordering and the published repaired interval in both ray directions.
    BREP edge/vertex and ray model tolerances are explicitly scaled as model-
    space lengths.

  * Prepared face records now decompose exact-parameter NURBS surfaces at all
    nonempty knot rectangles into positive-weight rational Bezier patches.
    Each patch retains its original UV domain and a conservative control-hull
    bound.  Trace-mode homogeneous ray-plane coefficient hulls reject spans
    whose first or second numerator cannot contain zero.  The roundoff bound
    accounts for large-coordinate subtraction.  Cobb has six supported faces
    and six spans; the representative near-tangent line retains two spans and
    this count is unchanged by all similarity fixtures.

  * After a unique authorized closure is found, the resolver reflects the
    existing adjacent-face UV root across the associated trim tangent and run
    a fixed-budget, line-searched solve on a bounded extension of that
    prepared Bezier support.  Every current leak produces one correctly
    oriented opposite event.  The maximum endpoint error against the implicit
    sphere over all 15 is approximately 2.71e-8 mm.  Continuation attempts,
    candidate count, face/UV/t, residual, normal dot, and iteration count are
    available in --cobb-report.  A certified continuation now becomes a
    CRACK_HIT endpoint retaining face, UV, normal, and adjacency provenance.

  * Scalar de Casteljau restriction now subdivides each non-excluded
    ray-plane coefficient pair using a fixed 64-box depth-first stack and
    maximum order 16 scratch nets.  Retained depth-24 boxes include global UV
    bounds and rational-convex-hull ray-t intervals.  Every bowed-matrix raw
    Newton root must fall inside one such UV/t box; overflow or silent loss is
    a test failure.  The machine report emits every retained box.

  * The analytic sphere grazing sweep independently requires both roots to be
    covered while their chord is at least T, and requires no single box to
    contain both once the chord is at least 10T.  Exact tangency retains a
    conservative contact box but produces no final segment.  Negative-h
    ambiguity is measured on every representable mirrored sample and may not
    spread beyond 1e-6 T; the present largest is approximately 2.27e-9 T.

  * Each retained ordinary box now seeds a projected, bounded local corrector
    over its prepared Bezier support.  For chord >= T, all 36 sphere cases
    recover both analytic roots with maximum endpoint error about 8.56e-8 mm.
    The 23 positive sub-T cases and exact tangency each form one contact-sized
    cluster.  Miss-side local candidates are confined to about 2.84e-10 T.
    The nine deterministic seeds per box produce at most 72 attempts per
    sphere line and 54 per bowed Cobb line, use fixed trace capacity, and
    perform no ray-time allocation.  Candidate roots, residuals, normals,
    iterations, failures, and deduplication counts are emitted under
    --cobb-report.

  * The classifier similarity gate includes local-root failure counts, scaled
    residuals, normalized regular-root positions, and the physical cluster
    count at exact contact.  Identity, rigid rotation/translation, translated
    0.01x, and translated 10,000x fixtures pass when their model-space
    tolerances are scaled with the geometry.  Raw contact representatives may
    differ with floating conditioning but always collapse to one cluster.

  * A fixed 64-entry preliminary root-cluster array now classifies same-face
    observations.  Resolved sphere grazing produces ordered ENTER/LEAVE
    clusters, tangency produces one CONTACT, cluster capacity never exhausts,
    and the bowed leak histogram is 14/0/1 for one/two/three clusters.  This
    remains upstream of trim/topology compatibility and material winding.

  * A caller-owned 128-pointer leaf array now shadows the std::list surface-
    tree traversal.  All traced corpus rays require identical leaf count and
    pointer order.  Current maxima are 8 for the sphere grazing sweep, 12 for
    the bowed Cobb matrix, and 68 for the 0.01x transformed classifier.  The
    fixed overload counts beyond capacity and exposes overflow.  At this
    shadow checkpoint the production solver still consumed the list, so no
    shot result changed.

  * Production now consumes the fixed leaf array when it fits.  Overflow is
    detected before solving and selects the legacy list path.  Trace mode
    still constructs both and exposes fallback state, so order equivalence
    remains continuously checked while normal bounded rays avoid list-node
    allocation during leaf discovery.

  * Production trim classification no longer constructs a filtered
    std::list for every converged Newton candidate.  It applies the same UV
    bounding-box filter while scanning the SurfaceTree's prepared
    m_trims_above list and feeds the retained BRNodes to a common classifier.
    This removes the per-candidate list-node allocations without changing the
    prepared tree or the established tolerance policy.

  * The former allocating path is retained as isTrimmedAllocating for
    transition-time trace diagnostics only.  Every traced candidate in the
    sphere/grazing, similarity, edge-sector, seam-metadata, and 416-ray bowed
    Cobb corpora must match candidate count, trim status, exact selected
    BRNode pointer, and bitwise-identical closest-trim distance.  Any mismatch
    fails rt_brep_ray.  The legacy closest-node semantics, including the
    under-trim update rule, are deliberately preserved pending a separately
    justified correctness audit.

  * brep_hit no longer retains a const face reference whose assignment
    operator wrote through const_cast and could overwrite ON_BrepFace
    geometry.  It now holds a non-owning const pointer and copies that pointer
    safely.  The uninitialized active member and its only consumer, an unused
    debug helper, were removed along with the unused MissList declaration.

  * A fixed 128-hit workspace now collects raw production hits, performs a
    stable insertion sort, and applies index-based equivalents of every
    legacy near-miss, near-hit, grazing, duplicate, and direction cleanup
    stage.  Solid and plate segment publication consume the fixed result
    directly.  Workspace slots are assigned in full only as hits arrive, so
    the other capacity slots are not eagerly initialized for every shot.

  * Trace mode still runs the legacy std::list path from the same solved hits.
    It bitwise-compares every raw hit after stable sorting, hard-gates the hit
    count after each cleanup stage, and compares every final hit field.  The
    complete directed/Crofton/similarity/metadata/Cobb corpus has zero raw-
    order, stage-count, or final-event mismatches.  Observed raw-hit high-water
    marks are 4 for sphere grazing and the transformed classifier, and 6 for
    the bowed Cobb matrix, versus capacity 128.

  * Hit-workspace overflow is detected before cleanup or segment publication.
    Production then reruns the deterministic surface solve into the legacy
    list; trace mode exposes overflow and fallback as hard-gated counters.
    Surface-leaf traversal has its independent 128-pointer fallback.  Thus the
    bounded common path avoids dynamic leaf, trim-query, raw-hit, sort, and
    cleanup containers, while unsupported capacity cases retain correctness.

  * Outside-span continuation rectangles are reparameterized algebraically
    into Bernstein form.  Outward interval one-dimensional transformation
    matrices are applied directly as a tensor operator to the complete source
    coefficient intervals, bounding both inherited uncertainty and transform
    roundoff without intermediate split dependency growth.  A certificate
    requires every transformed weight interval to remain positive and the
    fixed isolator to retain only boxes belonging to the recovered root
    cluster.  No retained box may cover the original root.

  * All 15 legacy one-event crack cases are certified and repaired in
    production.  The worst eligible case visits 93 boxes with stack high-
    water 20 and retains a t cluster no wider than approximately 3.47e-5 mm.
    Every repaired endpoint matches the implicit sphere within 1e-7 mm.  The
    416-ray matrix reports zero below-envelope leaks, while g > T, contact,
    exterior, ambiguous, edge-overflow, and edge-evaluation-failure cases do
    not activate closure.  The CSV continuation record exposes certificate
    counts, capacity state, t bounds, and the resulting interval.

  * Closure also requires the repaired material interval to be at least the
    model distance tolerance after conversion through |ray direction|.
    Certificate t comparisons use a span/coordinate-conditioned numerical
    tolerance rather than a fixed model-space constant.  These conditions and
    the published segment endpoints pass identity, rigid, 0.01x, and 10,000x
    similarity cases when geometry and model tolerances scale together.

  * Incident edge/trim correspondence no longer assumes equal normalized
    parameter speed.  The discrepancy sampler, both material-sector frames,

STEP ray-prep observability and dense-tree census (Finding 80):

  `SurfaceTree` now retains the first deterministic construction failure as
  one of null surface, bounding box, midpoint normal evaluation, or unexpected
  subdivision, plus the failing recursion depth and UV domain.  Successful
  construction is unchanged.  The synthetic positive-weight `2^400` rational
  patch is now gated specifically as a normal-evaluation failure; its bbox is
  available, but OpenNURBS cannot supply the required midpoint normal.

  `brep-audit --prep-only file.g primitive` invokes actual BREP primitive prep
  and emits `brlcad-brep-ray-prep-audit-v1` JSON with time, peak RSS, face
  count, bbox, overall failure category, and per-face SurfaceTree failure
  cells.  `empty_brep` is separate from `surface_tree`.  The existing drawing
  audit did not call ray prep and could not observe this state.

  `brep-audit --surface-trees-only file.g primitive` serially profiles each
  face tree and emits `brlcad-brep-surface-tree-audit-v2` JSON with total and
  per-face time/leaves, source surface class and topology, domain, native
  NURBS dimensions, converted NURBS dimensions, and failure cell.  Building
  one face at a time bounds diagnostic retained memory and localizes a dense
  tree; it is not a production parallel-prep benchmark.

  Seven NIST databases all completed uncapped actual prep: 10 to 597 faces,
  about 0.88 to 7.39 seconds, and about 35 to 257 MiB peak RSS.  Thirty small
  non-GrabCAD primitives and thirty objects stratified across database sizes
  through 58 MiB also prepared without a SurfaceTree failure.

  An uncapped, two-process scan of the first BREP in all 135 recurta AP203
  databases completed in 239.57 seconds: 130 prepared, four exceeded a
  15-second classification cap, no process crashed, and the only normal
  failure was `10058.g`'s zero-face BREP.  The 130 successes cover 4,923
  faces.  The largest sub-cap model had 992 faces in 9.30 seconds; the slowest
  sub-cap model had 17 faces in 11.39 seconds.

  Uncapped replay classified all four time outliers as valid prep:

    * `10007.g`: 15 faces, 50.97 seconds, 77.8 MiB;
    * `10057.g`: 301 faces, 15.33 seconds, 859.7 MiB;
    * `10062.g`: 3,623 faces, 17.72 seconds, 270.9 MiB; and
    * `10088.g`: 5,771 faces, 17.38 seconds, 236.8 MiB.

  The low-face cases expose the tree algorithm rather than database scale.
  Serial `10007.g` profiling took 46.88 seconds.  Face 7 took 24.95 seconds
  for 12,722 leaves; faces 1, 3, and 5 took 6.56, 7.28, and 7.46 seconds.
  They are trimmed `ON_RevSurface` patches with rational order-3-by-3,
  9-by-9 NURBS forms.  Serial `10057.g` profiling took 85.99 seconds and
  412.2 MiB for 113,008 leaves.  Repeated revolved faces with rational
  order-3-by-2, 9-by-2 forms each cost about 2.4 to 2.6 seconds and 1,215
  leaves.  Production retained/overlapped parallel construction explains its
  larger 859.7 MiB peak.  The one-face revolved surface in `10145.g` creates
  8,192 leaves.

  A 1-GiB RLIMIT_AS pass with four external processes was deliberately
  discarded as a geometry census.  Each prep also requested twelve internal
  workers; the virtual-memory cap caused pthread creation failure,
  `std::bad_alloc`, and secondary termination even for models that later
  prepared normally uncapped.  This is useful bounded-resource hardening
  evidence, but it is not a solver or SurfaceTree-unavailability result.

  Direct verification on the final source:

    .build/src/librt/tests/rt_brep_ray --local-root-only

  PASS: thirteen arithmetic cases remain 10 certified/3 rejected, and the
  four weighted prepared-surface cases remain 3 certified/1 prep-rejected.
  The rejected surface is additionally checked for the exact non-none
  `FAILURE_NORMAL_EVALUATION` diagnostic.

    ctest --test-dir .build --parallel 13 \
      -R '^rt_brep_ray($|_)' --output-on-failure

  PASS: 13/13 in 192.67 seconds.  Local root took 2.57, grazing root
  5.98, contact trim 39.32, the complete process 53.54, Crofton 62.93,
  interval 165.31, and seam 192.24 seconds.

    ctest --test-dir .build --parallel 13 \
      -R '^(rt_brep_ray_local_root|rt_primitive_brep|rt_cache_.*|regress-nurbs_(NIST03_MISS_01|SPH_SOLID_HIT_LEN_01|BOX_PLATE_HIT_LEN_01))$' \
      --output-on-failure

  PASS: 12/12 in 9.25 seconds, including serial/parallel cache matrices,
