      Accepted directions were LEAVE, ENTER, ENTER, LEAVE, LEAVE, ENTER.
      Historical same-direction cleanup kept the last ENTER and first LEAVE,
      effectively intersecting overlapping shell ranges.  It published two
      segments with inverted endpoint normals.  Tightening `brep-audit` so a
      solid entry normal dot must be negative and an exit dot positive made
      this a hard failure instead of telemetry only.

    pair 26
      origin=(-109.55455871766632, -18.860350561763859,
              -44.046489176349951)
      direction=(0.97624929252184156, 0.17547275793085171,
                 0.12706939078607674)
      chord=238.1312905423778

      This distinguished true overlap from duplicate observations of one
      seam event.  Same-direction face-67/104 roots differ by about 2.4e-5,
      within the object's 5e-4 model tolerance.  Treating them as independent
      winding changes made the reverse ray lose its interval.

  A second concrete bug existed in both allocating and fixed cleanup loops.
  Each initialized an entering sign but attempted to replace it only under an
  iterator/index condition that was unreachable because iteration began at
  the second hit.  The direct fix and first-hit sign regression were useful,
  but raw-root tracing proved they were not the complete behavioral solution.

  New audit diagnostics in `brep-audit`:

    --ray-pair=N
      Select one indexed deterministic chord pair.  With `--ray-count=0`,
      pairs 0..2 are the three axis chords and later values select the same
      fixed-seed random chord stream used by the full audit.

    --legacy-only
      Build fresh requested-depth SurfaceTrees and run the legacy solve only,
      omitting expensive prepared local-box tracing.  This makes cleanup and
      depth questions separable without using a cache.

  Failure records now preserve selected pair index, trace mode, all raw roots
  and final intervals, intersected leaves, solver calls, cleanup-stage counts,
  overflow, face component, UV, normal dot, trim status/distance, hit class,
  direction and adjacent face.  Filtering maps failure records to the correct
  selected ray.  `_rt_brep_shot_legacy_trace` is exported internally so the
  detailed audit can retain telemetry while explicitly bypassing the prepared
  attempt.

  The final legacy solid rule has two separate operations:

    1. Same-event coalescence at model resolution.  Its ray-parameter envelope
       is max(BREP_SAME_POINT_TOLERANCE, tol.dist/|D|), widened by 128 epsilon
       at parameter scale.  Only consecutive same-direction observations are
       coalesced.  Keep the first ENTER and last LEAVE, which are the union
       extrema and map into each other under ray reversal.  Opposite
       directions are never coalesced because they can bound a real close
       grazing interval.

    2. Nonzero-winding material assembly.  Starting outside, retain the first
       ENTER changing winding 0->1 and the LEAVE changing 1->0.  Nested outward
       shells raise winding and therefore produce a union envelope.  Disjoint
       shells return to zero and produce separate intervals.  Inward-oriented
       cavities produce the expected temporary zero winding.  Finding 84
       supersedes the original asymmetric prefix/suffix handling: a reverse
       scan removes only entries without a later exit and a forward scan
       removes only exits without an earlier retained entry.  The maximal
       balanced stream is therefore retained under reversal without bridging
       a gap.  A sole unmatched observation is retained for the later
       certified seam/closure resolver; dropping it broke all intended Cobb
       crack repairs and was caught before promotion.

  `brep_hit_workspace` compacts in place with no allocation.  The allocating
  list performs exactly the same choices for equivalence testing.  Plate and
  non-solid paths keep their previous first-hit convention.  The prepared
  pipeline remains stricter: it publishes only a complete owned event ledger
  and falls back atomically on unresolved winding/event identity.  General
  prepared winding above one still needs explicit independently owned shell
  events; the legacy parameter-local policy is not used as a proof.

  `_rt_brep_direction_cleanup_test` now accepts a model-distance tolerance and
  covers:

    * nested ENTER/ENTER/ENTER/LEAVE/LEAVE/LEAVE union envelopes;
    * reversal of the complete envelope;
    * odd ENTER/LEAVE/ENTER and reversed LEAVE/ENTER/LEAVE streams;
    * leading/trailing unmatched events bracketing nested material;
    * same-direction tolerance clusters with reversal; and
    * a coalesced singleton retained as a seam-closure witness.

  The independent analytic fixture constructs radius-five implicit spheres at
  x=-3 and x=+3, converts each to BREP, and appends the two closed shells into
  one BREP primitive.  The oracle remains the implicit CSG union.  Its x-axis
  interval is [12,28].  Before the fix the BREP returned the intersection
  [18,22] in both directions; after the fix both directions return [12,28].
  The existing disjoint-sphere union continues to return distinct intervals.
  This isolates solid assembly from STEP, trim, tree density, and BREP Boolean
  conversion.

  An intermediate version that discarded every unmatched leading/trailing
  event passed UCP but failed the seam gate.  Cobb classifier cases lost their
  singleton closure candidate, all 14 below-envelope production repairs
  disappeared, and two prepared promotions became regressions.  Retaining a
  single witness restored:

    * Cobb classifier similarity/parameter invariance;
    * ambiguous trim-correspondence fallback;
    * 14/14 below-envelope repairs with zero crack leaks;
    * two prepared promotions and zero regressions;
    * 322/322 prepared selections in the bowed matrix; and
    * the complete 416-ray bowed seam matrix.

  Final optimized validation on the exact source:

    .build/src/librt/tests/rt_brep_ray --seam-only

  PASS in about 383 seconds.  All seam, closure, overlap, similarity, defect,
  prepared-promotion, and isolated trim/edge checks pass.

    ctest --test-dir .build -R '^rt_brep_ray($|_)' \
      -E '^rt_brep_ray_seam$' -j14 --output-on-failure

  PASS: 17/17 in 156.82 seconds.  Together with the standalone seam result,
  the complete registered 18-shard gate passes.  Cleanup and hard Crofton pass
  independently; the new combined-overlapping-sphere fixture reports two
  directed cases and zero failures.

    .build/bin/brep-audit --ray-depth-compare --ray-count=32 \
      /home/cyapp/step_corpus_g/second/bearings/UCP-206.g UCP_206_item.s

  PASS: 70 rays, 16 hit rays, 20 segments, zero invalid rays, orientation
  anomalies, depth differences, or reversal mismatches.  Maximum depth-three
  versus depth-eight endpoint difference is 7.19e-10.  Eight rays select

  Performance direction remains correctness first, optimization second.  The
  UCP trace now supplies a concrete workload for the later Abert-informed

  immutable data, vectorize conservative projection/rejection, implement a

Trim endpoint classification and NIST depth discrepancy (Finding 82):

  The next broader imported checks produced these legacy-only 70-ray results:

    non_grabcad/recurta/step_ap203/10077.g id2_item.s
      PASS, 5,458/138,371 depth-three/eight nodes, 22 segments;
    nist/NIST_MBE_PMI_1.g Document_item.s
      PASS, 1,763/53,051 nodes, 20 segments;
    second/case_fan_120mm.g case_fan_120mm_v3_item.s
      PASS, 682 faces, 31,388/744,484 nodes, 24 segments; and
    non_grabcad/a.g A_item.s
      correctly reported non-solid for its 471 faces.

  `nist/NIST_MBE_PMI_11.g`, `Document_item.s`, then exposed a real
  density-dependent result.  Exact deterministic pair 1 is:

    origin=(-7.1054273576010019e-15, -58.828516522601504,
            -0.075000000000000622)
    direction=(0, 0.99999999999999989, 0)
    chord=117.65703304520302

  Before repair, adaptive depths zero through seven returned only interval
  `[74.828516522601504,90.328516522601504]`; depth eight also returned
  `[27.328516522601504,42.828516522601504]`.  Both depths were reversal
  stable.  All depths found the same surface root on face 7 at
  `t=27.328516522601504`, UV approximately
  `(4.71238898038469,1.695)`, normal dot `-1`.  The difference was trim
  classification, not root convergence.

  At depths zero through seven the leaf and full face CurveTree called this a
  clean miss.  At depth eight the leaf had
  `m_checkTrim=false,m_trimmed=false`, accepted the root without evaluating
  the point, and disagreed with the full CurveTree.  The dense tree was
  accidentally masking a classifier defect.  This is direct evidence that
  depth eight is a reference for detecting change, not ground truth.

  Face topology establishes the intended answer.  Face 7 is a cylindrical
  patch with outer loop 9 covering approximately
  `u=[pi,2*pi],v=[0.27,1.77]`; the root is plainly inside.  The upper boundary
  is split between trims 39 and 40.  Trim 39 ends about `2.2e-8` in u beyond
  the query, while trim 40 begins roughly `1.6e-4` on the other side.  The
  two intervals are also the physically symmetric cross section of the
  annular model.

  The defect was a tolerance inconsistency inside the CurveTree classifier.
  `CurveTree::isTrimmed` selected candidates with the structural
  `BREP_UV_DIST_FUZZ` envelope (`1e-6`), but `BRNode::isTrimmed` repeated the u
  bounding-box test exactly.  The correct upper trim was counted as a
  candidate and then rejected before curve evaluation, leaving only lower
  crossings and the wrong winding.  `BRNode::isTrimmed` now uses the same UV
  envelope and clamps the admitted u coordinate to the leaf endpoint before
  curve evaluation.  This does not expand `BREP_EDGE_MISS_TOLERANCE`,
  model-distance closure, or hit-event coalescence, and a query beyond the UV
  envelope is still outside.

  `brep-audit` failure records now include fixed cleanup stage counts and
  per-root leaf/full-face trim status, distance, candidate count, hit class,
  and aggregate equivalence counters.  These fields made the accidental
  depth-eight shortcut visible without changing production behavior.

  A new source-only fixture, available as
  `rt_brep_ray --trim-classifier-only`, constructs a planar outer loop with a
  split upper trim reproducing the endpoint discrepancy.  It requires a
  query inside the `BREP_UV_DIST_FUZZ` envelope to be classified inside with
  the expected crossing distance, and a query beyond that envelope to remain
  outside.  It is registered as `rt_brep_ray_trim_classifier`, following the
  rule that difficult imported failures are reduced into the C/C++ corpus.

  Validation on the exact source:

    .build/src/librt/tests/rt_brep_ray --trim-classifier-only

  PASS.  Exact NIST pair 1 passes for every candidate depth zero through
  seven against depth eight, with four hits, two intervals, zero classifier
  equivalence differences, and reversal equality.  The complete audit:

    .build/bin/brep-audit --ray-depth-compare --ray-count=32 \
      /home/cyapp/step_corpus_g/nist/NIST_MBE_PMI_11.g Document_item.s

  PASS: 70 rays, 10 hit rays, 14 segments, zero invalid rays, orientation
  anomalies, depth differences, or reversal mismatches; maximum endpoint
  delta `1.42e-14`; 44 prepared selections at both depths.  Depth three uses
  2,146 nodes/1,556 leaves and depth eight 62,790/31,932.

  Final optimized validation on the exact clamped source is 19/19 registered
  ray shards.  The new classifier plus complete seam/crack matrix passed 2/2
  in 234.82 seconds; all other 17 shards passed concurrently in 175.53
  seconds.  Rebuilt ASAN/UBSAN runs of the classifier fixture and exact NIST
  depth-three/depth-eight pair pass without a diagnostic.  Leak scanning
  remains disabled for the established host ptrace limitation.

  This repair removes an accidental dependency on the densest tree, but does
  not justify lowering the production depth yet.  Complete the remaining
  imported strata and analytic/seam/grazing gates first.  Once correct answers
  and fallback authority are stable, the planned Abert-informed performance
  phase remains an explicit deliverable: profile normal production, then
  optimize broadly applicable face/span traversal, immutable data layout,
  conservative vectorized rejection, batched vshot, and the corrector if it
  is measurably hot.  Matching Abert exactly is not a requirement.


Loop-topology trim classifier and exact join ownership (Finding 83):

  The first per-loop parity implementation exposed and repaired four
  independent correctness defects that the historical closest-trim direction
  rule could not represent.

  1. Outer and inner loops are now classified separately.  A point is in
     material when it is in at least one declared outer loop and no declared
     inner loop.  Imported curve orientation is not used to infer hole
     topology.  Unknown loops retain a parity compatibility path.

  2. The parity ray follows a nonperiodic parameter direction when possible:
     +U when V is closed, otherwise +V.  Queries are normalized into closed
     surface domains.  CurveTree prep constructs per-loop periodic leaf images
     only in the transverse coordinate, sorted for both U and V scans.  It
     does not replicate infinitely along the parity ray.  The derived index is
     rebuilt after normal construction and cache deserialization, so the
     serialized format and pointer ownership do not change.

  3. `BRNode::crossesAbove` and `crossesRight` implement exact half-open
     endpoint ownership.  A separate fixed-workspace curve-distance estimate
     supplies proximity and adjacency.  Classification no longer chooses
     material from the orientation of the nearest curve above the query.

  4. The 0.005 UV endpoint allowance is authorized only at a real topological
     join displacement.  Prep compares each ordered trim endpoint with its
     neighbor, reducing coordinate differences modulo a closed surface
     period.  Only the incident leaf whose lower fixed-coordinate endpoint is
     displaced toward the gap may extend, and only by the caller's existing
     tolerance.  Exact joins retain one half-open owner and receive no crack
     extension.

  The fourth rule was required by exact UCP pair 3.  Its unmatched face-8
  event occurred at UV
  `(0.49347747834767419,0.37960829068592117)`.  Production originally called
  it inside with trim distance 0.0155288.  An independent sampled outer loop
  converged from 16 through 4,096 samples per trim to outside, with minimum
  boundary distance 0.0157269, safely beyond the crack envelope.  Exact trim
  44 already supplied a crossing; trim 45 joined it to about 8e-17 but the
  blind endpoint allowance added a duplicate third crossing.  Join-gated
  extension removes that event.  Exact UCP pair 3 then has one interval in
  both directions with zero depth delta.

  The genuine NIST crack remains repaired.  Face 7 at UV approximately
  `(4.71238898038469,1.695)` agrees with the sampled oracle, returns inside at
  depths three and eight, and restores both material intervals.  Its ordered
  upper trims have an actual roughly 1.6e-4 coordinate displacement, so prep
  grants the bounded endpoint extension.  The source classifier shard now
  contains a displaced join, an explicitly typed same-orientation inner hole,
  and an exact triangle join whose false extension would change an outside
  query from two crossings to three.  All pass:

    .build/src/librt/tests/rt_brep_ray --trim-classifier-only

  `brep-audit --trim-query face,u,v` is now a high-resolution independent
  diagnostic.  It samples each ordered loop at 16, 64, 256, 1,024, and 4,096
  segments per trim and records loop type, raw/curve/cover endpoints, join and
  closing gaps, lifted bounding boxes, query image, signed area, horizontal
  and vertical crossings, and boundary distance.  It also reports the
  production parity-ray direction, exact half-open BRNode crossings,
  candidates, prepared leaf images, and closest adjacent face.

  The polygon result is an oracle only when the three densest levels agree and
  every lifted loop closes within BREP_EDGE_MISS_TOLERANCE.  UCP face 103 loop
  119 closes two U periods away in the selected cover
  (`cover_closing_gap=188.495559...`).  Its planar polygon answer changes with
  lift, while quotient-space production correctly accepts the full periodic
  band.  Such output is marked `oracle_applicable=false`; per-level invalid
  matches and `final_matches_production` are null.  It is not a production
  mismatch.  Doubly periodic surfaces still require a separately qualified
  torus-domain classifier before this rule is generalized.

  SurfaceTree trim prep now uses a CurveTree cell certificate rather than
  retaining a copied list of trim pointers in every BBNode.  A cell stays
  pointwise when a trim box is geometrically near or an actually displaced
  tolerance endpoint can change parity over its fixed-coordinate range.
  Exact endpoints far along the ray axis do not force subdivision because
  their incident crossings change parity by an even count.  Ray-time point
  classification scans immutable prepared vectors without allocation.

  This is already a material acceleration-structure reduction at the same
  requested depth.  The final FRAME 70-ray audit passes with no depth, face,
  orientation, or reversal difference and maximum endpoint delta
  `1.5781040474394104e-8`.  Its depth-three/eight trees are now
  32,661/818,332 nodes and 20,052/412,008 leaves.  The preceding conservative
  endpoint-column build used 66,998/2,574,043 nodes and 44,223/1,297,795
  leaves.  NIST passes all 70 rays with maximum endpoint delta 1.42e-14 and
  retains 2,146/62,794 nodes and 1,556/31,936 leaves.

  The final single-process UCP 70-ray audit passes with zero depth, face,
  orientation, invalid-ray, or reversal differences and maximum endpoint
  delta `7.1902661602507578e-10`.  The refined classifier builds
  7,587/254,910 nodes and 5,146/131,064 leaves at requested depths three/eight;
  measured wall prep was 0.98/26.07 seconds on this host.  This trim-heavy
  object has 122,835 CurveTree leaves, so profile normal production separately
  from trace mode before drawing performance conclusions.

  A separate UCP pair-43 failure was not a trim error.  Near-hit cleanup
  erased any same-direction NEAR_HIT adjacent to a clean event, even when the
  two ray parameters were several units apart.  Both allocating and fixed
  cleanup now require `brep_solid_events_coincident` at the model-derived
  parameter tolerance before removing or merging them.  Exact pairs 0, 1,
  3, and 43 all pass depth-three/eight and reversal checks; pair 43's maximum
  endpoint delta is `9.9475983006414026e-13`.

  Production root classification retains measured join-gap healing.  The
  optional raw half-open result is exposed only to audit diagnostics.  This is
  intentional: production must represent the solid modeling intent across a
  sub-tolerance displaced seam, while the audit needs an unhealed view of the
  numerical boundary.  Healing remains bounded by the existing tolerance and
  a prep-certified displacement; it does not restore the former blind endpoint
  extension.

  Two valid root correctors can land on opposite sides of an exact trim by a
  few ulps.  When both roots are independently near the trim and agree in ray
  parameter, face/span, direction, and adjacency, NEAR_HIT versus NEAR_MISS is
  now recorded as a `near-side-equivalent` raw classification difference, not
  a semantic physical-event difference.  Solid authority remains with the
  subsequent seam/edge transaction.  The final Cobb matrix contains two such
  equivalences and zero semantic root-event mismatches.

  A separate `REGULAR_NEAR_TRIM` event certificate repairs the isolated
  displaced-trim transition.  It is available only after a manifold-edge
  transaction has selected the boundary component and the bounded complement
  search certifies a disjoint regular component.  Publication additionally
  requires an inside NEAR_HIT from the local-root source, at least one source
  box, and no independent edge or vertex ownership.  All six similarity and
  reversal witnesses contain exactly one manifold-edge and one regular-near-
  trim event; maximum normalized error is 6.22e-9.

  The non-isoparametric fixture showed that a roughly 1.25e-6 difference from
  the analytic sphere is real geometry, not a missed polish: its deliberately
  bowed NURBS surface has a transverse solve residual of only 2.31e-13.  The
  certified interval and resolved seam transaction are the oracle on that
  altered surface; the sphere remains a tight representative-drift ratchet.
  Opposite-face and contact-face fixtures each pass all 16 transform/reversal
  cases.

  The oblique grazing trend passes 16/16 configurations inside the certified
  conditioning envelope as clearance converges toward point contact.  Six
  deliberately almost-parallel ray/edge cases remain outside the theorem and
  all fail closed without publishing a speculative hit.  This establishes the
  desired behavioral trend: stable contact resolution while the bounds are
  decisive, then an explicit fallback rather than a silent hit loss.

  Final optimized seam validation:

    .build/src/librt/tests/rt_brep_ray --seam-only

  PASS.  The 416-ray bowed matrix has zero below-envelope leaks, 14/14 legacy
  crack repairs, zero reversal inconsistencies, 280 certified production
  selections, 136 uncertified fallbacks, and 24 validated seam pairs.  Two
  failed ownership attempts are accounted box failures and publish nothing.
  Eight contact pairs have two source boxes and roots each and one allowable
  raw half-open miss-side root each.  The experimental local cleanup had two
  ambiguous comparisons only when both gap and clearance exceeded the model
  tolerance by 10%; no ambiguity occurred inside tolerance and no comparison
  was an oracle regression.  Finding 84's event-local near-miss cleanup
  removes both remaining ambiguities without changing the selection,
  fallback, or seam-pair populations.  A 280-selection floor preserves the
  conservative fallback increase as an explicit performance/certification
  ratchet.

    ctest --test-dir .build -R '^rt_brep_ray($|_)' \
      -E '^rt_brep_ray_seam$' -j14 --output-on-failure

  PASS: 18/18 in 139.15 seconds.  The exact NIST pair 1 and UCP pairs 0, 1, 3,
  and 43 pass depth-three/eight partition and reversal comparison.  All seven
  optimized serial/parallel cache tests pass after removal of per-BBNode trim
  pointer lists and legacy CurveTree leaf-key serialization.

  Final ASAN+UBSAN validation on rebuilt binaries, with leak scanning disabled
  for the established host ptrace limitation:

    env ASAN_OPTIONS=detect_leaks=0:halt_on_error=1 \
      UBSAN_OPTIONS=halt_on_error=1:print_stacktrace=1 \
      ctest --test-dir .build-asan \

  Correctness remains ahead of optimization, but the next performance work is
  now concrete.  Recover the 42 selections lost to conservative certification
  only with general proofs; profile normal production separately from trace;
  then pursue the Abert-informed object/face/span BVH, compact immutable data,
  conservative SIMD rejection, batched vshot, and corrector/evaluation work if
  measured hot.  Preserve directed implicit-to-BREP partitions, paired
  Cauchy-Crofton area/volume convergence, grazing ladders, crack perturbations,
  similarity/reversal tests, and the difficult-root C/C++ corpus as hard gates.

  Imported agreement remains evidence of invariant behavior, not a geometric
  oracle.  The stronger truth program is unchanged: analytic implicit-to-BREP
  directed partitions, paired implicit/BREP Cauchy-Crofton area and volume,
  signed grazing ladders converging toward point tangency, Cobb seam/crack
  perturbations, scale/translation/rotation invariance, and later CSG-tree
  substitution in both directions.  Keep the production depth at eight while
  completing those gates.  The topology-aware cell theorem can be optimized;
  it must not be replaced by unrestricted endpoint fuzz or closest-trim
  orientation.


Local near-miss resolution and reversal-invariant balanced runs (Finding 84):

  The 848-face `IBEX_SOLID_item.s` in
  `original/SC_solid_stp_20260429.g` completed the sampled ray comparison for
  the largest model in the initial 14-database preparation stratum.  Its
  deterministic pair 22 has origin

    (95.848591855513888, -55.740815264982629,
     -11.3876265194396)

  direction

    (-0.58941604087522914, 0.74193704918254422,
     0.31955929936283961)

  and chord `255.39858798473995`.  Fresh adaptive-depth-three and depth-eight
  trees found the same nine roots.  Before repair the forward legacy path
  returned no intervals and the reversed path returned two.  Prepared tracing
  also failed closed with six unresolved events, so it did not conceal or
  supply the fallback answer.

  Raw cleanup telemetry isolated two direction-sensitive policies.  First,
  NEAR_MISS cleanup used sorted adjacency and matching normal direction as if
  they proved one physical event.  Forward cleanup let a leaving near miss at
  about 71.715 interact with a clean entry at about 75.263 despite their
  several-unit separation.  Reversal changed ordering and retained a
  different stream.  Near-miss versus non-miss removal, consecutive near-miss
  merging, and near-miss versus clean/near-hit relabeling now all require
  `brep_solid_events_coincident` using the established model-derived parameter
  envelope.  The fixed and allocating paths implement the same locality rule.

  Second, the old solid winding code ignored leading unmatched exits but
  deleted the whole suffix after an unmatched trailing entry.  A stream
  equivalent to ENTER, ENTER, ENTER, LEAVE, LEAVE consequently lost its
  balanced interior in one direction while the reversed form retained it.
  Cleanup now reverse-scans to remove only entries with no later exit, then
  forward-scans to remove only exits with no earlier retained entry.  This is
  allocation-free parenthesis matching.  The surviving stream is compacted
  to the same nonzero-winding union intervals as before.  A lone original
  observation remains available to the certified crack/closure repair.

  A new source test helper accepts complete synthetic hit-class streams.
  `--cleanup-only` ratchets both ENTER/ENTER/ENTER/LEAVE/LEAVE and the reduced
  SC stream, including a locally coincident near-hit/near-miss pair and a
  distant adjacent near-miss/clean pair.  Each test supplies the exact
  reversed stream and compares the full retained partition under
  `t_reverse = chord - t_forward`.  It is deliberately C++ data rather than a
  model-specific `.g` fixture and joins the difficult-case corpus required to
  prevent fixed-this/broke-that solver work.

  Exact pair 22 now returns two intervals in both directions and at both
  depths:

    [58.107629653..., 71.715107548...]
    [93.746252051..., 184.158146005...]

  The complete 70-ray legacy-only SC comparison passes with 16 hit rays, 54
  misses, 26 segments, zero invalid/orientation/depth/reversal differences,
  and maximum depth delta `3.3064736726373667e-8`.  The final depth-three tree
  has 270,839 nodes and 172,669 leaves; depth eight has 3,494,794 nodes and
  1,787,007 leaves; both use 60,527 CurveTree leaves.  Peak resident memory was
  about 973 MB.

  The full prepared-plus-legacy 70-ray trace also passes.  Both requested
  depths report 35 eligible/attempted rays, zero selections, 31 uncertified
  fallbacks, two surface-box overflows, and two event-class fallbacks.  Each
  reports 2,498 candidate spans, 18,336 subdivision boxes, 1,235 isolated
  boxes, 213 fold attempts, eight fold certificates, 18 surface-box
  overflows, 35 physically complete ledgers, and 225 unresolved physical
  events.  Wall time was 7m43.79s, CPU time 559.33s, and peak RSS 950,184 KB
  on this host.  This is trace instrumentation, not a controlled normal-shot
  benchmark, but it makes the general optimization target clear: reduce
  object/face/span candidate traversal and proof work before assuming Newton
  iteration is the primary cost.

  Optimized validation after the repair is 18/18 non-seam ray shards in
  150.68 seconds plus a complete passing seam shard.  The seam matrix retains
  280 selections, 136 uncertified fallbacks, 24 seam pairs, zero leaks, and
  14/14 known crack repairs.  Its formerly two out-of-tolerance experimental
  local ambiguities are now zero because distant sorted events cannot modify
  one another.  Rebuilt ASAN/UBSAN `--cleanup-only` passes with no diagnostic;
  the exact high-memory SC replay was qualified in the optimized build rather
  than multiplied under ASAN.

  This repair improves the tolerant legacy fallback; it is not prepared
  authority.  The prepared ledger still fails the whole ray on incomplete
  ownership, unresolved event identity, unsupported trim/seam topology, or
  nonzero final winding.  Parameter coincidence prevents a known local crack
  observation from corrupting an otherwise usable fallback but does not prove
  arbitrary roots equivalent.

  Correctness remains the immediate priority.  Production adaptive depth
  stays eight while analytic implicit-to-BREP partitions, paired
  Cauchy-Crofton area/volume convergence, signed grazing ladders, Cobb crack
  perturbations, similarity/reversal invariance, feature-targeted imported
  rays, and later CSG-tree substitutions expand.  Once these answers and
  fallback boundaries are stable, the performance phase should profile normal
  production, prioritize a compact object/face/span BVH and conservative
  batched rejection, implement a real `vshot`, and optimize OpenNURBS
  evaluation/Newton correction only when measured hot.  The goal is a broadly
  applicable certified pipeline, not exact reproduction of Abert et al.


NEXT ACTIONS
------------

Current priority after the local-cleanup/initial-stratum checkpoint:

A. Keep the production adaptive depth at eight.  The first reproducible
   14-database depth-three/eight prep stratum, the imported comparisons, the
   status-2 pole repair, UCP winding/overlap repair, NIST trim-endpoint repair,
   and SC local-cleanup repair are recorded above.  The selected objects in
   that initial stratum now have sampled ray comparisons.  Expand into new
   strata with `--ray-depth-compare`, using `--ray-pair` and `--legacy-only`
   to ratchet every failure before paying for a full trace.  Retain complete
   partition/reversal results, orientation, prepared selection and fallback
   populations,
   face/span/status-2/trim/scale fields, actual requested-depth prep
   statistics, RSS, and cache behavior.  Add feature-targeted rays so a high
   miss fraction does not masquerade as coverage.  Successful tree builds and
   agreement with depth eight do not by themselves establish an
   arbitrary-BREP default.
B. Preserve adaptive depth three as the present conservative experimental
   plateau.  Do not spend correctness effort forcing special fixtures to
   depth zero.  Any candidate production reduction must retain the complete
   19-shard optimized ray gate, the seven rebuilt-cache cases, sanitizer
   qualification, and exact identical-line partition results.
C. Use the deterministic hard replay as the seed of the difficult-solver C/C++
   corpus.  The status-2 analytic-revolution sphere pole is ratcheted under
   scale, translation, rotation, and reversal.  The UCP solid-assembly failure

   oracle, exact imported pairs 0/3/26, and the complete 70-ray audit.  The
   NIST depth discrepancy is ratcheted by a split-trim endpoint fixture and

   hit-class stream that distinguishes coincident and merely adjacent events,
   plus exact imported and complete 70-ray replay.  Add every subsequent

   object-level face/span BVH, compact immutable span/trim arrays,
   conservative SIMD coefficient work, and a real batched vshot.  Optimize
   Newton/OpenNURBS evaluation only if profiles identify it as hot.

current ordering above supersedes their older references to dense-tree
prerequisites that the new structural-depth measurements have now resolved.

1. Preserve the new local-root certificate as narrow declared-contact
   authority while continuing to expand its difficult-system corpus.  The

   Promote the revolved order-3-by-3 and order-3-by-2 outliers through their

   Profile the fixed-expansion Taylor cost separately from
